### [1] [Leggett-Garg Inequality Violation in Muon $g-2$ Experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20788)
*Brian Batell,Morgan Cassidy,Kun Cheng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reports the first observation of Leggett-Garg inequality (LGI) violation in polarized muon spin precession, using Fermilab Muon g−2 data (~10 billion decays); temporal correlators are reconstructed from decay spectra, yielding 5.5σ LGI violation in a single bin—significance increases with bin combination—though limited by detector-modeling systematics, which a dedicated experiment could reduce to ~10⁻³, enabling ultra-precise tests of temporal quantum correlations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To experimentally test macrorealism via Leggett-Garg inequality violation in a new, high-precision quantum system—polarized muon spin precession—leveraging existing high-statistics muon g−2 data as a resource for foundational quantum tests.

Method: Reconstruction of temporal correlators of longitudinal muon polarization from time-dependent muon decay asymmetry spectra; application to Fermilab Muon g−2 experimental data; modeling of detector acceptance and efficiency to infer idealized correlators; statistical evaluation of LGI violation (K₃ ≤ 1.5) using binned time-series analysis.

Result: Observed LGI violation with 5.5σ significance in a single time bin; increased significance upon bin combination; violation robust under simplified detector modeling assumptions.

Conclusion: Muon spin precession constitutes a viable, high-precision platform for testing temporal quantum correlations; systematic uncertainties—primarily from detector modeling—are the current limiting factor, but reducible to O(10⁻³), paving the way for sub-permil tests of macrorealism.

Abstract: We present the first study of Leggett-Garg inequality violation in polarized muon spin precession. We formulate a procedure to reconstruct temporal correlators of the longitudinal muon polarization from measured time-dependent muon decay spectra and apply it to publicly available Fermilab Muon $g-2$ data corresponding to approximately $10$ billion muon decays. Using a simplified model of the detector acceptance and efficiency, the Leggett-Garg inequality is found to be violated with a single-bin significance of $5.5σ$, while combining neighboring bins further increases the significance. While our analysis is limited by systematic uncertainties associated with the detector modeling, a dedicated experimental analysis could reduce these uncertainties toward the statistical level, $\mathcal{O}(10^{-3})$, potentially enabling one of the most precise measurements of temporal quantum correlations.

</details>


### [2] [Minimal Proton-Mass Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20792)
*Majed Khalaf,Eric Kuflik,Alessandro Lenoci,Hitoshi Murayama,Edoardo Vitagliano*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: A minimal dark matter model proposes a single complex scalar with baryon/lepton number, stabilized not by a new symmetry but by proton stability constraints; its dimension-7 portal yields proton–electron Yukawa coupling at low energy, fixing DM mass near proton mass; detectable via stellar proton burning, hydrogen decay, brown dwarf/neutron star heating, and nucleon-decay–like direct detection signals; relic abundance set by UV freeze-in.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct the simplest possible dark matter scenario consistent with absolute proton stability and SM minimality—without introducing new exact stabilizing symmetries—while linking DM mass and interactions to known particle physics scales.

Method: Constructs a renormalizable effective field theory with a complex scalar DM field carrying B and L; imposes accidental stability from SM gauge and Lorentz invariance plus proton longevity; matches dimension-7 semileptonic operator onto low-energy proton–electron Yukawa coupling below QCD confinement; computes cosmological production via UV freeze-in; evaluates astrophysical and experimental constraints (stellar energy loss, hydrogen decay lifetimes, NS heating, direct detection nuclear recoil spectra mimicking nucleon decay).

Result: DM mass is tightly constrained to ~938 MeV ± O(10 MeV) due to joint requirements of proton stability and absence of fast DM decay; relic density naturally achieved via freeze-in from heavy mediator decays; model survives current bounds but is testable by next-generation hydrogen lifetime experiments (e.g., Hyper-K), neutron star thermal observations, and directional/low-threshold direct detection searches for p → e⁺ + DM or p + e⁻ → DM signals.

Conclusion: This scenario demonstrates that viable, testable dark matter can emerge from a strictly minimal SM extension—no new symmetries, no extra gauge sectors—where DM stability and mass scale are dictated by the same physics ensuring proton longevity; it establishes a concrete link between baryogenesis, proton decay limits, and dark matter phenomenology.

Abstract: We present a minimal dark matter scenario: a single complex scalar carrying baryon and lepton number, with no new exact stabilizing symmetry. Its leading interaction is a dimension-7 semileptonic portal that, below confinement, generates a low-energy Yukawa coupling with the proton and electron. Requiring absolute stability of both the proton and dark matter forces the dark matter mass into a narrow window around the proton mass, which may be anthropically selected. Despite its minimal field content, the model can be probed by many observables: proton burning in stars, hydrogen decay, brown dwarfs and neutron star heating, and nucleon decay-like signatures in direct detection. UV-dominated freeze-in produces the observed relic abundance. This framework provides a unique testable example of dark matter arising from a minimal extension of the Standard Model.

</details>


### [3] [Phenomenology of heavy-flavour jet angularities at hadron colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20798)
*Andrea Ghira,Lorenzo Mai,Simone Marzani,Daniel Reichelt,Steffen Schumann,Leon Stöcker*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes resummed and matched predictions for jet angularities in Z+jet events with b-jet tagging, including mass effects, SoftDrop grooming, and non-perturbative corrections via transfer matrices from Sherpa; it compares b-jets to light-flavour jets to assess finite-mass effects.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the impact of finite b-quark mass on jet angularities in Z+jet events, especially under grooming and non-perturbative effects, where mass corrections are often neglected but potentially significant.

Method: Resummation (at leading-log or next-to-leading-log accuracy) combined with fixed-order matching (e.g., NNLO or NLO), consistent inclusion of b-quark mass effects at both resummed and fixed-order levels, SoftDrop grooming applied to jets, and non-perturbative corrections modeled via parton-to-hadron transfer matrices calibrated from Sherpa Monte Carlo simulations.

Result: Quantified deviations between b-jet and light-flavour jet angularities due to finite b-mass; demonstrated sensitivity of angularities to grooming and non-perturbative effects; provided precision predictions validated against MC expectations.

Conclusion: Finite b-quark mass induces measurable, kinematically localized effects on jet angularities—particularly in ungroomed configurations—and must be consistently included in precision QCD analyses of heavy-flavour jets; SoftDrop mitigates but does not eliminate mass-dependent distortions.

Abstract: We compute resummed and matched predictions for jet angularities in hadronic Z+jet events, where the jet is initiated by a b-quark. The analysis is performed both with and without grooming the candidate jets using the SoftDrop algorithm. Mass effects are consistently included at both fixed-order and resummed levels. Our theoretical predictions also incorporate non-perturbative corrections from the underlying event and hadronization, implemented through parton-to-hadron transfer matrices extracted from dedicated Monte Carlo simulations with Sherpa. Finally, we compare results for b-jets with the ones from light-flavour jets, in order to quantify the impact of finite-mass effects.

</details>


### [4] [Quantum Simulation of Generalized Parton Distributions in the Schwinger Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22602)
*Tianyin Li,Hongxi Xing*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: A quantum algorithm is proposed for simulating Generalized Parton Distributions (GPDs) in the Schwinger model using Wilson fermions—chosen for exact charge conjugation symmetry preservation—enabling momentum-dependent hadronic state preparation and light-cone correlation measurements with polynomial resource scaling; validated via exact diagonalization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable accurate quantum simulation of Generalized Parton Distributions (GPDs), which require strict charge conjugation symmetry—a property not preserved by widely used staggered fermions—necessitating a lattice discretization scheme like Wilson fermions.

Method: Quantum algorithm design using Wilson fermion discretization on the lattice; includes non-zero-momentum hadronic state preparation, light-cone correlation function measurement with embedded Wilson lines, and polynomial-scaling complexity analysis.

Result: Demonstrated polynomial scaling in qubit count and precision ε; benchmarked via exact diagonalization yielding physically consistent mass spectra, GPDs, and PDFs satisfying theoretical and constraint-based expectations.

Conclusion: Wilson fermions are essential for faithful quantum simulation of GPDs due to their charge conjugation symmetry; the proposed framework is resource-efficient and numerically validated, establishing a viable path toward quantum computation of hadronic structure.

Abstract: We present a quantum algorithm for simulating Generalized Parton Distributions (GPDs) in the Schwinger model. Unlike the staggered fermions widely utilized in current quantum simulations, we employ Wilson fermions for lattice discretization. This choice is critical for the quantum computation of GPDs due to their strict preservation of charge conjugation symmetry. We construct a comprehensive algorithmic framework that includes the preparation of hadronic states with non-zero momentum and the measurement of light-cone correlation functions incorporating Wilson lines. We provide a complexity analysis, demonstrating that the resources required for our algorithm scale polynomially with both the number of qubits and the desired precision $\varepsilon$. Finally, we benchmark our approach using exact diagonalization, extracting mass spectra and GPDs (also parton distribution functions) that are consistent with theoretical expectations and fundamental physical constraints.

</details>


### [5] [Warm fermionic dark matter from freeze-in at stronger coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20815)
*Duarte Feiteira,Vinícius Oliveira*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates warm fermionic dark matter (DM) in the Higgs portal freeze-in scenario with low reheating temperature, finding velocity-suppressed production, non-thermal momentum spectra, and Lyman-α constraints excluding DM masses < 100–180 keV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore observable signatures—especially invisible Higgs decays—of warm fermionic DM in freeze-in with stronger couplings and low reheating temperature, where thermal equilibrium is avoided yet relic abundance remains viable.

Method: Computes DM relic abundance including both pre- and post-reheating contributions in the minimal Higgs portal; analyzes velocity suppression of fermionic DM production; derives momentum distribution and compares against the αβγ parametrization; applies Lyman-α forest constraints to bound DM mass.

Result: Fermionic DM production is strongly velocity suppressed, requiring higher reheating temperatures than scalar DM to match observed relic density; resulting momentum distribution is highly non-thermal and incompatible with standard αβγ parametrization; Lyman-α data excludes DM masses below ~100–180 keV depending on reheating history.

Conclusion: Warm fermionic DM in low-reheating Higgs portal freeze-in is viable only above ~100 keV, with distinctive non-thermal kinematics and testable signatures via Higgs physics and cosmological structure formation.

Abstract: We study warm fermionic dark matter (DM) in the framework of freeze-in at stronger coupling, in the minimal Higgs portal scenario. The reheating temperature is taken to be low, so that DM production from the Standard Model thermal bath is Boltzmann-suppressed and the DM stays out of equilibrium even for a sizeable coupling. This opens the possibility of observable signatures, in particular invisible Higgs decays. We compute the DM relic abundance including both the pre- and post-reheating contributions. We find that the fermionic DM production reaction is strongly velocity suppressed, requiring larger reheating temperatures than those obtained for scalar DM in order to reproduce the correct relic abundance. The resulting DM momentum distribution is strongly non-thermal and its shape is not captured by the common $αβγ$-parametrization. We find that the Lyman-$α$ constraint excludes DM masses below about $100 -180\,\mathrm{keV}$, depending on the reheating history.

</details>


### [6] [Angular Analysis of $B \to D^{*}\ell \barν_{\ell}$ from Lattice and Experiment: $|V_{cb}|$ and New Physics Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23410)
*Marzia Bordone,Ollie Heald,Andreas Jüttner*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper performs a combined angular analysis of B → D*ℓν decays using Belle data and lattice QCD form factors to extract |Vcb| and constrain new physics via scalar/tensor Wilson coefficients, finding no resolution to the exclusive-inclusive |Vcb| discrepancy and excluding certain leptoquark models at TeV scales.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the persistent exclusive–inclusive |Vcb| tension and probe new physics beyond the Standard Model through angular observables in semileptonic B → D*ℓν decays.

Method: Bayesian joint fit to Belle angular coefficient measurements (ℓ = e, μ) and lattice QCD form factors (HPQCD, JLQCD, FNAL/MILC), using BGL parameterisation with unitarity priors; includes full massive-lepton kinematics and SMEFT renormalisation group evolution for Wilson coefficient interpretation.

Result: |Vcb| = 0.03997(71) under massless-lepton approximation; scalar and tensor NP Wilson coefficients constrained; scalar leptoquark excluded below 1.0 TeV and vector leptoquark/colourless scalar boson excluded below 2.5 TeV at 68% CL.

Conclusion: The analysis confirms the exclusive |Vcb| value without resolving the inclusive–exclusive discrepancy and places stringent, model-independent bounds on TeV-scale new physics coupling via scalar and tensor currents to b → cℓν transitions.

Abstract: We present a combined angular analysis within and beyond the Standard Model (SM) of experimental measurements for the $B \to D^{*}\ell \barν_{\ell}$ angular coefficients provided by the Belle collaboration, together with lattice-calculated hadronic form-factor data from the HPQCD, JLQCD, and FNAL/MILC collaborations. We focus on determining the CKM matrix element $|V_{cb}|$ and constraining a set of Wilson coefficients associated with new physics (NP) mediated by scalar and tensor currents. SM predictions for the angular coefficients are obtained using form-factor parameterisations based on the Boyd-Grinstein-Lebed (BGL) ansatz, with unitarity constraints imposed as Bayesian priors. Experimental and theoretical data are analysed jointly by considering the cases $\ell = e, μ$ separately and comparing with the massless approximation. For the latter, we determine $|V_{cb}| = 0.03997(71)$, with no resolution of the exclusive-inclusive puzzle. Using the full expressions for the angular coefficients in the presence of scalar, vector, and tensor currents, the corresponding Wilson coefficients are constrained through a joint Bayesian fit to lattice and experimental data. By including the renormalisation group evolution of the Wilson coefficients in the SM effective field theory (SMEFT), these constraints translate into bounds on the effective scale of potential heavy NP at the TeV scale. We find, at the $68\%$ confidence level, that NP mediated by a scalar leptoquark and a vector leptoquark/colourless scalar boson are excluded at the effective scales 1.0 and 2.5 TeV, respectively.

</details>


### [7] [Centauric 1-Jettiness in DIS and Universal Power Corrections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20825)
*Andrew Dotson,June-Haak Ee,Christopher Lee,Yiannis Makris,John Terry*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces Centauric 1-jettiness (τ₁^C), a DIS event shape matching Centauro jet algorithm geometry; derives SCET factorization for τ₁^C → 0, enabling N³LL resummation + NLO matching; shows soft function reduces to rescaled hemisphere measurement, placing τ₁^C in same non-perturbative universality class as DIS thrust/jet mass; leading non-perturbative shift ∝ Ω₁/R, validated via Pythia; enables αₛ extraction by breaking Ω₁–αₛ degeneracy using jet radius R.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a DIS event shape that geometrically matches the Centauro jet algorithm and enables precise determination of the strong coupling αₛ by separating perturbative and universal non-perturbative contributions.

Method: Defined Centauric 1-jettiness τ₁^C with tunable beam/jet reference vectors; proved exact geometric correspondence to Centauro algorithm in Breit frame; performed SCET factorization in small-τ₁^C limit; computed N³LL resummation matched to NLO QCD (O(αₛ²)) via NLOJet++; identified soft function equivalence to rescaled hemisphere measurement; analyzed non-perturbative shift scaling using boost invariance along photon axis.

Result: Factorized cross section for τ₁^C at N³LL+NLO; soft function shown equivalent to hemisphere measurement; leading non-perturbative correction governed by universal Ω₁ and scales as 1/R; Pythia confirms 1/R scaling; R serves as experimental handle to disentangle αₛ and Ω₁.

Conclusion: Centauric 1-jettiness provides a theoretically robust, experimentally viable DIS event shape that unifies geometric jet definition with high-precision SCET resummation and universal non-perturbative treatment—enabling improved αₛ determinations from DIS.

Abstract: We introduce the \emph{Centauric 1-jettiness}, $τ_1^C$, a generalized event shape for Deep Inelastic Scattering (DIS) with adjustable beam and jet reference vectors and thus beam and jet regions. We demonstrate that a specific choice of weights allows this observable to exactly reproduce the geometric boundaries of the Centauro jet algorithm in the Breit frame. Within the framework of Soft-Collinear Effective Theory (SCET), we derive a factorized cross section in the small-$τ_1^C$ region in terms of known perturbative ingredients. This allows the resummation of large logarithms to N$^3$LL accuracy, which we then match to fixed-order NLO QCD ($\mathcal{O}(α_s^2)$) predictions from \texttt{NLOJet++}. We establish that the soft measurement reduces to a rescaled hemisphere measurement, placing Centauric 1-jettiness in the same universality class, for the leading non-perturbative corrections, as DIS thrust and jet mass. As a consequence, the leading non-perturbative shift depends on the same universal first-moment-shift parameter $Ω_1$ and scales exactly as $1/R$ with the jet radius, thanks to the boost invariance of the Centauro algorithm along the photon axis in the Breit frame, a scaling that we test using \textsc{Pythia} simulations. These results open new strategies for determining the strong coupling from DIS event shapes, with $R$ providing a handle to break the degeneracy between $α_s$ and the universal non-perturbative shift parameter $Ω_1$.

</details>


### [8] [Counting axions with IAXO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20826)
*Benjamín Grinstein,Carlos Miró,Pablo Quílez Lasanta*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether next-generation helioscopes like IAXO can distinguish between single- and multi-axion signals, analyzing spectral signatures of axion flavor oscillations and extending conclusions to N-axion systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether a detected axion-like signal in next-generation helioscopes (e.g., IAXO) could originate from one or multiple axions—given that multiple axions coupling to photons may produce degenerate signals.

Method: Recasting CAST limits and projecting IAXO/IAXO+ sensitivity in two-axion parameter space; analyzing spectral features of axion flavor oscillations in quasi-degenerate and hierarchical mass regimes; assessing discrimination capability using expected detector energy resolution.

Result: IAXO can discriminate two-axion signals from single-axion hypotheses in specific regions of parameter space, particularly where oscillation-induced spectral modulations are resolvable; results generalize to N-axion systems.

Conclusion: Multi-axion scenarios are distinguishable from single-axion ones in helioscope experiments under favorable mass and coupling conditions, enabling model discrimination with upcoming detectors.

Abstract: The existence of multiple axion species is a generic prediction of a number of extensions of the Standard Model. If more than one axion couples to photons, their combined signal in helioscope experiments may mimic that of a single axion with different parameters. This raises a fundamental question: if a next-generation helioscope such as IAXO detected a signal, would we be able to disentangle whether it originated from one or multiple axions? To answer this question, we first recast current CAST bounds and derive IAXO/IAXO+ projections in the two-axion parameter space, identifying the regions where a signal could be observed. Then, we analyze the spectral signatures of axion flavor oscillations in both the quasi-degenerate and hierarchical mass regimes, and point out where IAXO can discriminate a two-axion signal from the single-axion hypothesis given the expected energy resolutions of the detector. Finally, we show that these results extend to a broad class of $N$-axion systems.

</details>


### [9] [When CPT Violation Hides in Plain Sight: How CP Measurements Are Compromised and How to Fix Them](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20830)
*Miaochen Jin,Gabriela Barenboim,Carlos A. Argüelles,Pablo Fernández-Menéndez,Ivan Martinez-Soler*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: CPT violation, modeled as a neutrino–antineutrino mass-squared splitting asymmetry δΔm²₃₁, mimics the CP-violating phase δ_CP in long-baseline appearance data, causing a degeneracy that biases δ_CP inference; atmospheric neutrino experiments (IceCube-DeepCore, KM3NeT/ORCA) provide model-independent, competitive constraints on δΔm²₃₁, enabling unambiguous δ_CP extraction at DUNE.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the tension between T2K and NOvA δ_CP measurements and ensure DUNE’s δ_CP determination is not biased by unaccounted CPT violation — a fundamental symmetry whose violation would impact interpretation of leptonic CP violation.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of how CPT-violating δΔm²₃₁ induces an energy-dependent effective phase φ_eff(E) degenerate with δ_CP in the ν_μ→ν_e appearance asymmetry ⟨ΔP⟩; statistical inference of δΔm²₃₁ using atmospheric neutrino oscillation data from IceCube-DeepCore and KM3NeT/ORCA-6 via likelihood-based global fits.

Result: CPT violation with |δΔm²₃₁| ≳ 0.3×10⁻³ eV² can fully obscure the true δ_CP in DUNE’s CPT-conserving fit; current IceCube-DeepCore + ORCA-6 data yield |δΔm²₃₁| ≤ 0.57×10⁻³ eV² (90% CL); projected sensitivity with IceCube Upgrade + full ORCA reaches σ(δΔm²₃₁) ~ 10⁻⁴ eV² within 10 years.

Conclusion: Atmospheric neutrino telescopes are essential to break the δ_CP–δΔm²₃₁ degeneracy; their independent CPT test is required to validate DUNE’s precision measurement of leptonic CP violation.

Abstract: The extraction of the leptonic charge-parity (CP)-violating phase $δ_{\rm CP}$ from long-baseline neutrino oscillation experiments rests on the assumption of charge-parity-time (CPT) conservation. We show that CPT violation, parametrized as an asymmetry $δΔm^2_{31} \equiv Δ\bar{m}^2_{31} - Δm^2_{31}$ between neutrino and antineutrino mass splittings, induces an effective, energy-dependent phase shift $φ_{\rm eff}(E)$ that is functionally degenerate with $δ_{\rm CP}$ in the appearance asymmetry $\langleΔP\rangle$. This has a profound implication for long-baseline experiments, where the tension between T2K and NO$ν$A CPT-conserving best-fit $δ_{\rm CP}$ values can be significantly alleviated by a CPT-violating truth; and a CPT-conserving fit can miss the true CP phase entirely for $|δΔm^2_{31}|\gtrsim 0.3\times10^{-3}~\text{eV}^2$ for DUNE. We then demonstrate that atmospheric neutrino telescopes provide the natural tool to resolve this degeneracy: using existing data from IceCube-DeepCore (7.74 yr) and KM3NeT/ORCA-6 (433 kt-yr), we derive a world-leading constraint on CPT-violation at $| δΔm^2_{31}|\leq 0.57\times10^{-3}~\text{eV}^2$ at 90% CL. With the IceCube Upgrade and full ORCA detector, we can reach a $1σ$ constraint at $10^{-4}~\text{eV}^2$ within a decade, providing the independent CPT constraint needed to ensure that DUNE's $δ_{\rm CP}$ measurement is unambiguous.

</details>


### [10] [Event-Level QCD Inference Framework for Quark-Gluon Imaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20923)
*Patrick Barry,Pi-Yueh Chuang,Ian Cloët,Emil Constantinescu,Arkaprabha Ganguli,Chao Peng*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Introduces an event-level analysis framework for quark-gluon imaging that avoids detector unfolding and binning, improving PDF inference accuracy—especially with limited statistics—compared to traditional histogram-based methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To eliminate information loss from detector unfolding and event binning in high-energy physics analyses, particularly for parton distribution function (PDF) inference and future 3D quark-gluon imaging where data sparsity and high-dimensional differential cross sections pose challenges.

Method: Develops an event-level likelihood-based inference framework using synthetic deep inelastic scattering (DIS) data; performs closure tests against known ground-truth PDFs under ideal (perfect) detector conditions; compares performance quantitatively using mean-squared error (MSE) against traditional binned histogram approaches.

Result: Event-level framework achieves ~35% lower MSE than histogram approach for moderate event counts; superior performance is more pronounced at low statistics; binning elimination is shown to be critical for higher-dimensional inference such as 3D quark-gluon imaging.

Conclusion: Event-level analysis provides a more statistically efficient, information-preserving alternative to histogram-based methods in QCD inference, with growing advantages in high-dimensional, low-statistics regimes like 3D quark-gluon imaging.

Abstract: We introduce and demonstrate an event-level analysis framework for quark-gluon imaging. For a first application we use it for the inference of parton distribution functions from synthetic deep inelastic scattering data. This framework removes the need for unfolding of detector effects and the binning of events, and therefore eliminates two key sources of information loss. We contrast this event-level framework with the traditional histogram approach by performing a closure test for parton distribution functions from event data obtained from a known ground truth. In this study we assume a perfect detector, which makes unfolding straightforward. The elimination of binning in the event-level framework is demonstrated to have important benefits over the traditional histogram approach, and performs better in the closure test, particularly for a smaller number of events. For example, defining a mean-squared error distance metric, we find that the event-level framework performs around $35\%$ better than the traditional approach for a moderate number of events. The benefits of an event-level framework should increase for inference associated with 3D quark-gluon imaging, because these differential cross sections are of higher dimension and the comparative number of measured events is significantly reduced.

</details>


### [11] [Gluon GTMD at strong coupling: fixed-spin saddle factorization and Reggeization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20981)
*Kiminad A. Mamo,Ismail Zahed*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs conformal moments of unpolarized gluon GTMDs at strong coupling via gauge/string duality, distinguishing local (b_T=0) and finite-separation (b_T>0) regimes; it identifies universal soft factors, derives UV/IR endpoint behaviors dependent on holographic background, and connects to Regge physics via analytic continuation in conformal spin j.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified, strongly-coupled description of generalized transverse-momentum-dependent parton distributions (GTMDs)—the most complete two-parton correlators—enabling hadron tomography, rapidity evolution, and Reggeization, especially for future Electron-Ion Collider observables.

Method: Uses gauge/string duality (AdS/CFT-inspired holography) to compute conformal moments of unpolarized gluon GTMDs; employs semiclassical string worldsheet analysis with minimal-area surfaces; distinguishes b_T = 0 (local operator limit) and b_T > 0 (finite transverse separation); applies cusp/perimeter subtraction and analytic continuation in conformal spin j.

Result: Derives factorized structure for GTMD moments: universal staple-worldsheet soft factor + target-dependent spin-j Witten amplitude; identifies Collins-Soper rapidity evolution from cusp logarithms; obtains universal UV endpoint reduction and IR endpoint behavior dependent on holographic background (algebraic/exponential/Gaussian falloff for soft-wall/gap-matched hard-wall/repulsive-wall); links low-x Regge regime to holographic Pomeron spectral curve.

Conclusion: The framework establishes a first-principles holographic foundation for GTMD moments, unifying descriptions of hadron spatial-momentum structure, rapidity evolution, and Regge physics—offering predictive power for EIC-era QCD studies.

Abstract: Generalized transverse-momentum-dependent parton distributions (GTMDs) are the most complete two-parton correlation functions in QCD, encoding the joint spatial and momentum structure of hadrons. Through appropriate projections and limits they yield generalized parton distributions (GPDs), transverse-momentum-dependent distributions (TMDs), parton distribution functions (PDFs), and phase-space (Wigner) distributions. We construct conformal moments of unpolarized gluon GTMDs at strong coupling using gauge/string duality. For fixed even conformal spin $j$, we distinguish the local boundary limit at $b_T=0$ from the finite-separation regime $b_T>0$, where the planar semiclassical amplitude is governed by a minimal worldsheet. There the GTMD moment factorizes into a universal staple-worldsheet soft factor and a stripped spin-$j$ Witten amplitude carrying target dependence. The cusp of the renormalized minimal area generates the rapidity-logarithmic Collins-Soper structure. We derive universal ultraviolet and infrared endpoint reductions. As $b_T\to0^+$, the finite-separation sector matches onto the local conformal moment through a universal overlap kernel. At large $b_T$, after cusp/perimeter subtraction, it factorizes into target projections and infrared transfer kernels. The ultraviolet endpoint is universal within the leading saddle, whereas the infrared tail depends on the holographic completion: soft-wall, gap-matched hard-wall, and repulsive-wall backgrounds generate algebraic, exponential, and Gaussian falloffs, respectively. Analytic continuation in $j$ yields the low-$x$ Regge regime governed by the holographic Pomeron spectral curve. The framework describes hadron tomography, transverse structure, rapidity evolution, and Reggeization for GTMD moments and provides a unified starting point for holographic studies of observables relevant to the Electron-Ion Collider.

</details>


### [12] [Neutrino Dipole Moments and Radiative Signatures from Partial Compositeness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20987)
*Benoît Assi,Pedro A. N. Machado*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a composite neutrino model where heavy neutrinos are bound states from a near-conformal strong sector; light neutrino masses are suppressed via anomalous scaling in an inverse seesaw, and enhanced electromagnetic dipole transitions enable observable radiative signals (νγ, multi-γ) at MiniBooNE and MINERvA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain sub-eV light neutrino masses while generating experimentally accessible electromagnetic transition dipole moments—beyond minimal Dirac/Majorana models—by leveraging conformal dynamics and composite structure.

Method: Constructs a composite neutrino model with heavy composite singlets mixing via inverse seesaw; computes anomalous dimensions to suppress light neutrino masses; matches conformal UV dynamics to low-energy effective theory to derive enhanced d_μN couplings; performs full event-level simulation of νX → UX → νγX production and decay at MiniBooNE/MINERvA, including cross sections, kinematics, and photon distributions.

Result: Predicts d_μN ~ 10⁻⁶–10⁻⁸ GeV⁻¹—parametrically larger than loop-suppressed SM extensions; identifies dominant single-photon signal for benchmark compositeness scales; finds multi-photon final states arise from fragmentation into multiple decaying heavy neutrinos, offering a novel probe of compositeness.

Conclusion: Composite neutrino models with near-conformal dynamics provide a theoretically motivated framework for light neutrino mass generation and yield distinctive, detectable radiative signatures—especially multi-photon events—at current neutrino experiments, enabling direct tests of strong-sector neutrino compositeness.

Abstract: We investigate composite neutrino models where heavy neutrinos emerge as bound states from a near-conformal strongly coupled sector. Standard Model neutrinos mix with these composite singlets via an inverse seesaw mechanism, where the anomalous scaling dimensions of the composite-sector operators naturally suppress light neutrino masses to sub-eV scales. Matching the conformal dynamics onto low-energy theory yields enhanced electromagnetic transition dipole operators with couplings $d_{μN} \sim 10^{-6}$-$10^{-8}\,\mathrm{GeV}^{-1}$, parametrically larger than the loop-level predictions of minimal Dirac or Majorana models. We carry out a dedicated event-level simulation of the production-and-decay chain $νX \to \mathcal{U} X \to νγX$ and compute the resulting event rates at MiniBooNE and MINERvA within the model, accounting for the composite production cross section and decay kinematics in detail. We further present predictions for the photon energy, angular, and multiplicity distributions. For the benchmark scenarios accessible at these experiments the radiative signal is predominantly single-photon; the composite structure additionally permits fragmentation of the up-scattered state into multiple heavy neutrinos, each decaying as $N\toνγ$, with multi-photon final states emerging for lighter compositeness scales or higher beam energies as a qualitatively new probe of the composite dynamics.

</details>


### [13] [Pion structure from its light-front wave function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20998)
*Khépani Raya,Zhen-Ni Xu,Zhao-Qian Yao,José Rodríguez-Quintero*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives light-front wave function representations of pion structure using continuum Schwinger methods, enabling computation of distribution amplitudes, form factors, and momentum/spatial distributions consistent with experiment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand pion structural properties for elucidating mass generation in the Standard Model and hadronic matter emergence.

Method: Derives well-founded, practical light-front wave function representations guided by continuum Schwinger methods.

Result: Enables exploration of pion distribution amplitudes/functions, elastic and gravitational form factors, and associated momentum and spatial distributions; results are consistent with theoretical expectations.

Conclusion: The derived representations provide a robust framework for probing pion structure, with predictions testable at modern facilities like electron-ion colliders.

Abstract: Understanding the structural properties of the pion is essential for elucidating the mechanisms of mass generation within the Standard Model and their role in the emergence and properties of the hadronic matter. Light-front wave functions encode extensive information about the internal structure of these systems and provide the link to measurable quantities such as generalized parton distributions and transverse-momentum-dependent distributions. Guided by recent progress in continuum Schwinger methods, we derive well-founded and practical representations of these quantities, enabling the exploration of several facets of the pion structure, including distribution amplitudes and distribution functions, elastic and gravitational form factors, and the associated momentum and spatial distributions. The results presented here are consistent with expectations and can be tested at modern experimental facilities, including the new generation of electron-ion colliders.

</details>


### [14] [Spin correlations and quantum entanglement in $γγ\to t\bar t$ at polarized photon colliders with NLO QCD corrections](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21152)
*Jun Jiang,Zong-Guo Si,Han Zhang,Xin-Yi Zhang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates spin correlations and quantum entanglement in γγ → t̄t production via Compton backscattered photons, including NLO QCD corrections; it finds enhanced cross sections but stable spin observables, strong entanglement near threshold, and high sensitivity of entanglement and Bell nonlocality to beam polarization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To theoretically characterize quantum correlations—specifically spin entanglement and Bell nonlocality—in top-quark pair production at future photon colliders, where photons originate from Compton backscattering.

Method: Computes total and differential cross sections and spin observables for γγ → t̄t using perturbative QCD at next-to-leading order (NLO); constructs the t̄t spin density matrix to quantify entanglement (via concurrence) and Bell nonlocality (via CHSH inequality violation), under varied initial photon beam polarizations.

Result: NLO QCD corrections significantly increase the total cross section but induce only small changes in spin observables; entanglement (concurrence) peaks near the t̄t invariant-mass threshold (~2mt); both entanglement and Bell nonlocality exhibit strong dependence on photon beam polarization configurations.

Conclusion: Top-quark pairs produced in polarized γγ collisions exhibit robust quantum entanglement and measurable Bell nonlocality, especially near threshold and with optimal beam polarization—making them a promising probe of quantum foundations and QCD dynamics at photon colliders.

Abstract: We study spin correlations and quantum entanglement in the process $γγ\to t\bar t$ with the photons coming from Compton backscattered laser beam. We present predictions for the cross sections and spin observables including next-to-leading order (NLO) QCD corrections under various beam-polarization configurations. The NLO QCD corrections significantly enhance the total cross section while having only a minor impact on spin observables. Using the spin density matrix of the $t\bar t$ system, we further investigate quantum entanglement and Bell nonlocality. We find that the entanglement is the strongest near the $t\bar t$ invariant-mass threshold, while both entanglement and Bell nonlocality are highly sensitive to the initial beam polarization. Our results provide a theoretical basis for future studies of quantum correlations in top-quark pair production at photon colliders.

</details>


### [15] [Large Language Model-Assisted Framework for BSM Model Building](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21316)
*Shaikh Saad*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: bsm_agent is an open-source symbolic framework that integrates LLMs as a natural-language interface for deterministic, reproducible BSM model building—automating Lagrangian construction, anomaly checks, EWSB conditions, and mass matrices—while strictly separating conversational orchestration from physics-critical symbolic computation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the gap between intuitive natural-language interaction (enabled by LLMs) and rigorous, reproducible symbolic physics calculations required for reliable beyond-Standard-Model model building.

Method: Combines a deterministic Python-based symbolic physics backend (for Lagrangian generation, gauge-anomaly cancellation, operator expansion, EWSB stationary conditions, and tree-level mass matrix derivation) with an LLM orchestration layer that parses natural-language field specifications, resolves ambiguities via confirmation steps, triggers backend routines, and formats outputs; supports local (Ollama), remote self-hosted, and commercial (OpenAI/Anthropic) LLM providers.

Result: Fully automated, reproducible BSM model construction from user-specified new field quantum numbers via natural language; validated correctness of symbolic outputs; modular separation ensures physics results are independent of LLM stochasticity.

Conclusion: bsm_agent demonstrates a robust architecture for trustworthy AI-augmented theoretical physics: LLMs serve only as interpretable, interactive controllers over deterministic symbolic engines—enabling accessibility without compromising rigor or reproducibility.

Abstract: Recent advances in artificial intelligence (AI), particularly large language models (LLMs), have created new opportunities for natural-language interaction with scientific software, but reliable theoretical model building still requires deterministic symbolic calculations. We present \texttt{bsm_agent}, an open-source symbolic framework for beyond the Standard Model (BSM) model building that combines a deterministic physics backend with an LLM chat interface. Starting from the SM field content and a user-specified set of additional scalars and/or fermions, the package constructs renormalizable Lagrangian, performs gauge-anomaly checks, expands operators into component fields, and derives electroweak symmetry breaking stationary conditions and tree-level mass matrices. The key novelty of the framework is that all of these tasks are performed automatically once the user specifies the quantum numbers of the new fields through a natural-language interface, eliminating the need for manual model construction. The symbolic calculations are performed entirely by the Python backend to ensure the correctness and reproducibility of the physics results; the LLM is used only as an orchestration layer that interprets natural-language requests, manages confirmation steps for ambiguous inputs, triggers backend tools, and formats report-ready summaries. The package supports three provider classes: local Ollama inference, remote self-hosted model servers accessed through the implemented remote provider interface, and commercial hosted APIs via OpenAI and Anthropic. This separation between conversational control and deterministic computation preserves reproducibility while making interactive BSM model construction substantially more convenient.

</details>


### [16] [Theoretical consistency and phenomenology of supercooled cosmological phase transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21380)
*Maciej Kierkla*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This dissertation computes the next-to-leading order (NLO) thermal bubble nucleation rate for a supercooled first-order phase transition in the early Universe within the SU(2)cSM—a classically scale-invariant extension of the Standard Model—using high-temperature dimensional reduction and exact fluctuation determinant evaluation; it yields a strong, LISA-detectable gravitational wave signal across the viable parameter space, with higher-order corrections shown to significantly impact dynamics and predictions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a state-of-the-art, systematically improved description of thermal bubble nucleation in supercooled cosmological phase transitions—particularly in classically scale-invariant models—where conventional approximations fail and gravitational wave signatures are sensitive to higher-order quantum and thermal effects.

Method: High-temperature dimensional reduction (DR) applied to a classically scale-invariant SU(2)cSM; construction of the 3D effective field theory (EFT); RG-improved, thermally corrected effective potential; explicit computation of NLO bubble nucleation rate via exact evaluation of fluctuation determinants around the bounce solution; systematic power-counting and consistency checks; numerical parameter scans and gravitational wave spectrum prediction using standard envelope approximation.

Result: A robust NLO nucleation rate calculation demonstrating significant deviations from LO predictions; identification of a broad region of parameter space where the supercooled PT produces strong, detectable gravitational waves by LISA; quantification of how higher-order corrections alter transition temperature, strength (α), duration (β⁻¹), and GW amplitude/frequency.

Conclusion: The SU(2)cSM realizes a testable, supercooled first-order phase transition whose gravitational wave signature is robustly detectable by LISA; accurate predictions require NLO treatment of thermal fluctuations and RG improvement, establishing a new benchmark for EFT-based cosmological phase transition analysis.

Abstract: This dissertation investigates a supercooled phase transition (PT) in the early Universe. Using high-temperature dimensional reduction (DR), we compute the NLO thermal bubble nucleation rate. By explicitly evaluating fluctuation determinants, we provide a state-of-the-art description of thermal bubble nucleation. As a case study, we consider the SU(2)cSM, an extension of the conformal Standard Model with an additional SU(2)$_X$ gauge sector and scalar field that acquires a vev through radiative symmetry breaking. This symmetry breaking proceeds via a supercooled first-order phase transition. The first part of the thesis introduces the theoretical framework, including effective actions, RG improvements, finite-temperature quantum field theory, effective field theory techniques, and thermal bubble nucleation. The second part applies these methods to the SU(2)cSM. We establish a consistent power-counting scheme, construct the leading-order effective potential, analyse symmetry breaking and parameter space, derive an RG-improved potential, and incorporate thermal corrections. We then apply high-temperature DR to a classically scale-invariant model for the first time, derive the corresponding three-dimensional EFT, and compute the NLO nucleation rate. A detailed numerical evaluation of fluctuation determinants enables a comparison of different approximation schemes and their limitations. Finally, we present the phenomenological implications. We determine phase transition parameters, perform parameter scans, and predict the resulting gravitational wave signals. We find that the supercooled phase transition in the SU(2)cSM produces a strong signal detectable by LISA throughout the parameter space considered, making the model experimentally testable. We also demonstrate that higher-order corrections can significantly affect both phase transition dynamics and gravitational wave predictions.

</details>


### [17] [Accessing the HQET B-Meson Shape Function from a LaMET Quasi-Shape Function](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21489)
*Ao-Sheng Xiong,Jun Ding,Ji Xu,Jun Zeng,Shuai Zhao*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces the HQLaMET framework—a two-step factorization scheme—to enable lattice QCD computation of the B-meson shape function, disentangling scales $P_B^z$, $m_b$, and $\Lambda_{\text{QCD}}$, thereby bridging a current gap since lattice results for shape functions are still absent despite existing LCDA calculations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The absence of lattice QCD results for the heavy meson shape function—unlike the now-available light-cone distribution amplitudes (LCDAs)—hinders first-principles predictions for inclusive decays; this work addresses that gap.

Method: Develops the HQLaMET framework: a two-step factorization scheme combining Heavy Quark Effective Theory (HQET) and large-momentum effective theory (LaMET) to isolate and compute the $B$-meson shape function on the lattice while cleanly separating the scales $P_B^z$, $m_b$, and $\Lambda_{\text{QCD}}$.

Result: A consistent, scale-separated theoretical framework is established; its feasibility is demonstrated via a graphical illustration starting from a phenomenological HQET-based shape function model.

Conclusion: HQLaMET provides the necessary formal foundation for future nonperturbative lattice QCD determinations of the heavy meson shape function.

Abstract: The shape function and the light-cone distribution amplitude of heavy meson jointly characterize the nonperturbative structure of the heavy meson on the light-cone, with the former being essential for theoretical predictions of inclusive decays and the latter for exclusive decays. While first-principles lattice QCD results for the heavy meson LCDA have become available in recent years, lattice results for the shape function remain absent. In this work, we establish a two-step factorization scheme -- known as the HQLaMET framework -- for computing the $B$-meson shape function on the lattice, which fully disentangles the effects of the disparate scales $P_B^z$, $m_b$, and $Λ_{\textrm{QCD}}$. For illustration, starting from a phenomenological model for the shape function in HQET, we provide a graphical presentation of the entire procedure of this framework. The results of the current work lay the foundation for nonperturbative lattice QCD determinations of the shape function in the near future.

</details>


### [18] [Heavy-quark pair-production in DIS at NLO QCD matched to a parton shower](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21510)
*Santiago Castro,Clara Del Pio,Adam Kardos,Sven-Olaf Moch,Aris Spourdalakis*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper presents NLO QCD predictions for heavy-quark pair production in DIS, matched to parton showers via POWHEG, with consistent mass treatment and double-counting avoidance; it validates virtual corrections using massification and focuses on the dominant gluon-initiated channel relevant for HERA and the future EIC.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide accurate, matched theoretical predictions for heavy-quark pair production in DIS—critical for precision QCD studies and PDF extractions—at colliders like HERA and the future Electron-Ion Collider, while ensuring proper treatment of heavy-quark masses and avoiding double counting between fixed-order and parton-shower radiation.

Method: Next-to-leading order (NLO) QCD calculation of heavy-quark pair production in deep-inelastic scattering, implemented within the POWHEG framework for matching to parton showers; includes consistent heavy-quark mass effects, subtraction of double-counted radiation, and cross-check of virtual corrections via massification of one-loop amplitudes in the small-mass limit.

Result: A fully consistent NLO+PS prediction for gluon-initiated heavy-quark pair production in DIS; independent validation of virtual corrections through massification confirms correctness; improved reliability of predictions in kinematic regions dominated by this channel.

Conclusion: The developed framework delivers robust, physically consistent predictions for heavy-quark production in DIS, enabling more precise tests of QCD dynamics and parton distribution functions at current and future lepton-hadron colliders.

Abstract: We present theoretical predictions for heavy-quark pair-production in deep-inelastic scattering (DIS) at next-to-leading order (NLO) in quantum chromodynamics (QCD), matched to a parton shower in the POWHEG framework. We revisit the NLO heavy-quark pair-production cross section and implement a consistent matching to parton-shower evolution, with careful treatment of heavy-quark mass effects and the avoidance of double counting between fixed-order and parton-shower radiation. In addition, we compare the virtual NLO corrections available in the literature to one-loop amplitudes obtained through massification in the small-mass limit. This provides an independent validation of the virtual contributions. The study is presently restricted to the gluon-initiated channel, which dominates the kinematic region of interest at the HERA collider and remains important for the future Electron-Ion Collider.

</details>


### [19] [Probing Light Dark Fermions in $B \to D^{(*)}\ell X_{\rm inv}$ via Rate Distributions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21642)
*Lipika Kolay,Soumitra Nandi,Shantanu Sahoo,Ria Sain*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates how assuming a massive invisible particle (e.g., sterile neutrino or dark-sector fermion) instead of a massless neutrino in $B \to D^{(*)} \ell \bar\nu$ decays alters kinematic and angular distributions, within a general weak effective theory; it assesses implications for $|V_{cb}|$ extraction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard analyses assume the neutrino is massless; however, new physics—such as sterile neutrinos or dark-sector fermions—could introduce a massive invisible particle, invalidating that assumption and biasing measurements like $|V_{cb}|$.

Method: Analyzes modifications to kinematic and angular observables in $B \to D^{(*)} \ell \bar\nu$ decays induced by a massive invisible fermion, using a general weak effective field theory; includes discussion of UV-complete effective and simplified models.

Result: A massive invisible fermion produces distinct, potentially observable deviations in differential decay rates and angular distributions; these distortions systematically affect $|V_{cb}|$ determinations if unaccounted for.

Conclusion: The standard massless-neutrino assumption is insufficient for robust new-physics-sensitive analyses; incorporating a massive invisible fermion provides a consistent framework to reinterpret existing data and design future measurements.

Abstract: Experimental analyses of the semileptonic decays $ B \to D^{(*)} \ell \barν$ typically rely on the assumption that the missing energy originates from a massless neutrino, as predicted by the Standard Model. However, this assumption may not hold in scenarios where the invisible final-state particle is instead massive, such as a sterile neutrino or a dark-sector fermion. In this work, we explore how the presence of a massive dark sector fermion modifies the kinematic and angular distributions of these decays. Our analysis is carried out within the framework of a general weak effective theory, and we also discuss effective and simplified models in which these interactions may arise. In addition, we study the implications of these effects for the extraction of the CKM matrix element $ |V_{cb}|$. Overall, our results show that relaxing the standard assumption of a massless neutrino can lead to observable effects and provide a framework for systematically investigating their impact on semileptonic $B$- decay distributions.

</details>


### [20] [Globally Charged Vacuum Decay](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21653)
*Giulio Barni,Jose R. Espinosa*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies vacuum decay for charged scalar fields, showing that fixed-charge projection modifies the Euclidean bounce: it becomes complex in the complex-field formulation but admits a real two-field description; the resulting non-$O(4)$-symmetric bounce lowers the barrier and enhances decay rate, and real-time evolution yields subluminal bubble expansion due to phase-gradient energy from charge rearrangement.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how conserved global charge affects vacuum decay dynamics—specifically, how projecting the path integral onto a fixed-charge sector alters the Euclidean saddle-point structure, symmetry, tunneling rate, and real-time bubble evolution.

Method: Reformulates the fixed-charge vacuum decay problem using a two-field real Euclidean formalism (instead of complex scalar field), solves the associated 2D PDE for the bounce, analyzes symmetry breaking ($O(4) \to $ reduced), computes decay rates, continues solutions to Lorentzian signature to study bubble wall dynamics, and compares fixed-charge framework with finite-temperature/finite-chemical-potential approaches.

Result: The fixed-charge bounce is real and non-$O(4)$-symmetric; decay rate increases with charge; bubble wall reaches a subluminal terminal velocity due to phase-gradient energy cost of charge redistribution; the fixed-charge ensemble is distinct from but related to finite-$\mu$ thermal descriptions.

Conclusion: Conserved global charge fundamentally reshapes vacuum decay: it necessitates a modified Euclidean path integral with twisted boundary conditions or an equivalent real two-field description, breaks spherical symmetry, accelerates tunneling, and induces intrinsic velocity limits on bubble expansion—even in vacuum—due to topological energy costs of charge flow.

Abstract: Vacuum decay at zero temperature is generically described by a real $O(4)$-symmetric Coleman bounce. When the scalar field driving the decay carries a conserved global charge, this picture changes qualitatively: the path integral must be projected onto a definite charge sector, the Euclidean field obeys twisted boundary conditions, and the saddle is complex. For the simplest case of a $U(1)$ global symmetry, we first reformulate this problem in a two-field real Euclidean description with a real saddle. We then solve the resulting two-dimensional partial differential equation problem describing the decay of a homogeneous charged medium to a deeper vacuum via bubble nucleation. At finite charge the bounce departs from $O(4)$ symmetry, the barrier between vacua is lowered, and the decay rate increases. Continuing the solution to real time, we find that charge rearrangement around the expanding wall costs phase-gradient energy and drives the bubble to a subluminal terminal velocity even in vacuum. We also clarify how the fixed-charge construction interfaces with finite-temperature and finite-chemical-potential descriptions.

</details>


### [21] [Interactions in the G$_2$ vacuum structure and the static potentials](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21693)
*Seyed Mohsen Hosseini Nejad*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes vacuum domain structures in G₂ gauge theory using a domain model, highlighting the roles of SU(3) and SU(2) subgroups in confinement; it finds repulsive vortex interactions in nonzero-flux domains, favorable Casimir scaling and convexity—especially for SU(3)—and confirms center-vortex neutrality under large Wilson loops, consistent with G₂ vacuum structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand confinement in the exceptional G₂ gauge group by modeling its vacuum as structured domains and identifying which subgroup-based domain configurations reproduce key confinement signatures (e.g., Casimir scaling, convexity, asymptotic string tension ordering).

Method: Domain model analysis of G₂ vacuum; refinement of static interquark potentials in the intermediate distance regime; classification of vacuum domains by aggregate flux and embedded SU(3)/SU(2) subgroups; evaluation of Wilson loop phase behavior and potential convexity/Casimir scaling properties.

Result: SU(3)-based domains yield fully convex static potentials with exact Casimir scaling; SU(2)-based and zero-flux domains also show favorable scaling and convexity; nonzero-flux domains exhibit repulsive subgroup vortex interactions; all subgroup center vortices inside a large Wilson loop contribute trivial phase, implying net center neutrality; long-range potentials obey representation-dependent string tension ordering.

Conclusion: The domain model with SU(3)-dominated vacuum structure provides a viable, self-consistent description of confinement in G₂ gauge theory, satisfying essential theoretical constraints including Casimir scaling, convexity, center neutrality, and correct asymptotic representation dependence.

Abstract: We investigate the domain structures of the G$_2$ vacuum within the framework of the domain model, analyzing the interactions among vacuum domains. Refinements to the static potentials in the intermediate regime reveal the crucial role of SU($3$) and SU($2$) subgroups in the confinement mechanism of the G$_2$ gauge group. Inside vacuum domains of G$_2$ with nonzero aggregate flux, repulsive interactions among subgroup vortices are found. Models containing either SU($3$) or SU($2$) domains, as well as vacuum domains with zero aggregate flux, exhibit favorable Casimir scaling and convexity properties at intermediate distances; notably, the SU($3$) version shows no concave regions in the potential at all. For a Wilson loop of increasing size, all subgroup center vortices belonging to a vacuum domain with nonzero aggregate flux, when fully enclosed by the loop, do not contribute a nontrivial phase, supporting the viability of this picture as a model for the vacuum structure of G$_2$ theory. The long-range behavior of the static potential yields the expected ordering of the (constant) potential values at large distances with respect to representation.

</details>


### [22] [Analytic results for heavy-quark contributions to charged-current DIS at NNLO](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21702)
*Fabrizio Caola,Giulio Gambuti,Martin Link*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the full NNLO QCD corrections to heavy-quark (charm) production in charged-current deep-inelastic scattering, keeping exact charm-mass dependence; it provides analytic expressions for structure functions F₂, F₃, F_L in quark/gluon channels—including up to three final-state charm quarks—using reverse-unitarity, IBP, and canonical DEs; two-charm contributions are expressed in real Goncharov polylogarithms, while the three-charm case involves elliptic integrals represented via Chen iterated integrals and fast-converging series valid for Q ≳ 5 GeV.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide precise theoretical predictions for charm production in charged-current DIS at NNLO with full charm-mass dependence—essential for high-precision determinations of parton distribution functions (PDFs), CKM matrix elements (e.g., |V_cd|, |V_cs|), and tests of QCD in the massive regime—beyond massless or leading-power approximations.

Method: Uses reverse-unitarity to construct cut diagrams; applies integration-by-parts (IBP) reduction and canonical differential equations (DEs) to solve master integrals; expresses results with ≤2 final-state charm quarks in terms of manifestly real Goncharov polylogarithms; handles the three-charm contribution—containing elliptic integrals—via Chen iterated integrals and rapidly convergent series expansions valid for Q ≳ 5 GeV.

Result: Complete analytic NNLO coefficient functions for F₂, F₃, F_L in both quark and gluon channels, fully retaining m_c dependence; explicit closed-form expressions for all contributions with ≤2 charm quarks; a general elliptic representation (Chen integrals) and efficient numerical series for the three-charm case; validated against known LO/NLO results, massless NNLO limits, and asymptotic (Q ≫ m_c) expansions.

Conclusion: The computation establishes a new benchmark for massive QCD calculations at NNLO: it enables percent-level precision in charm-production cross sections for current and future DIS experiments (e.g., at the Electron-Ion Collider), supports global PDF+CKM fits with controlled heavy-quark uncertainties, and demonstrates robust methods for handling elliptic structures in multi-scale perturbative QCD.

Abstract: We present analytic results for the next-to-next-to-leading-order QCD corrections to heavy-quark production in charged-current deep-inelastic scattering, retaining the exact dependence on the charm quark mass. We compute the complete partonic coefficient functions for the structure functions $F_2$, $F_L$, and $F_3$ in the quark and gluon channels, including contributions with up to three heavy quarks in the final state. Working within the reverse-unitarity framework, we use integration-by-parts and canonical differential-equations techniques to express all contributions with at most two final-state heavy quarks in terms of manifestly real Goncharov polylogarithms which allow for a robust and efficient numerical evaluation. The three-heavy-quark contribution involves elliptic structures for which we give a general representation in terms of Chen iterated integrals, as well as expressions in terms of rapidly convergent expansions that are valid in the perturbative $Q\gtrsim 5~{\rm GeV}$ region and also allow for a flexible and fast numerical evaluation. We validate our results against known exact results at lower orders, massless NNLO coefficient functions, and existing leading-power expansions in the asymptotic limit where the virtuality $Q$ is much larger than the charm mass.

</details>


### [23] [Generalized parton distributions of a deuteron in an AdS/QCD hard-wall model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21706)
*Minaya Allahverdiyeva,Shahin Mamedov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes deuteron gravitational form factors (GFFs) and generalized parton distributions (GPDs) using the hard-wall AdS/QCD model, finding agreement with soft-wall AdS/QCD predictions and experimental gravitational radius.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a holographic description of deuteron structure via gravitational form factors and GPDs, bridging QCD-inspired models with experimental observables.

Method: Computes deuteron GFFs within the hard-wall AdS/QCD framework; derives GPDs from GFFs using sum rules; compares results with soft-wall AdS/QCD and experimental data.

Result: Hard-wall GFFs show good momentum dependence agreement with soft-wall AdS/QCD; gravitational mean square radius matches experiment; derived GPDs exhibit shapes consistent with those obtained from electromagnetic form factors in soft-wall AdS/QCD.

Conclusion: The hard-wall AdS/QCD model successfully describes key deuteron gravitational and partonic structure observables, validating its utility for nuclear hadron physics beyond single-nucleon systems.

Abstract: We investigate the gravitational form factors (GFFs) and the generalized parton distributions (GPDs) of the deuteron within the framework of the hard-wall AdS/QCD model. The momentum dependence of the GFFs obtained here is in good agreement with the results of the soft-wall AdS/QCD model. The value of the gravitational mean square radius in this model agrees with the experimental data. GFFs provide a holographic description of the. Deuteron GPDs are obtained from GFFs via sum rules and have shapes of plots similar to those for GPDs extracted from the electromagnetic form factors (EFFs) calculated in the framework of the soft-wall AdS/QCD model.

</details>


### [24] [The feasibility of single $Λ$ production via ${\ell}^{-}$ $+$ $p$ ${\to}$ $Λ$ $+$ $ν_{\ell}$ at $e^{+}e^{-}$ colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21995)
*Yueling Yang,Peisheng Tian,Shuangshi Fang,Bingbing Yang,Junfeng Sun*

TL;DR: This paper studies single Λ hyperon production via lepton-nucleon deep inelastic scattering (LNDIS) at e⁺e⁻ colliders, using leptons from resonance decays (J/ψ, ψ(2S), Υ, Z⁰) scattering off detector protons; cross sections are computed with baryonic transition form factors from QCD sum rules and lattice QCD; results show energy-dependent cross sections highly sensitive to form factor choices, revealing large theoretical uncertainties and experimental challenges—suggesting anomalous Λ production could signal new physics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the feasibility and characteristics of single Λ hyperon production via lepton-nucleon deep inelastic scattering (LNDIS) in e⁺e⁻ collider environments, motivated by its potential as a probe of baryonic transition form factors and possible new physics signatures.

Method: Computes differential and total LNDIS cross sections for ℓ⁻ + p → νℓ + Λ using baryonic transition form factors parameterized via the z-expansion scheme, implemented within both QCD sum rule and lattice QCD frameworks; incident leptons are modeled as originating from decays of resonances (J/ψ, ψ(2S), Υ(1S), Υ(2S), Z⁰) produced in e⁺e⁻ collisions and scattering off stationary protons in detector material.

Result: Cross sections increase with center-of-mass energy and exhibit strong sensitivity to the choice of baryonic transition form factors—leading to substantial theoretical uncertainty; LNDIS is found to be experimentally challenging to observe at current e⁺e⁻ colliders due to low rates and background contamination.

Conclusion: The study establishes baseline predictions for single Λ production via LNDIS at e⁺e⁻ colliders, emphasizes the critical role of precise baryonic form factor determination, and proposes that unexpected excesses in Λ yield could constitute evidence of physics beyond the Standard Model.

Abstract: We present a comprehensive investigation of single $Λ$ hyperon production via the lepton-nucleon deep inelastic scattering (LNDIS) process, ${\ell}^{-}$ $+$ $p$ ${\to}$ $ν_{\ell}$ $+$ $Λ$, in the experimental environment of electron-positron colliders. Our approach utilizes incident leptons originating from the decays of resonances (${J/ψ}$, $ψ(2S)$, $Υ(1S)$, $Υ(2S)$, and $Z^{0}$) produced in $e^{+}e^{-}$ collisions, which then scatter off stationary protons in the surrounding detector materials. The differential and total cross sections are calculated using baryonic transition form factors parameterized with the $z$-expansion scheme within both the quantum chromodynamics (QCD) sum rule and lattice QCD frameworks. Our results indicate that the cross section increases with center-of-mass energy and is highly sensitive to the choice of form factors, resulting in significant theoretical uncertainties. This study highlights the experimental challenges in observing the LNDIS process at $e^{+}e^{-}$ colliders and underscores the need for improved determination of baryonic form factors. It serves as a valuable reference for future experimental searches and suggests that an anomalous observation of single $Λ$ hyperon production at $e^{+}e^{-}$ colliders could indicate new physics.

</details>


### [25] [The shear viscosity of quark-gluon matter calculated with parton transport and comparisons with the Chapman-Enskog results](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21722)
*Mason Alexander Ross,Zi-Wei Lin*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the shear viscosity of quark-gluon matter using the Green-Kubo relation within an improved ZPC model, compares it with leading-order Chapman-Enskog analytical results, and finds good agreement—differences of ~9% for isotropic constant cross sections and ~3% for realistic finite-temperature pQCD cross sections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantitatively assess the reliability of the leading-order Chapman-Enskog approximation for shear viscosity in quark-gluon plasma by benchmarking against the first-principles Green-Kubo formalism.

Method: Numerical evaluation of shear viscosity via the Green-Kubo relation using an improved ZPC transport model; includes full temperature-dependent 2↔2 parton scattering cross sections from perturbative QCD with thermal screening, massless quarks/gluons, Boltzmann statistics, and chemical equilibrium; comparison with leading-order Chapman-Enskog analytical results over 150–600 MeV.

Result: Green-Kubo and Chapman-Enskog results agree closely: differences are ~9% for isotropic constant cross sections and only ~3% for realistic finite-temperature pQCD cross sections, indicating higher-order corrections to Chapman-Enskog are small in the latter case.

Conclusion: The leading-order Chapman-Enskog method provides a robust and quantitatively accurate approximation for shear viscosity in quark-gluon matter when realistic pQCD-based cross sections are used, validating its use in hydrodynamic simulations of heavy-ion collisions.

Abstract: We numerically calculate the shear viscosity of quark-gluon matter via the Green-Kubo relation with an improved ZPC model. We include all $2\leftrightarrow 2$ parton cross sections at finite temperature, which are based on perturbative QCD and screened with thermal masses, and consider massless quark-gluon systems with Boltzmann statistics in chemical equilibrium. We then compare the Green-Kubo results with the analytical results from the leading-order Chapman-Enskog method for the same parton cross sections over the temperature range $150-600$ MeV. We also examine the simpler case of isotropic and constant parton cross sections. Overall, we find that the two methods agree rather well. Specifically, the Green-Kubo results are greater than the Chapman-Enskog results by an average of $\sim 9\%$ for isotropic and constant cross sections and by an average of $\sim 3\%$ for finite-temperature pQCD cross sections, where the difference between the two methods is presumably due to higher-order corrections to the leading-order Chapman-Enskog results.

</details>


### [26] [Monte Carlo Event Generators for Future Lepton Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22007)
*Alan Price*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews key challenges in Monte Carlo event generator development for future high-precision lepton colliders, focusing on electroweak corrections, initial-state radiation, beam dynamics, perturbative QCD, and non-perturbative modelling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Future high-precision lepton colliders demand significantly improved accuracy and scope from Monte Carlo event generators, necessitating a review of current theoretical and technical challenges.

Method: Selective review and analysis of major physics and computational challenges in MC generator development, including electroweak corrections, ISR, beam effects, pQCD, and hadronisation/non-perturbative modelling.

Result: Identification and discussion of critical bottlenecks—such as higher-order electroweak effects, coherent ISR treatment, realistic beam spectra, NNLO+ parton shower matching, and improved hadronisation models—that must be addressed for next-generation collider simulations.

Conclusion: Advancing MC generators requires coordinated progress across multiple fronts: theory (higher-order calculations), algorithmics (efficient multi-scale simulation), and experimental input (beam and detector constraints); no single solution suffices for the precision goals of future lepton colliders.

Abstract: Monte Carlo event generators are essential tools in collider physics, providing the link between
  theoretical predictions and experimental measurements through fully exclusive event simulation.
  Future collider programmes, particularly high-precision lepton colliders will place significantly increased demands on their accuracy and scope.
  This contribution reviews key challenges in MC generator development, including electroweak corrections,
  initial-state radiation, beam dynamics, perturbative QCD, and non-perturbative modelling.
  The discussion is not exhaustive and reflects a selective choice of topics.

</details>

  <summary>Details</summary>
