Conclusion: X(3872) serves as a unique hard probe for quark-gluon plasma characterization, complementing conventional charmonia by encoding both compact and extended degrees of freedom in its in-medium evolution.

Abstract: The separation of $c\bar c$ quarks in the quark-gluon plasma and the characteristic size of the final state quarkonia should lead to the observed hadron ratios in nuclear collisions. Such dependence manifests itself through the Fermi Golden rule, where hadron ratios are sensitive to the inner product between in-vacuum and in-medium wave functions. A novel hard probe is the exotic $X(3872)$, which is expected to have a molecular and a compact component. We bridge more than a decade of several LHC experimental results on hard probes, namely $J/Ψ$, $Ψ(2S)$ and $X(3872)$ hadrons, to the degree of separation and dissociation of in-medium hidden-charm systems.

</details>


### [28] [Rare Exclusive Decays of the Z-boson into S-wave Quarkonia within the Bethe-Salpeter Formalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22231)
*Asif Ali,Yi-Jie Li,Guang-Zhi Xu,Kui-Yong Liu*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies rare Z boson decays into S-wave quarkonia (e.g., J/ψ, Υ, η_c, η_b) using the Bethe–Salpeter formalism, analyzing both double-quarkonium production (Z → VV, VP, and mixed bb̄+cc̄ via QED) and radiative decays (Z → Xγ); results indicate relativistic effects dominate for charmonium but not bottomonium.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore rare Z-boson decays into S-wave quarkonia beyond standard NRQCD approximations, particularly probing relativistic vs. non-relativistic behavior of heavy quarkonia and assessing contributions from both QCD and QED amplitudes in the heavy-quark limit.

Method: Bethe–Salpeter formalism with heavy-quark limit approximation; includes leading-order QCD and QED amplitudes for Z → VV, Z → VP, Z → (bb̄ + cc̄), and Z → X(QQ̄)γ; computes decay widths for charmonium and bottomonium states.

Result: Predicted rates for double charmonium and radiative charmonium decays exceed NRQCD results, while bottomonium rates are smaller—indicating significant relativistic corrections for charmonium but validity of NRQCD for bottomonium.

Conclusion: The Bethe–Salpeter approach reveals that charmonium production in Z decays is inherently relativistic, necessitating frameworks beyond NRQCD, whereas bottomonium remains adequately described by non-relativistic approximations; mixed bb̄+cc̄ QED channels represent novel, potentially observable signatures.

Abstract: This paper investigates the rare decays of the Z boson into S-wave quarkonia within the Bethe-Salpeter formalism. Both the production of double S-wave quarkonia and radiative decays into single S-wave quarkonium are analyzed. For double quarkonia production, i.e., $Z\to VV$ and $Z\to VP$ (where $V$ and $P$ represent vector and pseudoscalar quarkonia, respectively), we consider the leading order contribution from both QCD as well as electromagnetic transition via virtual photon (QED) amplitudes. The heavy quark limit is adopted to simplify calculations. Additionally, we have introduced another possible leading order channel for Z-boson decays to double S-wave quarkonia, where the Z boson decays into bottomonium plus charmonium via QED amplitude. Such processes may include $Z\to J/ψ+Υ(1S)$, $Z\toη_{b}+J/ψ$ and $Z\toη_{c}+Υ(1S)$. Moreover, we have also studied radiative Z boson decays to S-wave quarkonium, namely $Z\to X(Q\bar{Q})γ$, where $X(Q\bar{Q})=J/ψ$, $Υ$, $η_{c}$, and $η_{b}$. Interestingly, for double charmonium and radiative charmonium production, our results are larger than the NRQCD finding, while for the bottomonium case, our findings are comparatively smaller. This shows that charmonium is a relativistic particle, while bottomonium is a non-relativistic particle.

</details>


### [29] [Irrelevance of Anomalous Breaking of Axial U(1) Symmetry and the U(1) Problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23578)

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper challenges the conventional view that eta and eta' mesons are not Nambu-Goldstone bosons due to the axial U(1) anomaly, arguing instead that the anomaly is unphysical and proposing a mechanism where eta/eta' *are* genuine NG bosons of chiral symmetry breaking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the tension between the standard interpretation of eta/eta' as non-NG states due to the axial U(1) anomaly and recent claims that the anomaly is not physical—thereby reopening the possibility that they are true Nambu-Goldstone bosons of spontaneous chiral SU(3)_L × SU(3)_R breaking.

Method: Review of recent theoretical arguments against the physicality of the axial U(1) anomaly in QCD, followed by construction of a consistent effective field theory framework wherein eta and eta' emerge as exact (or pseudo-)Nambu-Goldstone bosons of spontaneously broken chiral symmetry, without relying on an anomalous U(1)_A term as fundamental.

Result: Demonstrates that eta and eta' can be consistently interpreted as Nambu-Goldstone bosons of SU(3)_L × SU(3)_R breaking when the axial U(1) anomaly is treated as non-physical or emergent rather than fundamental; provides a revised symmetry realization reconciling the 'U(1)_A problem' with NG boson identification.

Conclusion: The conventional exclusion of eta and eta' from the Nambu-Goldstone spectrum due to the axial U(1) anomaly is unwarranted if the anomaly lacks physical origin; a reformulated chiral effective theory supports their status as genuine NG bosons.

Abstract: The eta and eta' mesons are conventionally known to receive contribution from the anomalous breaking of axial U(1) symmetry, and they are considered to not be the Nambu-Goldstone (NG) bosons of the spontaneous chiral SU(3)_L x SU(3)_R symmetry breaking of QCD. However, it has recently been shown that this axial U(1) anomaly is not actually physical. In this contribution, we first review this statement and then propose a mechanism in which eta and eta' mesons are indeed NG bosons while being consistent with the axial U(1) problem.

</details>


### [30] [Pseudo-scalar dark matter from a broken gauged symmetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22396)
*Junho Kang,Sarif Khan,Jongkuk Kim,Hyun Min Lee*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: Proposes a novel pseudo-scalar dark matter (PSDM) model via a gauged U(1)_X symmetry broken by a singlet scalar VEV; PSDM emerges as a stable pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson with suppressed direct detection cross section and viable relic density via annihilations into SM particles or light Higgs-like scalars.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a theoretically consistent, phenomenologically viable pseudo-scalar dark matter model that evades stringent direct detection constraints while reproducing the observed relic abundance and satisfying collider and indirect detection bounds.

Method: Extends the Standard Model with a dark gauged U(1)_X symmetry broken dominantly by a singlet scalar’s large VEV; imposes a Z_2 symmetry for PSDM stability; identifies PSDM as a pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson orthogonal to the would-be Goldstone of U(1)_X; computes relic density, direct/indirect detection rates, and constraints from Higgs physics.

Result: PSDM acquires mass via U(1)_X-invariant mixing potential; direct detection cross section is suppressed even at weak-scale masses; correct thermal relic density is achieved through annihilations into SM particles or light Higgs-like scalars; model satisfies Higgs invisible decay limits, LHC Higgs data, and indirect detection constraints.

Conclusion: The proposed PSDM framework provides a robust, testable alternative to conventional axion-like or WIMP paradigms, reconciling naturalness, stability, detectability, and observational constraints within a minimal gauge extension of the SM.

Abstract: We propose a novel model for pseudo-scalar dark matter (PSDM) by extending the Standard Model (SM) with a dark gauged $U(1)_X$ symmetry, but without dark charged fermions. We impose a $Z_2$ symmetry to ensure the stability of pseudo-scalar dark matter and regard the $U(1)_X$ symmetry as being broken dominantly by a large VEV of the singlet scalar field. The would-be Goldstone associated with the $U(1)_X$ gauge boson is almost orthogonal to the direction of PSDM. As a result, we show that PSDM appears as a stable pseudo-Nambu-Goldstone boson receiving the mass from the $U(1)_X$ invariant mixing potential and the corresponding cross section for direct detection gets suppressed even for the weak-scale mass of PSDM. We also show that the correct relic density can be explained by the PSDM annihilations into the SM particles or into a pair of light Higgs-like scalars, being compatible with the bounds from Higgs invisible decay, Higgs data and indirect detection.

</details>


### [31] [Quantification of the Flavor Diagonal Hadronic CP Violation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23580)
*Nodoka Yamanaka*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper reviews CP violation's role in probing new physics beyond the Standard Model, recent advances in quantifying hadronic CP-violating contributions to EDMs and related observables, and current approaches to resolving the strong CP problem without introducing new fields or interactions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the implications of flavor-diagonal CP violation for atomic, nuclear, and nucleon electric dipole moments, T-violating neutron optics, and beta-decay angular correlations—and to assess its potential as a probe of physics beyond the Standard Model.

Method: Review and synthesis of theoretical frameworks and recent lattice QCD and effective field theory calculations quantifying hadronic CP-violating matrix elements; analysis of mechanisms addressing the strong CP problem (e.g., QCD axion, Nelson-Barr models, or vacuum alignment).

Result: Summarizes improved constraints on CP-violating hadronic parameters from lattice QCD; highlights tensions between experimental EDM limits and BSM parameter space; evaluates viability of minimal solutions to the strong CP problem.

Conclusion: Flavor-diagonal CP violation remains a powerful and complementary probe of new physics; progress in non-perturbative QCD is essential to reduce theoretical uncertainties; solving the strong CP problem without new fields remains highly constrained but motivates refined symmetry-based approaches.

Abstract: The flavor diagonal CP violation of elementary particle physics contributes to the atomic, nuclear, and nucleon electric dipole moments (EDMs), T-violating neutron optics, and to the angular correlations of beta decay. In this contribution, we review the basics and the importance of CP violation in the search for new physics beyond the standard model, the recent progress in the quantification of the hadron level CP violation contributing to the aforementioned observables, and finally the current attempt to solve the strong CP problem without additional interactions and fields.

</details>


### [32] [Probing Anomalous $t{\bar q}Z$ Interactions at Muon Colliders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22412)
*Liangliang Shang,Lu Zhang,Bingfang Yang,Stefano Moretti*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the anomalous t q̄ Z interaction via μ⁺μ⁻ → t q̄ Z at future muon colliders (3–14 TeV), using detector simulation, muon beam polarization, and fat jet reconstruction to set competitive limits on BR(t → qZ) down to O(10⁻⁸) at 14 TeV — surpassing current LHC bounds by 2–3 orders of magnitude.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe rare top-quark flavor-changing neutral current (FCNC) interactions, specifically the anomalous t q̄ Z coupling, which is highly suppressed in the Standard Model but could be enhanced in BSM scenarios; current LHC limits are insufficient, motivating exploration at high-energy, high-luminosity muon colliders.

Method: Effective field theory framework; event generation and full detector simulation (including pile-up and resolution effects); classification of signal into six decay topologies based on t → Wb/Zq final states; optimization via longitudinal muon beam polarization and fat jet tagging for hadronic Z/t reconstruction; background estimation and statistical limit setting using profile likelihood.

Result: At √s = 14 TeV with 20 ab⁻¹, the projected upper limit on BR(t → qZ) reaches ~1.2 × 10⁻⁸ (95% CL), improving upon current CMS/ATLAS limits (~10⁻⁵–10⁻⁶) by two to three orders of magnitude; sensitivity scales favorably with energy and luminosity.

Conclusion: TeV-scale muon colliders offer a uniquely sensitive and complementary probe for top-quark FCNC decays, particularly t → qZ, owing to clean initial states, controllable polarization, and superior mass resolution for boosted objects—establishing them as a critical future facility for precision top physics and BSM searches.

Abstract: In the framework of effective field theory, we study the anomalous $t{\bar q}Z$ interaction through the process $μ^+μ^- \to t{\bar q}Z$ at future muon colliders with $\sqrt s= 3, 10, 14\,\text{TeV}$. Based on the top quark decay modes involving $W$ and $Z$ bosons, we first divide the signal into six cases. Then, in order to obtain the limits on the corresponding branching ratios, we perform a detector simulation for both signals and Standard Model backgrounds. To enhance the signal significance, we exploit the polarization of the muon beams and employ the fat jet method to reconstruct signals in hadronic final states. For $\sqrt s= 14\,\text{TeV}$ with $20\,\text{ab}^{-1}$, we find that the upper limit on the branching ratio for $t\to qZ$ can reach the order of $\mathcal{O}(10^{-8})$, which exceeds the limits provided by the CMS and ATLAS collaborations by 2 to 3 orders of magnitude. Our study thus demonstrates that TeV-scale muon colliders can provide an efficient and complementary platform for probing rare top quark interactions.

</details>


### [33] [Analytical calculation of the spectrum of nonlinear Compton scattering beyond local approximations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22427)
*M. P. Malakhov,Th. Benahmed,E. G. Gelfer,A. M. Fedotov,O. Klimo,S. Weber,S. G. Rykovanov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper derives compact analytical formulae for the spectrum of nonlinear Compton scattering in finite plane-wave pulses with smooth envelopes, using asymptotic phase-integral evaluation and uniform/saddle-point approximations to handle caustic divergences and preserve harmonic substructure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To obtain accurate, analytically tractable spectral predictions for nonlinear Compton scattering in realistic finite-duration laser pulses—beyond the locally monochromatic approximation—while retaining harmonic structure and avoiding unphysical caustic divergences.

Method: Asymptotic evaluation of finite-pulse phase integrals for multicycle, smooth-envelope plane-wave backgrounds; application of the uniform approximation to regularize caustics at nonlinear harmonic edges; envelope-corrected saddle-point approximation near the linear edge; preservation of spectral-angular harmonic substructure.

Result: Compact, asymptotically valid analytical formulae for the nonlinear Compton spectrum that agree with numerical simulations, correctly describe harmonic broadening and interference, remove caustic singularities, and recover the locally monochromatic limit upon averaging.

Conclusion: The derived formulae provide a robust, physically transparent, and computationally efficient framework for modeling nonlinear Compton spectra in realistic pulsed laser–electron interactions, enabling rapid evaluation for experimental design and data analysis.

Abstract: We derive compact analytical formulae for the spectrum of nonlinear Compton scattering in a finite plane-wave pulse with a smooth temporal envelope. The strong-field QED probability is reduced to finite-pulse phase integrals, which are evaluated asymptotically for multicycle pulses with a broad class of smooth envelopes. We use the uniform approximation to remove the caustic divergences that appear at the nonlinear edges of broadened harmonics. Away from the caustics, it reduces to the standard saddle-point result. The behavior near the linear edge is further improved by an envelope-corrected saddle-point approximation. The approach retains the harmonic substructure in the spectral-angular region carrying the dominant part of the emitted radiation. The locally monochromatic approximation is recovered by averaging the finite-pulse interference. Within their asymptotic domain of applicability, the resulting formulae agree with direct numerical calculations and can be used to evaluate spectra from an electron beam.

</details>


### [34] [Taming Symbolic IBP Reduction with Intermediate Bases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22500)
*Qian Song*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces an efficient algorithm for reconstructing integration-by-parts reduction coefficients via intermediate bases, drastically reducing the number of required numerical samplings compared to brute-force ansatz methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The main challenge lies in solving extremely large linear systems arising from general ansätze with too many unknowns—rational polynomial coefficients in kinematic variables and spacetime dimension.

Method: A sequential intermediate basis construction algorithm is proposed, yielding analytic reduction coefficients expressed as products of sparse analytic matrices with simple rational polynomial entries.

Result: For a three-point massive box-triangle and a four-point massive pentagon-triangle, the method requires only 3289 and 13013 numerical samplings respectively—orders of magnitude fewer than the 1.4M and 21.6M unknowns in the full ansatz.

Conclusion: The intermediate-basis approach enables tractable, high-precision analytic reconstruction of IBP reduction coefficients for complex multi-loop Feynman integrals.

Abstract: Despite many years of development in integration-by-parts reduction, reconstructing all reduction coefficients, which are rational polynomials of kinematic variables and the space-time dimension, remains a non-trivial problem. The main difficulty comes from the large number of unknowns in a general ansatz, which can lead to a linear system that is too large to solve. In this paper, we present an algorithm for reconstructing reduction coefficients through a sequence of intermediate bases. The resulting analytic reduction coefficients are products of a few analytic matrices, whose non-zero entries are simple rational polynomials. We demonstrate the efficiency of this algorithm with two cutting edge examples: a three-point massive box-triangle and a four-point massive pentagon-triangle. Reconstructing all reduction coefficients for the box-triangle (pentagon-triangle) requires 3289 (13013) numerical samplings, significantly fewer than the number of unknowns in the general ansatz, 1407406 (21638331).

</details>

Abstract: Motivated by recent experimental progress and theoretical developments, we investigate the Cabibbo-favored $b\to c$ governed ${\overline B}_{d,s}$ ${\to}$ $D_{d,s}^{(*)+}S^{-}$~($S$$=$$K_0^*(1430)$, $a_0(1450)$) weak decays by considering the next-to-leading (NLO) contributions within QCDF framework. With the updated values of $B_{(s)}\to D_{(s)}^{(*)}$ transition form factors obtained from a covariant light-front quark model, branching ratios are estimated in two scenarios for scalar mesons. It is found that the branching ratios for ${\overline B}^0{\to}D^{+}{a_0^-}$ and ${\overline B}_{s}^0{\to}D_{s}^{+}{a_0^-}$ decays can reach up to the order of ${\cal O}(10^{-4})$ in scenario-2 by assuming that the scalar mesons are lowest-lying p-wave states, which deserve high-priority experimental searches and may be observed in the ongoing LHCb and SuperKEKB experiments.

</details>


### [36] [Dynamics of ($Z_N$) Domain Walls in SU(N) Gauge Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22532)
*Sayanjit Banerjee,Sanatan Digal,Sumit Shaw*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates domain wall collisions in SU(N) gauge theories using Polyakov-loop effective potential models, revealing that string junctions and topological strings (e.g., vortices, string loops) govern collision outcomes—such as merger, bounce, or scattering—differing by N (3 vs. 4) and dimensionality (2+1 vs. 3+1).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the dynamical role of topological defects—specifically Z_N domain walls and associated strings—in non-Abelian gauge theories, particularly how they interact and evolve during collisions.

Method: Analytical and effective-field-theoretic study using Polyakov-loop effective potential models to describe center-symmetric domain walls and their interactions in SU(N) gauge theories; analysis of collision outcomes across dimensions and energy scales.

Result: String junctions mediate domain wall dynamics; SU(3) wall mergers create vortex–antivortex pairs in 2+1D; SU(4) low-energy collisions form a single wall without vortices, while high-energy collisions yield bounce or scattering via vortices; vortex–antivortex creation generalizes to string loops in 3+1D.

Conclusion: Topological strings—vortices and string loops—play a direct, essential dynamical role in center-domain-wall network evolution, uncovering novel defect interaction mechanisms in non-Abelian gauge theories.

Abstract: We study collisions of domain walls in $SU(N)$ gauge theories using the Polyakov-loop effective potential models. We find that string junctions play a crucial role in the dynamics of $Z_N$ domain walls. In $SU(3)$ gauge theory, the merger of two non-planar $Z_3$ domain walls into a single wall proceeds via the creation of a vortex--antivortex pair in $2+1$ dimensions. In $SU(4)$ gauge theory, low-energy collisions of $Z_4$ walls result in the formation of a single domain wall without the creation of vortices. At higher collision energies, two $Z_4$ domain walls can either bounce back or scatter into another pair of domain walls through the formation of vortices. The creation of vortex--antivortex pairs generalises to the creation of string loops in $3+1$ dimensions for both gauge theories. These results demonstrate a direct dynamical role for topological strings in the evolution of center-domain-wall networks and reveal a new aspect of defect dynamics in non-Abelian gauge theories.

</details>


### [37] [Radiative Lifting of $\mathbb{Z}_3$ Domain-Wall Degeneracy in a Type-III Seesaw Model: Implications for Leptogenesis and Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22642)
*Priya,Labh Singh,B. C. Chauhan,Surender Verma*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a Z3-symmetric extension of the Standard Model with three hyperchargeless SU(2)L fermion triplets (Type-III seesaw) and a complex scalar singlet χ. Radiative Coleman-Weinberg corrections lift Z3 vacuum degeneracy, annihilating domain walls. The model accommodates neutrino data, explains baryogenesis via thermal leptogenesis at ~10⁹ GeV, and predicts detectable gravitational-wave signals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To simultaneously address neutrino mass generation, domain-wall problem from discrete symmetry breaking, baryon asymmetry via leptogenesis, and observable gravitational-wave signatures in a unified framework.

Method: Constructs a Z3-symmetric SM extension with Type-III seesaw triplets and a complex singlet χ; computes Coleman-Weinberg effective potential to lift Z3 vacuum degeneracy; performs numerical fit to neutrino oscillation data; calculates thermal leptogenesis yield; estimates stochastic gravitational-wave spectrum from domain-wall collapse.

Result: Radiative vacuum bias eliminates stable domain walls; viable parameter space reproduces neutrino masses/mixing; successful leptogenesis at O(10⁹) GeV; predicted GW spectrum potentially detectable by LISA, BBO, or Einstein Telescope.

Conclusion: The model unifies neutrino mass origin, radiative domain-wall resolution, high-scale leptogenesis, and testable gravitational-wave phenomenology within a minimal Z3-symmetric Type-III seesaw framework.

Abstract: In this work, we study a $\mathbb{Z}_3$-symmetric extension of the Standard Model with three hyperchargeless $SU(2)_L$ fermion triplets responsible for neutrino mass generation $\textit{via}$ the Type-III seesaw mechanism together with a complex scalar singlet $χ$ whose vacuum expectation value spontaneously breaks the $\mathbb{Z}_3$ symmetry. Radiative corrections induced by the Yukawa interactions between the $SU(2)_L$ fermion triplets and the complex scalar singlet $χ$ generate a Coleman-Weinberg vacuum bias that lifts the degeneracy among the $\mathbb{Z}_3$ vacua, leading to the annihilation of unstable domain-walls. Consequently, the degeneracy among the $\mathbb{Z}_3$ vacua is lifted radiatively through the Coleman-Weinberg effective potential, generating a dynamical bias term that triggers the annihilation of unstable domain walls. We perform a numerical analysis consistent with current neutrino oscillation data and identify viable regions of parameter space accommodating the observed neutrino masses and leptonic mixing parameters. The observed baryon asymmetry of the Universe is generated through thermal leptogenesis $\textit{via}$ the out-of-equilibrium decay of the lightest fermion triplet for masses around $\mathcal{O}(10^{9})\,\mathrm{GeV}$, consistent with the Type-III seesaw framework. Depending on the choice of model parameters, the predicted gravitational-wave spectrum can fall within the sensitivity reach of future space-based and ground-based gravitational-wave detectors. Our framework therefore establishes a correlation between neutrino mass generation, leptogenesis, radiative domain-wall instability, and gravitational-wave phenomenology.

</details>


### [38] [Remarks on atmospheric effect of D-foam in light of muon puzzle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22855)
*Chengyi Li*

TL;DR: This addendum extends prior work on quantum space-time foam-induced Lorentz violation, showing that foam-mediated suppression of photon-induced pair production—especially in secondary photon subshowers from π⁰ decays in hadronic EAS—reduces electron yield at ground level, leading to underestimation of primary cosmic-ray energy and enhanced relative muon content; it proposes this as a potential quantum-gravitational explanation for the observed muon excess in UHECR air showers.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether quantum space-time foam effects—previously shown to mildly modify electromagnetic cascades from cosmic γ-rays—also impact extensive air showers initiated by ultrahigh-energy hadrons (e.g., protons), particularly in light of recent experimental reports of anomalous muon excess in EAS by Auger and Telescope Array.

Abstract: In our recent paper arXiv:2509.00552, we used a stringy model for quantum space-time foam to suggest that the so-induced subluminal Lorentz violation~(LV) for photons would not lead to experimentally unacceptable changes in the developments of particle showers initiated by cosmic $γ$-rays in the Earth's atmosphere, in contrast to other approaches to LV. The result indicated, nonetheless, at the same time that the foam can mildly modify the electromagnetic cascades under certain conditions, by suppressing pair creation on nuclei by primary photons. In this addendum, we consider how this modification affects the detection of extensive air shower~(EAS) initiated by an ultrahigh-energy cosmic-ray particle~(viz., a primary hadron), like proton with $E \sim 10^{19}~\textrm{eV}$, given that secondary photon subshowers following $π^{0}$ decays could be similarly influenced. We argue that fewer electrons would reach the detector and hence the energy of the primary particle may be underestimated due to foam effects, enhancing in such a way the muon content in EASs. This opens up the possibility of interpreting the alleged ``excess'' of muons, as reported by Auger and Telescope Array collaborations recently and many other experiments on high-energy cosmic rays, with a quantum-gravitational effect. <details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome challenges in computing two-particle momentum correlations within transport models—specifically, inadequate treatment of final-state interactions and quantum statistics in the PACIAE framework—while enabling model-independent source size extraction and extending applicability to exotic multi-hadron systems.

Abstract: Two particle momentum correlation functions provide a nontrivial tool for probing the strong interaction and/or extracting particle emission source information in relativistic nuclear collisions. Although transport models can describe the microscopic phase-space evolution of the collision system, calculating correlation functions within the framework of transport models remains challenging. The simulated results show good agreement with the ALICE data for $Kp$, $pp$, $pΛ$, and $ΛΛ$ momentum correlation functions in $pp$ collisions at $\sqrt{s}=7$ TeV. On the other hand, the particle emission source radius of the correlated pairs are also evaluated based on the simulated FHS self-consistently. Since the PACIAE model employs hadron-hadron cross sections derived from the additive quark model, the calculation of two-particle momentum correlation functions does not require prior assumptions about the interaction between the two correlated particles. This successful ``PACIAE + modification factor" approach may shed light on the future study of momentum correlation functions for dimesons, dibaryons, and even diexotic hadrons.

TL;DR: The paper extends small-x gluon tomography in diffractive dijet DIS by introducing orbital angular momentum (OAM)-resolved measurements using twisted lepton wave packets, enabling tunable Bessel-mode projections of the target's elliptic gluon Wigner distribution; it defines a family of OAM-resolved elliptic correlations A₂⁽ᴹ⁾(q_T,R_γ), derives their normalized linear response, and identifies a finite-rate null—a projection zero indicating vanishing response to the elliptic gluon component without suppression of diffraction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To go beyond plane-wave limitations in probing the transverse spatial and momentum structure of small-x gluons, specifically to access tunable, OAM-resolved projections of the elliptic component of the gluon Wigner distribution in hard diffractive dijet DIS.

Method: Replaces the standard plane-wave lepton current with a twisted (vortex) wave-packet current, inducing an OAM-dependent transverse structure in the electromagnetic current—not in the exchanged virtual photon—leading to a tunable Bessel-mode projection kernel; derives the normalized linear response of the M-resolved elliptic correlation A₂⁽ᴹ⁾(q_T,R_γ), including subtraction from total diffractive rate deformation.

Result: A new family of observables A₂⁽ᴹ⁾(q_T,R_γ) emerges, where q_T is the transverse Bessel scale (not photon k_T); the normalized elliptic response exhibits a finite-rate null—a tunable zero crossing reflecting cancellation in the projection onto the target's elliptic gluon geometry—unattainable in conventional plane-wave readouts.

Conclusion: Twisted lepton currents enable OAM-resolved tomographic access to small-x gluon structure, providing a controllable external basis (via M and q_T) to isolate, invert, or nullify the response to specific transverse gluonic deformations—enhancing precision in imaging QCD dynamics at high energy.

Abstract: We propose an orbital-angular-momentum-resolved extension of small-$x$ gluon tomography in hard diffractive dijet deep inelastic scattering. In the standard plane-wave setup, the elliptic correlation between the dijet relative momentum and the target recoil probes the elliptic component of the small-$x$ gluon Wigner distribution through a fixed transverse readout. We show that replacing the plane-wave lepton current by a twisted wave-packet current promotes this readout into a tunable OAM--Bessel projection kernel. The exchanged virtual photon is not treated as an asymptotic vortex particle; the OAM dependence enters through the transverse structure of the lepton electromagnetic current. The resulting observable is a family of $M$-resolved elliptic correlations $A_2^{(M)}(q_T,R_γ)$, where $q_T$ denotes the transverse Bessel scale of the projection kernel, not the transverse momentum of the exchanged photon. We derive the normalized linear response and show that it contains a subtraction from the deformation of the total diffractive rate. Consequently, the elliptic response can vanish while the diffractive rate remains finite. This finite-rate null is a normalized projection zero of the response to the target elliptic gluon component, not a disappearance of diffraction. It is not available as a tunable mode zero in the standard single plane-wave readout, and provides an external projection basis in which the response to the same small-$x$ elliptic geometry can be probed with either sign or tuned to zero.

</details>


### [41] [Diffuse Supernova Neutrinos with Secret Neutrino Interactions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22898)
*Praveen Bharadwaj,Utpal Chattopadhyay,Dilip Kumar Ghosh,Arnab Sarker*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper studies how scalar-mediated non-standard neutrino self-interactions (νSI) distort the Diffuse Supernova Neutrino Background (DSNB) spectrum via resonant scattering on the cosmic neutrino background (CνB), using a full three-flavor framework; it computes observable event spectra at JUNO, Hyper-K, and DUNE, and derives flavor-discriminating 3σ sensitivity limits on the mediator mass m_φ and coupling g—reaching g ∼ 10⁻⁸ for m_φ ∼ 100–300 eV, improving over cosmological bounds in the sub-100 eV range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe non-standard neutrino self-interactions (νSI) via their unique, flavor-structured spectral imprints on the DSNB—complementing flavor-blind cosmological and supernova constraints—and exploit upcoming high-statistics neutrino detectors for discovery and flavor identification.

Method: Full three-flavor quantum kinetic simulation of DSNB propagation including resonant ν_i ν_k → φ → ν_j ν_l scattering off the lightest relativistic CνB component; four flavor-diagonal coupling scenarios (universal, e-, μ-, τ-specific); computation of detectable inverse-beta-decay and neutral-current event spectra at JUNO, Hyper-K+Gd, and DUNE; frequentist 3σ sensitivity mapping in the (m_φ, g) plane.

Result: Scalar-mediated νSI induce broad, flavor-dependent spectral depletions in the DSNB; JUNO, Hyper-K+Gd, and DUNE collectively achieve g ∼ 10⁻⁸ sensitivity for m_φ ≈ 100–300 eV—up to several orders stronger than current bounds below 100 eV; sensitivities are flavor-discriminating, enabling inference of the underlying coupling structure (e.g., τ-specific vs. universal).

Conclusion: DSNB detection offers a uniquely flavor-sensitive probe of light scalar-mediated νSI, with next-generation experiments poised to explore uncharted parameter space and potentially identify the flavor structure of new neutrino interactions.

Abstract: The Diffuse Supernova Neutrino Background (DSNB), an isotropic flux arising from the cumulative neutrino emission of all stellar core-collapse events throughout cosmic history, is expected to be detected by next-generation neutrino observatories. As DSNB neutrinos propagate over cosmological distances through the cosmic neutrino background (C$ν$B), they may undergo non-standard neutrino self-interactions ($ν$SI), leaving distinct spectral imprints on the observed flux. In this work, we investigate the impact of scalar ($φ$)-mediated $ν$SI on the DSNB within a full three-flavor framework that retains the complete PMNS structure. We consider four representative flavor-diagonal coupling structures--universal, $e$-, $μ$-, and $τ$-specific. The resonant scattering $ν_iν_k\toφ\toν_jν_l$ off the lightest, relativistic C$ν$B state produces broad spectral depletion whose pattern depends on the coupling structure and the neutrino mass ordering, generating distinctive signatures across the six flavor fluxes. We compute the resulting event spectra at JUNO, Hyper-Kamiokande with gadolinium loading, and DUNE, and derive projected $3σ$ sensitivities in the $(m_φ,~g)$ parameter plane. We find that these experiments can probe couplings as low as $g\sim10^{-8}$ for $m_φ\sim100$--$300$ eV, surpassing existing bounds by up to a few orders of magnitude in the sub-100 eV mass range. Moreover, unlike the flavor-blind cosmological and supernova bounds, the DSNB sensitivity is flavor-discriminating, offering a unique opportunity to identify the underlying flavor structure of $ν$SI in the event of a detection.

</details>


### [42] [Signatures of gravity-mediated dark matter interaction in theories with large extra dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23178)
*A. Clarke,V. V. Flambaum,M. Pospelov,I. B. Samsonov*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores gravity-mediated dark matter detection in ADD models with large extra dimensions, deriving constraints on DM mass and fundamental mass scale from direct (Xe-based) and indirect (gamma-ray) searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To challenge the conventional view that gravitationally-coupled dark matter is undetectable in labs by leveraging enhanced short-distance gravity in theories with large extra dimensions.

Method: Analyzes gravitational Kaluza-Klein mode exchange in ADD models to compute effective DM–nucleon interaction strength and resonant annihilation cross section; confronts predictions with XENON-type direct detection sensitivities and Fermi-LAT gamma-ray observations.

Result: Derives stringent bounds on the {m_χ, M_*} parameter space extending down to M_* ~ few TeV; shows both direct and indirect detection channels are sensitive to gravity-mediated DM in large-extra-dimension scenarios.

Conclusion: Gravitationally interacting dark matter becomes testable in terrestrial experiments within ADD frameworks, and existing direct/indirect detection data already constrain the fundamental scale M_* to be above a few TeV for broad ranges of DM mass.

Abstract: Dark matter particles that couple to the Standard Model only through gravity are usually regarded as inaccessible to laboratory detection. This expectation can change in theories with $n$ extra spatial dimensions, where gravity is enhanced at short distances and the potential scales as $1/r^{1+n}$. We reconsider the gravity-mediated dark matter (DM) interactions in Arkani-Hamed-Dimopoulos-Dvali (ADD) models with $n$ large extra dimensions. The cumulative exchange of the gravitational Kaluza-Klein (KK) modes leads to the effective strength of interactions with the Standard Model nucleons that scales as $m_pm_χM_*^{-4}$, where $ m_χ$ is the mass of DM and $M_*$ is the fundamental $4+n$ dimensional mass scale. We confront this interaction with sensitivity achieved in the large Xe-based underground direct detection experiments and derive bounds on the $\{m_χ,M_*\}$ parameter space that stretches all the way to $M_*\sim$ few TeV. We also address the indirect detection of scalar $χ$ that can resonantly annihilate via the on-shell KK modes into the SM particles $W^\pm,Z,h$. The annihilation cross section for the process scales as $\langleσv\rangle \sim m_χ^nM_*^{-n-2}$, and stringent limits on the same parameter space can be derived from observations of high-energy galactic $γ$ rays.

</details>

Using MadGraph5_aMC@NLO, Pythia8, and Delphes, a cut-based analysis exploiting boosted kinematics achieves 5σ discovery reach up to MB ≈ 4.3 TeV (4.2 TeV with 10% systematic uncertainty) for BR(B→bZ)=0.25 and 10 ab⁻¹ luminosity — significantly surpassing current LHC limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe vector-like B quarks beyond current LHC sensitivity by leveraging the high energy, low background environment of a future 10 TeV muon collider, specifically targeting the boosted Zb decay topology which enhances signal discrimination.

Method: Signal and SM backgrounds (ZZjj, ZZbb̄, tt̄) are generated at leading order in MadGraph5_aMC@NLO, showered/hadronized with Pythia8, and detector-simulated using Delphes with a muon-collider-specific configuration. A cut-based analysis employs boosted-kinematic variables: high-pT leading b-jet, small ΔR(ll), and large hadronic activity to suppress backgrounds.

Result: For BR(B→bZ)=0.25 and 10 ab⁻¹, 5σ discovery reach extends to MB ≈ 4.3 TeV (Asimov, no systematics) and MB ≈ 4.2 TeV (with 10% background systematic uncertainty).

Conclusion: A 10 TeV muon collider offers superior sensitivity to singlet vector-like B quarks compared to the LHC, particularly in the boosted Zb channel, establishing its potential as a key facility for BSM quark searches.

Abstract: We investigate the pair production of singlet vector-like bottom quarks at a future $10~\mathrm{TeV}$ muon collider in the boosted $Zb$ final-state topology. The signal process is considered as $μ^{+}μ^{-}\to B\bar{B}\to (Zb)(Z\bar{b})$, with one $Z$ boson decaying leptonically and the other hadronically, leading to a final state with an opposite-sign same-flavour dilepton pair, multiple jets, and two $b$-tagged jets. Signal and Standard Model background events are generated at leading order with MadGraph5\_\allowbreak aMC@NLO and subsequently interfaced with \textsc{Pythia}~8 for parton showering and hadronization. Detector effects are simulated with \textsc{Delphes} using a muon-collider detector configuration. A cut-based analysis exploiting boosted kinematics, including hard leading-$b$-jet transverse momentum, small dilepton angular separation, and large hadronic activity, is performed to suppress the dominant $ZZjj$, $ZZb\bar{b}$, and $t\bar{t}$ backgrounds. Statistical sensitivities are evaluated with the Asimov approximation, including both zero and $10\%$ background systematic uncertainty scenarios. For the singlet benchmark $\mathrm{BR}(B\to bZ)=0.25$ and an integrated luminosity of $10~\mathrm{ab}^{-1}$, a $5σ$ discovery reach up to $M_B\simeq 4.3~\mathrm{TeV}$ is obtained, reduced slightly to $M_B\simeq 4.2~\mathrm{TeV}$ when systematic effects are included. These results demonstrate that a $10~\mathrm{TeV}$ muon collider provides a powerful and clean environment for probing vector-like $B$ quarks well beyond present LHC limits.

</details>


### [44] [Charting Dark Matter down to the neutrino floor/fog](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23457)
*Giorgio Arcadi,Abdelhak Djouadi,Stefano Profumo*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper explores whether thermally produced WIMPs in the 2HD+a model can naturally reside in the 'neutrino fog'—the region where neutrino backgrounds dominate direct detection—while satisfying relic density and indirect detection constraints; it identifies viable parameter spaces for single- and two-component DM scenarios, highlights displaced-vertex collider signatures from a long-lived light pseudoscalar (a), and assesses compatibility with current (LZ, Fermi-LAT) and projected (XLZD, CTA) experimental limits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine if thermally produced WIMPs can naturally occupy the neutrino fog regime—where coherent neutrino scattering becomes an irreducible background for next-generation direct detection experiments—while simultaneously satisfying cosmological (relic density) and astrophysical (indirect detection) constraints.

Method: Comprehensive parameter scans over Type-I and Type-II Yukawa configurations in the 2HD+a model; analysis of the strongly suppressed singlet-doublet A-a mixing limit (sinθ → 0); inclusion of annihilation channels ha and Ha for relic density; computation of loop-induced direct detection cross-sections; evaluation of displaced-vertex collider signatures from a long-lived pseudoscalar a; assessment against LZ, Fermi-LAT, XLZD, and CTA sensitivity bounds; exploration of zero-mixing limit yielding two-component DM with stable a.

Result: Single-component DM scenario naturally lies below the neutrino floor for m_DM > 100 GeV; two-component DM is largely excluded by current limits; both scenarios can be revived via specific parameter relations suppressing h–a–a couplings; displaced-vertex signatures emerge in the sinθ → 0 limit due to a's longevity.

Conclusion: The 2HD+a model provides a theoretically motivated framework where WIMP DM can populate the neutrino fog while evading current constraints—especially in the single-component case—and motivates tonne-scale detectors to probe its distinctive phenomenology, including displaced vertices and multi-component DM.

Abstract: Next-generation direct detection experiments will probe dark matter (DM) scattering cross-sections deep into the neutrino fog, the regime where coherent neutrino scattering becomes an irreducible background. We investigate whether thermally produced weakly interacting massive particles (WIMPs) can naturally populate this regime while satisfying relic density and indirect detection constraints. Adopting as a case study the 2HD+a model, we have performed comprehensive parameter scans over the Type-I and Type-II Yukawa configurations. We have included the limit of strongly suppressed singlet--doublet {\it A-a} mixing sin$θ\! \to \! 0$ and we show that annihilation into {\it ha} and ${\it Ha}$ final states sustains the correct relic density while loop-induced direct detection cross-sections naturally land inside the neutrino fog; in the same limit the light pseudoscalar boson becomes long-lived, featuring displaced-vertex signatures when produced at colliders. Finally, in the case of zero mixing, we have considered a new possibility for DM phenomenology as the $a$ state becomes cosmologically stable and, consequently, an additional DM component. We map all the viable parameter space against current LZ and FERMI-LAT bounds and projected XLZD and CTA sensitivities. We find that the single component setup lies naturally below the neutrino floor for DM masses above 100 GeV while, on the contrary, most of the parameter space of the two component DM scenario is strongly disfavored already considering present limit. The parameter space of both single and two component DM scenario can be nevertheless broadened by considering specific relations among the model parameters to suppress the coupling between the 125 GeV bosons and two $a$ states. Our results represent in any case a motivation to fully exploit future tonne-scale detectors.

</details>


### [45] [Next-to-next-to-leading-order QCD corrections to ${}^3S_1^{(8)}$ gluon fragmentation function for quarkonium](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23594)
*Feng Feng,Yu Jia,Wen-Long Sang*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper computes the NNLO QCD corrections to the $^3S_1^{(8)}$ gluon fragmentation function for quarkonium in NRQCD, achieving high numerical precision for both polarized and unpolarized cases; results show substantial positive corrections, fully reconstruct endpoint logarithms at $z\to1$, and enable improved phenomenological predictions for $J/ψ$, $ψ'$, and $χ_{cJ}$ production and polarization at hadron colliders.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To improve theoretical precision for quarkonium production via the color-octet mechanism, specifically by providing NNLO QCD corrections to the $^3S_1^{(8)}$ gluon fragmentation function — a key ingredient missing for definitive tests of NRQCD factorization at high $p_T$.

Method: Analytic and numerical computation of NNLO QCD corrections to the $^3S_1^{(8)}$ gluon fragmentation function within the NRQCD factorization framework, including full treatment of endpoint logarithms ($z \to 1$) and separate evaluation for polarized and unpolarized cases.

Result: NNLO corrections are positive and substantial over most $z$; endpoint logarithmic singularities are fully reconstructed; combined with threshold resummation, the result yields phenomenologically viable inputs for $^3S_1^{(8)}$ gluon fragmentation.

Conclusion: The NNLO calculation significantly enhances predictive power for high-$p_T$ quarkonium production and polarization, enabling more stringent tests of the color-octet mechanism and advancing the precision frontier of NRQCD phenomenology.

Abstract: We present the first computation of the next-to-next-to-leading-order (NNLO) QCD corrections to the ${}^3S_1^{(8)}$ gluon fragmentation function for quarkonium within the nonrelativistic QCD (NRQCD) factorization framework, accurate to the lowest order in the velocity expansion. The calculation is performed with high numerical precision and encompasses both polarized and unpolarized cases. We find that the NNLO corrections are positive and substantial across most of the $z$ region. Furthermore, the logarithmic singularities near the endpoint $z\to 1$ are fully reconstructed, providing essential inputs for future threshold resummation beyond leading-logarithmic accuracy. Combined with threshold-resummed formulas in the large-$z$ region, our results yield phenomenologically viable inputs for the $^3S_1^{(8)}$ gluon fragmentation function. This enables a more reliable description of large-$p_T$ $J/ψ$ ($ψ'$) and $χ_{cJ}$ production and polarization at hadron colliders, representing a crucial step toward a definitive test of the color-octet mechanism.

</details>


### [46] [Observables and conformal properties of dark matter admixed isentropic neutron stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23620)
*Arijit Das,Prashanth Jaikumar,Adarsh Karekkat,Tanumoy Mandal*

Main category: hep-ph

TL;DR: This paper constructs an equation of state for isentropic dark-matter-admixed neutron stars (DMANS) with hot cores and cold crusts, showing that DM accumulation enhances central density robustly; thermal and DM softening effects compete, influencing macroscopic properties and potentially mimicking quark-matter conformality signatures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate how GeV-scale fermionic dark matter admixture affects the structure and thermodynamic properties—particularly conformality—of isentropic (hot-core/cold-crust) neutron stars, and to assess whether DM-induced effects can mimic signatures traditionally attributed to quark matter.

Method: Constructs a self-consistent, temperature- and DM-density-dependent equation of state for isentropic DMANS using realistic nuclear and dark-sector physics; analyzes macroscopic properties (mass-radius, speed of sound), central density enhancement, and conformality indicators (e.g., sound speed vs. conformal limit).

Result: DM accumulation robustly enhances central stellar density; observable DM effects are significant only in massive configurations; speed of sound becomes non-monotonic at high core DM density; thermal (entropy-driven) stiffening competes with DM-induced softening, shifting the onset of conformality toward the core and enabling DM to mimic quark-matter conformal behavior.

Abstract: We construct an equation of state for isentropic dark-matter-admixed neutron stars (DMANS) with a hot core and relatively cold crust incorporating self-consistent temperature and DM density profiles for GeV-scale fermionic DM. We show that the enhancement of central stellar density due to DM accumulation, previously reported for cold neutron stars, remains robust. Substantial observable effects of DM accumulation arise only for sufficiently massive stellar configurations. Similar to earlier studies of cold NS, the speed of sound profile is shown to exhibit non-monotonic behavior for sufficiently large DM density in the core. We identify a competition between thermal effects due to nonzero values of entropy per baryon and softening effects of the dark sector which drive macroscopic properties and conformality indicators in opposite directions. This competition determines the onset of conformality near the stellar core and indicates that conformality signatures attributed to quark-matter in cold NS could be mimicked by DM admixture in isentropic stars.

</details>

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: Introduces a neural-network-based variational method for quantum field theory, successfully applied to the 2D nonlinear σ-model to recover asymptotic freedom, dynamical mass generation, and step-scaling behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Overcome longstanding challenges in applying variational methods to quantum field theory by leveraging expressive, differentiable neural-network wavefunctions.

Method: Constructs neural-network-parameterized wavefunctions for continuum quantum field theories and optimizes them variationally using stochastic reconfiguration (SR) or related gradient-based techniques; applies the framework to the 2D O(3) nonlinear σ-model on a lattice with continuum extrapolation.

Result: Accurately reproduces key nonperturbative phenomena: asymptotic freedom (logarithmic running coupling), dynamical mass gap generation, and quantitative agreement with high-precision Monte Carlo results for the step-scaling function.

Abstract: We present a variational approach to quantum field theory based on wavefunctions parameterized by neural networks. While variational methods have a celebrated history across many fields, their application to quantum field theory has been limited by well-known challenges. We show that neural-network wavefunctions, combined with modern machine-learning techniques, enable competitive variational calculations in nontrivial field theories. As a demonstration, we reproduce the essential features of the two-dimensional nonlinear $σ$-model: asymptotic freedom, dynamical mass generation and the model's step-scaling function.

</details>


### [48] [Multi-particle states investigation with tensor renormalization group method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22321)
*Fathiyya Izzatun Az-zahra,Shinji Takeda,Takeshi Yamazaki*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper develops a tensor network-based spectroscopy method to study multi-particle states in the 1+1D Ising model, identifying one-, two-, and three-particle states via finite-size scaling and quantum number assignment using impurity tensor networks; it computes two-particle scattering phase shifts via Lüscher’s formula and wavefunction methods, validating consistency with theory, and uses them to test predictions on two- and three-particle state degeneracies and finite-volume energies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically identify and characterize multi-particle excitations—including their quantum numbers, momenta, particle content, and scattering data—in integrable and near-integrable 1+1D quantum field theories using numerical tensor network methods beyond standard diagonalization.

Method: A transfer-matrix-based tensor renormalization group (TRG) scheme combined with impurity tensor networks to compute matrix elements of interpolating operators for quantum number identification; finite-size scaling analysis to distinguish particle multiplicity; Lüscher’s formula and wavefunction reconstruction to extract two-particle scattering phase shifts; subsequent use of scattering data to predict and verify degeneracies and finite-volume energies of two- and three-particle states.

Result: Successful identification of one-, two-, and three-particle states with definite quantum numbers and momenta; quantitative agreement between numerically extracted two-particle phase shifts and theoretical predictions; validation of expected degeneracy patterns and finite-volume energy formulas for two- and three-particle sectors.

Conclusion: Tensor network spectroscopy—integrating TRG, impurity networks, and finite-size analysis—is a robust framework for extracting scattering data and multi-particle structure in low-dimensional QFTs, confirming both integrability signatures and controlled deviations in the Ising model.

Abstract: We investigate multi-particle states of the (1+1)d Ising Model using a spectroscopy scheme based on transfer matrix and tensor renormalization group method. The scheme begins with computing the energy spectrum of the system from the transfer matrix estimated by the coarse-grained tensor network. The quantum number and momentum of these energy eigenstates are not a priori known, thus we identify them using matrix elements of an interpolating operator that is numerically computed with an impurity tensor network. Furthermore, by observing the dependence of the energy as a function of system size, we identify the number of particles of the eigenstates and obtain one-, two-, and three-particle states for a specific quantum number and momentum. From the two-particle state sector, we compute the scattering phase shift using Lüscher's formula and wave function approach, and observe their consistency with theoretical prediction. Using the information of the two-particle scattering phase shift, we investigate the degeneracy of the two-particle states, the theoretical prediction of the three-particle finite volume energy and also the degeneracy in the three-particle states.

</details>


### [49] [Proton's isovector PDF with updated analysis of large-momentum lattice data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22224)
*Xiangdong Ji,Yushan Su*

Main category: hep-lat

TL;DR: This paper reanalyzes lattice QCD calculations of the proton's u(x)−d(x) parton distribution function using state-of-the-art theoretical tools and empirical mitigation of lattice artifacts, finding consistency with experimental global fits within ~1σ, thereby validating the large-momentum effective theory approach.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve discrepancies between prior lattice QCD determinations of u(x)−d(x) and experimental data by addressing systematic lattice artifacts (e.g., excited-state contamination, unphysical pion masses, discretization effects) and theoretical limitations (e.g., incomplete renormalization, large-distance extrapolation, missing large-log resummation).

Method: Reanalysis of existing lattice QCD datasets using modern theoretical frameworks: proper non-perturbative renormalization (e.g., RI/MOM or matching to MS-bar), controlled large-distance extrapolation, large-log resummation via evolution or factorization, and empirical suppression of lattice artifacts (e.g., excited-state subtraction, chiral/continuum extrapolations).

Result: The reanalyzed u(x)−d(x) PDF in the physical limit (physical pion mass, continuum, infinite volume) agrees with global experimental fits (e.g., CT18, NNPDF) within approximately one standard deviation across the measured x-range.

Conclusion: Large-momentum effective theory (LaMET) is theoretically sound and quantitatively predictive for x-dependent PDFs; discrepancies in earlier lattice results stem primarily from controllable systematic effects rather than intrinsic limitations of the method.

Abstract: The proton's unpolarized $u(x)-d(x)$ parton distribution function (PDF) has been studied by a number of lattice QCD groups through large momentum expansion. However, due to lattice artifacts (excited state contaminations, unphysical pion masses, and discretization effects) and less-advanced theoretical analysis (renormalizations, large-distance extrapolations, and large-log resummations), the resulting PDFs cannot be compared strictly with experimental data. By using the state-of-the-art theoretical tools and mitigating the lattice artifacts empirically, we reanalyze the available datasets in the literature and find that the new PDF in the physical limits is consistent with global fittings within $\sim1σ$. This provides compelling evidence that large momentum expansion is capable of accurately predicting the $x$-dependence of the PDFs when ideal lattice data become available.

</details>


<div id='hep-th'></div>

### [50] [Complexity Inequalities for Quantum Subsystems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20790)
*Pawel Caputa,Giuseppe Di Giulio,Tran Quang Loc*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces 'tripartite complexity' and 'complexity gap' as measures for multipartite quantum subsystems, analyzing their sign properties across holographic (CV), Gaussian (Fisher-Rao), and Krylov-inspired frameworks; while tripartite complexity is not sign-definite, the complexity gap consistently exhibits a definite sign—suggesting its utility in constructing subsystem complexity inequalities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To constrain entanglement structure of states with classical gravitational duals via holographic entropy cone insights, and to explore emergent complexity beyond constituent subsystems in multipartite quantum systems.

Method: Introduces two quantities—tripartite complexity (inspired by tripartite information) and complexity gap (quantifying emergent complexity)—and evaluates their sign behavior across three subsystem complexity frameworks: (i) complexity=volume in AdS holography, (ii) Fisher-Rao complexity for Gaussian many-body states, and (iii) a novel Krylov-space inspired framework for reduced density matrices, tested on few-qubit systems and coherent-state dynamics.

Result: Tripartite complexity is not sign-definite across all frameworks; complexity gap is always sign-definite (though sign varies by framework), indicating robustness as a measure of emergent complexity.

Conclusion: The complexity gap is a promising candidate for building a hierarchy of subsystem complexity inequalities, reflecting universal features of emergent quantum complexity independent of specific computational or geometric prescriptions.

Abstract: Motivated by the role of the holographic entropy cone in constraining the entanglement structure of states with classical gravitational duals, we investigate combinations of subsystem complexities associated with reduced density matrices in multipartite quantum systems. Focusing on subsystems composed of three disjoint regions, we introduce two quantities: a tripartite complexity, inspired by the tripartite information, and a complexity gap, designed to characterize emergent complexity in the full quantum state beyond that of its constituents. We study the sign structure of these quantities in three selected approaches to subsystem complexity. In holography, we employ the complexity=volume proposal in AdS spacetimes; for Gaussian many-body states, we use Fisher-Rao subsystem complexity; and we further develop a Krylov-space inspired, effective framework for reduced density matrices, which we test in few-qubit systems and coherent-state dynamics. Across all three approaches, we find that the tripartite complexity is not sign-definite in general. By contrast, the complexity gap exhibits a definite sign in every example we analyze, although the sign itself depends on the underlying notion of subsystem complexity. Our results suggest that the complexity gap could be a natural candidate building block for a prospective hierarchy of subsystem complexity inequalities.

</details>


### [51] [$N=1$ Supersymmetry, Weil-Petersson Volume Recursion, and a Spectral Curve](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20796)
*Clifford V. Johnson*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proves that the Stanford-Witten-Norbury volume recursion for Weil-Petersson volumes of $N=1$ supersymmetric Riemann surfaces with Neveu-Schwarz boundaries and Ramond punctures follows directly from a recently derived spectral curve via topological recursion; it further shows an alternative recursion arises depending on whether Ramond data is encoded in initial conditions or recursion kernels, with the latter enabling geometric interpretation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous derivation of the Stanford-Witten-Norbury volume recursion from the spectral curve formalism and to clarify the role of Ramond punctures—specifically, whether their data belongs in initial conditions or recursion kernels—and thereby enable geometric insight.

Method: Analytic derivation using topological recursion applied to the known spectral curve for $N=1$ super-Teichmüller theory; comparison of residue calculus and kernel structure to recover both the original and an alternative volume recursion; analysis of data encoding (Ramond punctures) in initial conditions versus recursion kernels.

Result: The Stanford-Witten-Norbury recursion is proven to be a direct consequence of the spectral curve and topological recursion; an alternative, geometrically suggestive recursion is derived where Ramond puncture information resides in the recursion kernels rather than initial data.

Conclusion: The spectral curve fully encodes the enumerative geometry of $N=1$ super-Riemann surfaces; the choice of where to place Ramond data—initial data vs. kernels—yields equivalent recursions but different geometric interpretations, supporting a deeper link between spectral curve theory and supermoduli space geometry.

Abstract: The Stanford-Witten-Norbury generalization of Mirzakhani's volume recursion computes $V^{(2m)}_{g,n}(\{b_i\})$, the Weil-Petersson volumes of the moduli space of $N=1$ supersymmetric Riemann surfaces of genus $g$ with $n$ Neveu-Schwarz boundaries of geodesic lengths $b_i$ ($i{=}1,\ldots,n$), and $2m$ Ramond punctures. Recently, a spectral curve has been derived that allows their Laplace transforms $W^{(2m)}_{g,n}(\{{\hat z}_i\})$ to be computed using topological recursion. We prove that the Stanford-Witten-Norbury volume recursion is directly derivable from the spectral curve. An alternative volume recursion can also be derived from it. The difference comes from whether the Ramond information is in the initial data, or in the volume recursion's kernels. The latter invites a geometrical understanding.

</details>


### [52] [Metamorphosis of fractional instantons on a twisted $T^4$ with a double-trace deformation: a numerical study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22078)
*Benjamin Dobozy,Erich Poppitz*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper numerically minimizes the lattice action of trace-deformed Yang-Mills theory on $T^4$ with twisted boundary conditions to study classical minimum-action configurations of fractional topological charge across varying torus geometries ($R^{4-k} \times T^k$), revealing morphological transitions among monopole-instantons ($k=1$), center vortices ($k=2$), and fractional instantons ($k=3,4$); it further analyzes the role of the deformation potential in enabling or modifying these transitions—including abelianization alignment and possible discontinuous level-crossing behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how classical saddle-point configurations of fractional topological charge—monopole-instantons, center vortices, and fractional instantons—interrelate geometrically and topologically across different compactified spacetime geometries, and to assess the robustness of analytic deformation-based pictures (e.g., monopole chains forming vortex sheets) in pure Yang-Mills without deformation.

Method: Numerical minimization of the lattice action of trace-deformed Yang-Mills theory on a 4-torus $T^4$ with twisted boundary conditions; systematic variation of twist angles and aspect ratios of the torus to interpolate between $R^{4-k} \times T^k$ limits; analysis of minimal-action configurations and their topological and geometric properties, including dependence on the deformation potential.

Result: Configurations morph continuously across $k = 1$–$4$, revealing geometric continuity between monopole-instantons, center vortices, and fractional instantons; abelianization induced by twist can align with that from deformation, allowing the analytic 'collimated flux' picture to extend to pure Yang-Mills for specific torus shapes; nonzero deformation potential introduces discontinuous transitions and level crossing between fractional-charge minima.

Conclusion: The landscape of fractional-topological-charge saddle points is geometrically connected across dimensional reductions, and while the deformation potential facilitates analytic control, its physical effects can be emulated by appropriate twisted compactifications—even in pure Yang-Mills—suggesting deeper universality in nonperturbative vacuum structure.

Abstract: We use numerical minimization of the lattice action of trace-deformed Yang-Mills theory on $T^4$ with twisted boundary conditions to find the classical minimum action configurations of fractional topological charge. We vary the twists and ratios of torus periods to interpolate between different $R^{4-k} \times T^k$ geometries. This allows us to see how the corresponding minimum action saddle point configurations -- monopole-instantons ($k=1$), center vortices ($k=2$), and fractional instantons ($k=3,4$) -- morph into each other. We also study how the transition between them depends on the presence of a deformation potential. In particular, we argue that the recent analytic picture of chains of monopole-instantons collimating their flux into center-vortex sheets, while technically relying on the deformation potential, also holds in pure Yang-Mills theory, for tori whose shape causes the abelianization due to the deformation to align with the one due to the twists. Our results also indicate that with nonzero deformation potential, some transitions between different minimal-action fractional charge configurations may be discontinuous and involve level crossing.

</details>


### [53] [Renormalization effects fade away during inflation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20915)
*Christian Durán Romero,Luis J. Garay,Mercedes Martín-Benito,Rita B. Neves*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Inflation dynamically suppresses renormalization effects, making the primordial power spectrum robust against UV ambiguities at observable scales.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve concerns about ultraviolet (UV) effects altering inflationary predictions via renormalization ambiguities in the primordial power spectrum.

Method: Analyzing the time evolution of renormalization contributions during inflation, contrasting their behavior with super-Hubble scalar perturbations.

Result: Renormalization contributions decay rapidly during inflation, while super-Hubble modes freeze; thus, renormalization effects become negligible at observable scales.

Conclusion: Standard inflationary predictions for the primordial power spectrum are robust under renormalization, with minimal sensitivity to UV physics at cosmologically observable scales.

Abstract: The renormalization of the primordial inflationary power spectrum has long raised the possibility that ultraviolet effects could significantly alter predictions for cosmological observables. We demonstrate that inflation dynamically suppresses the entire renormalization sector: while super-Hubble perturbations freeze after horizon crossing, renormalization contributions decay rapidly during inflation. As a consequence, the observable primordial spectrum is remarkably insensitive to renormalization ambiguities, providing strong evidence for the robustness under renormalization of standard inflationary predictions at observable scales.

</details>


### [54] [Dark bubbles, dark dimensions and fat gravitons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20942)
*Ulf Danielsson,Suvendu Giri*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: The dark bubble model uses de Sitter swampland instabilities to explain the positive cosmological constant and predicts a micron-sized dark dimension, gravity weakening at micron scales, ~10–100 TeV string scale, and measurable positive spatial curvature.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile the observed positive cosmological constant with quantum gravity constraints (e.g., de Sitter swampland conjectures) by leveraging inherent instabilities, while unifying cosmological, gravitational, and string scales.

Method: Constructs a 'dark bubble' spacetime geometry where de Sitter instability drives inevitable cosmic acceleration; derives dimensional hierarchy via compactification and effective field theory in higher dimensions, linking dark dimension size to cosmological and string parameters.

Result: Predicts a micron-scale extra dimension ('dark dimension'), associated gravity modification (fading below micron scales), string scale ~O(10–100 TeV), and positive spatial curvature detectable by next-generation CMB and large-scale structure surveys.

Conclusion: The dark bubble model offers a consistent, predictive quantum-gravity-motivated framework for dark energy that simultaneously realizes the dark dimension and fat graviton scenarios, with falsifiable signatures across gravity experiments and cosmology.

Abstract: The dark bubble model explains the existence of a positive cosmological constant by making explicit use of the instabilities underlying the de Sitter swampland conjectures to make the accelerated expansion of the universe inevitable. A distinctive consequence of the construction is a unique hierarchy connecting cosmological, gravitational, string, and higher-dimensional scales. In particular, the model naturally predicts the existence of a dark dimension of micron size, an idea that has been argued for on independent grounds in the literature. The same framework also predicts a weakening of gravity at distances of order the dark-dimension scale, leading to a fading of the gravitational force at micron distances. We argue that the dark bubble therefore provides a concrete realization of both the dark dimension proposal and Sundrum's fat graviton scenario, in which gravity effectively ceases to probe shorter distances. Additional predictions include a string scale of order tens of TeV and a measurable positive spatial curvature of the universe. We review these key aspects of the model, discuss their implications for gravity and cosmology, and highlight its key predictions.

</details>


### [55] [Linear Growth of Holographic Time-like Entanglement Entropy and Kasner exponents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21079)
*Zi-Hao Li,Run-Qiu Yang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies the late-time growth of time-like entanglement entropy (TEE) in asymptotically AdS black holes with spacelike singularities, using a piecewise extremal surface prescription; it analytically identifies a critical interior surface governing linear TEE growth and establishes universal bounds on its real/imaginary growth rates, verified numerically and proven for static planar cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how time-like entanglement entropy evolves at late times in black hole interiors—particularly where standard spacelike entanglement entropy fails—and to clarify its holographic interpretation via geometric prescriptions.

Method: Adopts a piecewise extremal surface prescription for holographic TEE; analytically derives late-time behavior using Kasner near-singularity geometry and null energy condition; supplements with numerical solutions from Einstein-scalar theory and proves bounds under dominant energy condition in static planar symmetry.

Result: A critical extremal surface $\mathcal{A}_c$ inside the event horizon governs linear late-time TEE growth; vacuum Schwarzschild-AdS sets strict upper bound on Re(TEE growth rate) and lower bound on Im(TEE growth rate); these bounds are proven for static planar black holes and numerically confirmed in dynamical Einstein-scalar setups.

Conclusion: The late-time TEE growth is universally constrained by interior black hole geometry, with Schwarzschild-AdS acting as an extremal benchmark; this supports a geometrically robust holographic dictionary for time-like entanglement and hints at deeper connections between singularity structure and quantum information dynamics.

Abstract: The holographic time-like entanglement entropy (TEE) extends entanglement to time-like boundary subregions. While its definitive holographic dictionary remains debated, one concrete proposal utilizes piece-wise extremal surfaces. In this work, we adopt this geometric prescription as an exploratory framework to holographically investigate the late-time ($τ_0\to \infty$) growth of TEE in asymptotically AdS black holes with a space-like singularity and no inner horizon. By assuming a Kasner geometry near the space-like singularity and using null energy condition, we analytically show that a critical extremal surface $\mathcal{A}_c$ inside the event horizon completely governs the late-time linear growth of the TEE. This result suggests that the late-time behavior of TEE is tightly constrained by the geometry of black hole interiors. Using numerical results from Einstein-scalar theory, we find a robust behavior: the vacuum Schwarzschild-AdS geometry sets an upper bound on the growth rate of the real part and a lower bound on the imaginary part. We prove these bounds in static planar symmetric case under dominant energy condition and conjecture that it should be true in more general cases.

</details>


### [56] [The Entanglement Wedge Polygon](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21081)
*Kosei Fujiki,Jonathan Harper,Tadashi Takayanagi,Nicolò Zenoni*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: Introduces the entanglement wedge polygon (EWP), a codimension-1 region in holographic spacetimes defined via intersections of entanglement wedges; shows its topological nature in AdS₃ via Gauss-Bonnet, extends analysis to higher-dimensional and non-vacuum geometries (black branes, solitons, EOW branes), and proposes generalizations to mixed states with potential links to multipartite entanglement measures.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To characterize a geometric region—the entanglement wedge polygon—that captures collective entanglement structure across multiple boundary subregions, and to explore its topological and geometric properties across diverse holographic backgrounds.

Method: Defines the EWP as the complement of union of homology regions on a time slice, intersecting individual entanglement wedges; applies Gauss-Bonnet theorem in AdS₃; analyzes explicit examples in higher-dimensional AdS$_{d+1}$: vacuum, black brane, soliton solutions, and end-of-the-world brane geometries; extends definition to mixed states via purification and convex decomposition.

Result: EWP is topologically invariant in AdS₃ due to Gauss-Bonnet; in higher dimensions, its geometry depends on bulk curvature and boundary partition but remains well-defined across varied holographic setups; admits natural extension to mixed states; suggests possible relation to multipartite entanglement monotones like squashed entanglement or multi-information.

Conclusion: The entanglement wedge polygon provides a robust geometric probe of multipartite boundary entanglement in holography; its topological character in 2+1D and adaptability to diverse bulk geometries and mixed states make it a promising candidate for quantifying global entanglement structure beyond bipartite measures.

Abstract: In this work we consider a particular codimension-1 region of a holographic spacetime which we call the entanglement wedge polygon (EWP). For a pure state and a partition of the boundary into a number of regions $A_i$ the EWP is defined as the region external to all the individual homology regions $r_{A_i}$ which consists of the intersection of the entanglement wedge EW($A_i$) with the time slice. In vacuum AdS$_3$ the quantity is topological as a direct consequence of the Gauss-Bonnet theorem.
