In higher dimensions we make progress by considering a number of concrete examples including vacuum, black brane, and soliton solutions of AdS$_{d+1}$ as well as spacetime geometries with end of the world branes dual to boundary conformal field theories. We provide a suitable generalization to mixed states and comment on possible connections between the EWP and measures of multi-partite entanglement.

</details>


### [57] [Phase diagram of the massless Gross-Neveu model with two components](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21246)
*Michael Thies*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper extends the semiclassical analysis of the two-component Gross-Neveu model at finite density to finite temperature, constructing the full large-N phase diagram—including inhomogeneous phases—using stability analyses instead of solving the thermal Hartree-Fock problem, and identifies a critical filling fraction inducing a qualitative change in the phase structure.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize recent exact finite-density results for the Gross-Neveu model to finite temperature and explore how restricting the chemical potential to a subset of fermion flavors affects thermodynamic phase structure, particularly the emergence and stability of inhomogeneous phases.

Method: Semiclassical (large N) analysis combined with stability criteria—including translational, chiral, and modulational stability—to map the full phase diagram without solving the thermal Hartree-Fock equations explicitly.

Result: A complete large-N phase diagram incorporating homogeneous and inhomogeneous phases at finite temperature and density; identification of a critical filling fraction where the topology of the phase diagram changes qualitatively.

Conclusion: The interplay between flavor-selective chemical potential and thermal fluctuations leads to rich phase structure, with inhomogeneous phases persisting up to finite temperatures and a critical filling fraction acting as a structural bifurcation point in the phase diagram.

Abstract: Recently, Benini, Mamroud, Reis and Serone have presented exact results for the Gross-Neveu model at finite density, both at finite N and in the large N limit. Generalizing previous studies, they introduce a chemical potential acting only on a subset of fermion flavors - the two-component Gross-Neveu model. Here, we take up this idea and extend the semiclassical study to finite temperature. The full (large N) phase diagram including inhomogeneous phases is constructed without solving the thermal Hartree-Fock problem. All phase boundaries can be found on the basis of various kinds of stability analyses. As a result, we find a qualitative change of the phase diagram at some critical filling fraction.

</details>


### [58] [Non-relativistic limits of $\mathcal N=4$ supersymmetric Yang-Mills theory and S-duality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21494)
*Hyungrok Kim,Joseph Smith*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a family of non-relativistic limits of 4d MSYM via a Type IIB D3-$(p,q)$-string brane setup, yielding topological deformations of supersymmetric Galilean Yang-Mills or monopole moduli space quantum mechanics; these theories form a 3D moduli space with enriched PSL(2,Z) duality action beyond the relativistic S-duality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how the nonperturbative SL(2;Z) S-duality of relativistic 4d MSYM manifests in non-relativistic limits, and to classify consistent Galilean-invariant supersymmetric field theories arising from such limits.

Method: Uses a Type IIB brane construction (D3-brane + (p,q)-strings) to systematically define non-relativistic limits; analyzes resulting low-energy effective theories as topological deformations of supersymmetric Galilean Yang-Mills and quantum mechanics on BPS monopole moduli space; verifies duality via path integral (Abelian case) and spectral/symmetry matching (non-Abelian case).

Result: A three-dimensional moduli space of non-relativistic theories is identified, each labeled by (p,q) integers; Galilean theories admit θ-term and monopole-charge coupling deformations; PSL(2;Z) acts nontrivially on this moduli space, exhibiting richer structure than in the relativistic parent theory; duality is established exactly for Abelian case and supported spectroscopically and symmetrically for non-Abelian case.

Conclusion: Non-relativistic limits of 4d MSYM preserve and reorganize SL(2;Z) duality into a geometric action on a 3D moduli space, revealing novel topological deformations and symmetry structures—particularly invertible one-form electric/magnetic symmetries—in Galilean supersymmetric field theories.

Abstract: We investigate non-relativistic limits of four-dimensional maximally supersymmetric Yang-Mills theory (4d MSYM) and their relation to the nonperturbative $\operatorname{SL}(2;\mathbb Z)$ S-duality of the relativistic theory. We construct a general family of non-relativistic limits using a Type IIB brane set-up with a D3-brane and $(p,q)$-strings and show that the resulting theories are topological deformations of supersymmetric Galilean Yang-Mills theory or quantum mechanics on the moduli space of BPS monopoles. The deformations of the Galilean Yang-Mills theory are the familiar $θ$-term and a coupling to the monopole charge, while in the moduli space theory the only deformation is a $θ$-term. This family of theories fit together into a three-dimensional moduli space with nontrivial topology, on which $\operatorname{PSL}(2;\mathbb Z)$-valued dualities act in a richer and more complex way than in the relativistic parent theory. In the Abelian case, we establish the duality directly using the path integral, while in the non-Abelian case we support our claim by matching the one-particle spectrum as well as the Galilean spacetime symmetries and electric/magnetic invertible one-form symmetries.

</details>


### [59] [Asymptotically safe quantum gravity and its phenomenology -- a review](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21522)
*Astrid Eichhorn*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This review introduces asymptotically safe quantum gravity—a quantum field theory approach based on ultraviolet quantum scale symmetry (the Reuter fixed point)—and summarizes recent progress: robust evidence in 4D Euclidean pure gravity, inclusion of Standard Model and BSM matter, incorporation of Lorentzian signature, and cross-scale predictive power for particle physics, black holes, and cosmology; it also discusses connections to other quantum gravity frameworks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a pedagogical and up-to-date overview of asymptotically safe quantum gravity, emphasizing its viability as a predictive, UV-complete quantum field theory of gravity and its potential for empirical confrontation.

Method: Review and synthesis of theoretical developments in functional renormalization group (FRG) studies of quantum gravity, including evidence for the Reuter fixed point in pure gravity and matter-gravity systems, extensions to Lorentzian signature via spectral methods or Wick rotation, and phenomenological implications across energy scales.

Result: Robust evidence for asymptotic safety in 4D Euclidean pure gravity; consistent inclusion of Standard Model and BSM matter fields; emerging treatment of Lorentzian signature; high predictivity across scales—enabling testable predictions in particle physics, black hole physics, and early-universe cosmology.

Conclusion: Asymptotic safety is a viable, increasingly realistic, and predictive framework for quantum gravity; its connections with other approaches may reveal universal features of quantum spacetime.

Abstract: Asymptotically safe quantum gravity is an approach to quantum gravity. It is based on the premise that quantum field theory can describe the quantum nature of gravity in our universe. At its core lies quantum scale symmetry. This review provides an introduction to the key ideas of the approach and surveys the current status of the field. Over the last years, the field has taken large strides towards an increasingly realistic setting: First, compelling evidence for quantum scale symmetry exists in four-dimensional, Euclidean, pure gravity, establishing the Reuter fixed point robustly. Second, matter fields, including the Standard Model as well as beyond-Standard-Model-candidates, have been studied in depth, with increasingly conclusive evidence for quantum scale symmetry. Most recently, the final gap to a realistic description of quantum gravity is being closed, because Lorentzian spacetime signature can now be accounted for. As a consequence of quantum scale symmetry in the ultraviolet, the approach is highly predictive at all scales. This review discusses the physics of asymptotic safety across all scales. Predictive power for particle physics, black holes and cosmology provides a clear pathway to confronting quantum gravity with current and near-future observations. The review closes by discussing the connection to other approaches to quantum gravity. It advocates the perspective that such connections between approaches may lead us to an understanding of universal physical features of quantum gravity.

</details>


### [60] [Near-horizon modifications in finite $N$ holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21537)
*Rishkrith Bairy,Mrityunjay Nath,Debajyoti Sarkar*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates non-perturbative violations of bulk micro-causality in AdS/CFT at finite N, using boundary time cut-offs and HKLL smearing; it confirms consistent non-locality estimates in near-horizon modified AdS₂ and BTZ black hole backgrounds, where a throat parameter controls the scale of non-locality, and finds a dip-ramp-plateau spectral form factor in 3D probe dynamics—mirroring results from stretched horizon or brick wall models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how finite-N effects in AdS/CFT lead to non-perturbative breakdowns of bulk micro-causality, and whether such violations are universal across different black hole mimickers.

Method: Explicit bulk reconstruction using HKLL smearing with late boundary time cut-off; analysis in near-horizon modified AdS₂ and BTZ black hole geometries; spectral form factor computation for probe fields averaged over the throat parameter.

Result: Consistent non-locality scale set by the throat parameter in both black hole mimickers; emergence of dip-ramp-plateau structure in the averaged spectral form factor in 3D.

Conclusion: Non-perturbative causality violation is robust across different near-horizon geometries and tied to the throat scale; the dip-ramp-plateau in the spectral form factor suggests universal quantum chaotic features shared with stretched horizon/brick wall models.

Abstract: If one extends the AdS/CFT extrapolate dictionary to large but finite $N$, we are expected to obtain non-perturbative violations of bulk micro-causality. Previously this was achieved by implementing a late boundary time cut-off, while smearing the boundary operator via the HKLL kernel. By performing explicit bulk reconstructions in the backgrounds of near-horizon modified AdS$_2$ and BTZ black holes, we recover the same non-locality estimates as above. For these black hole mimickers, the near-horizon modification is controlled by a throat parameter which sets the scale of this non-locality. In three bulk dimensions, probe dynamics also exhibits a dip-ramp-plateau structure in their spectral form factor when averaged over the throat parameter. Such structure has also been found recently in the background with a stretched horizon or a brick wall.

</details>


### [61] [Asymptotic limits of constrained instantons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21561)
*Benjamin Elder,Kinga Gawrych,Arttu Rajantie*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper revisits false vacuum decay using a toy scalar field model with an unstable quartic potential, employing constrained instantons; it develops three asymptotic regimes—small, large, and large-Lagrange-multiplier—for analytic approximation of constrained instanton solutions, correcting and extending prior work by Affleck and Coleman.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically analyze false vacuum decay in a simple scalar field model using constrained instantons, addressing gaps and limitations in existing perturbative (Affleck) and thin-wall (Coleman) approaches.

Method: Constructs and analyzes constrained instanton solutions in three asymptotic limits: (i) small-field limit (perturbative, Affleck-type), (ii) large-field/thin-wall limit (Coleman-type), and (iii) large-Lagrange-multiplier limit (dimensional analysis + single numerical integration).

Result: Derives corrected perturbative expressions, identifies nonexistence conditions for the thin-wall limit, and establishes a robust large-multiplier regime yielding scalable analytic forms and an exact solution via one numerical computation.

Conclusion: Constrained instanton analysis of false vacuum decay admits multiple complementary asymptotic regimes; the large-Lagrange-multiplier limit provides a reliable, broadly applicable alternative when thin-wall fails, enhancing analytical control beyond standard approximations.

Abstract: We revisit the topic of false vacuum decay in field theory. We focus on a toy model of a real massive scalar field with an unstable quartic potential. This model has a false vacuum, and decay out of the false vacuum can be described via the method of constrained instantons, which work by introducing a constraint on the path integral. We identify and develop three different asymptotic limits which enable analytic construction of approximate {constrained} solutions. The first, in which the constrained solution is small compared to the inverse mass of the scalar field, is an application of the perturbative methods of Affleck, although we re-derive the main results and identify several terms which were previously neglected. Second, for very large constrained solutions we adapt the thin-wall approximation of Coleman. However, we find that the large instanton limit does not always exist. In this case we identify another useful limit, in which the Lagrange multiplier used to implement the constraint is large. In this limit, the solution's scaling with the parameters may be found via dimensional analysis and an exact solution is obtained with a single numerical computation.

</details>


### [62] [Quantum Gravity Cutoff from Axions: A Type IIB Landscape Study](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21650)
*Matthew Reece,Tom Rudelius,Christopher Tudball*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper quantitatively verifies a quantum gravity cutoff bound Λ_QG ≲ 2π√S f for extra-dimensional axions in Type IIB Calabi-Yau compactifications, confirming its validity near Kähler moduli space boundaries—including where axion string co-scaling breaks—thereby supporting its universality in quantum gravity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the proposed quantum gravity cutoff bound Λ_QG ≲ 2π√S f for extra-dimensional axions, motivated by naturalness and unitarity arguments, in a concrete string-theoretic setting.

Method: Analytical derivation and numerical verification within Calabi-Yau compactifications of Type IIB string theory, considering both C₂ and C₄ axions, including regimes near boundaries of the Kähler moduli space where axion string co-scaling fails.

Result: The bound Λ_QG ≲ 2π√S f holds robustly across the explored parameter space—including singular and boundary regions—supporting its status as a general quantum gravity constraint.

Conclusion: The bound is not an artifact of specific approximations but a robust feature of extra-dimensional axions in quantum gravity, reinforced by explicit string-theoretic computation.

Abstract: Extra-dimensional axions have coupling strength related to fundamental, ultraviolet physics. It has been proposed that the properties of such axions imply a bound on the quantum gravity cutoff: $Λ_\mathrm{QG} \lesssim 2π\sqrt{S} f$, where $f$ is the axion decay constant and $S$ is the instanton action. In the context of weakly-coupled string theory, we identify $Λ_\mathrm{QG}$ with the string scale $M_s$. In this paper, we carry out a quantitative study of this bound on the string scale in the context of Calabi-Yau compactifications of Type IIB string theory, considering both $C_2$ and $C_4$ axions. We show, both analytically and numerically, that the bound holds even near boundaries of the Kähler moduli space, including those where the co-scaling relationship for axion strings fails. This evidence bolsters previous arguments, based on naturalness and on unitarity, that the bound is a general feature of extra-dimensional axions in quantum gravity.

</details>


### [63] [On the Universality of Probe Complexity in $\mathcal{N}=4$ SYM](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21662)
*Eric L. Graef,Jeff Murugan,Horatiu Nastase,Hendrik J. R. Van Zyl*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies Krylov complexity of single-trace operators dual to open strings on giant gravitons in planar N=4 SYM, showing bounded Lanczos coefficients and integrable dynamics in protected/few-body sectors—hence insufficient for testing gravitational universality—and proposes a finite-density scaling regime as a precise boundary-theoretic test of universality.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To assess whether Krylov complexity growth in planar N=4 SYM can serve as a field-theoretic probe of recently proposed gravity-side universality conjectures, particularly for operators dual to open strings on giant gravitons.

Method: Analyzes Krylov complexity using orthogonal polynomial theory linked to the seed spectral measure; computes Lanczos coefficients {a_n, b_n} in protected and few-body sectors (fixed magnon number M, large open-string length L); introduces a finite-density scaling limit where magnon density ρ = M/L is held fixed to access chaotic/thermodynamic regimes.

Result: In the L→∞ limit at fixed M, Lanczos coefficients saturate: a_n = 2Mg, b_n → Mg — indicating band-limited, integrable Krylov dynamics; thus, these sectors cannot exhibit universal (e.g., exponential or ballistic) complexity growth expected in holographic chaos. A finite-density program is formulated to enable a meaningful universality test.

Conclusion: Protected and few-body sectors of giant graviton open-string operators are dynamically too constrained to probe gravitational universality of operator complexity; instead, universality must be tested in a thermodynamic limit where magnon density is finite, and the leading Krylov growth should depend only on coarse thermodynamic data (ρ, ε), not microscopic details.

Abstract: We investigate Krylov complexity for single-trace operators dual to open strings attached to giant gravitons in planar $\mathcal{N}=4$ super Yang-Mills theory. We show that in protected and few-body sectors, Krylov dynamics is governed by orthogonal polynomial theory associated to the seed spectral measure, leading to bounded Lanczos coefficients determined solely by spectral support. In particular, for fixed magnon number $M$ and open-string length $L\rightarrow\infty$, we derive $a_n=2Mg$ and $b_n\rightarrow Mg$, demonstrating integrable, band-limited dynamics. This establishes that such sectors are insufficient to test recently proposed gravity-side universality of operator complexity growth. We therefore formulate a finite-density program in which magnons scale with system size, and propose a concrete universality test: whether the leading Krylov growth depends only on coarse thermodynamic data $(ρ,\varepsilon)$ and not on microscopic probe structure. This provides a precise boundary-field-theory framework for testing gravitational universality conjectures.

</details>


### [64] [Holonomies and Boundary Symmetries in the Discrete Warped Chern-Simons Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22028)
*H. T. Özer,Aytül Filiz*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper develops a discrete warped Chern-Simons framework for 3D warped gravity using boundary holonomies and monodromy sectors, deriving entropy purely from discrete SL(2,R) × U(1) monodromy data and recovering continuum physics in the large-lattice limit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formulate a discrete, gauge-invariant description of warped 3D gravity that captures thermodynamic properties (e.g., black hole entropy) without relying on smooth geometric backgrounds—motivated by holography with warped CFTs and the need for a fundamentally holonomy-based foundation.

Method: Constructs a discrete boundary theory based on ordered products of link holonomies for SL(2,R) × U(1) gauge group; classifies physical sectors via conjugacy classes of SL(2,R) monodromies (hyperbolic/elliptic/parabolic) and U(1) holonomies; derives entropy from monodromy invariants; takes large-lattice limit to recover continuum Wilson loop conditions.

Result: A discrete entropy formula matching warped black hole and WCFT entropy in the continuum limit; explicit correspondence between discrete monodromies and continuous Wilson loops; classification of physical sectors via monodromy conjugacy classes; U(1) holonomy encodes warped charge contributions.

Conclusion: Warped gravitational thermodynamics can be fully encoded in boundary monodromy sectors within a discrete Chern-Simons framework, offering a background-independent, holonomy-centric alternative to conventional geometric approaches.

Abstract: We investigate a discrete warped Chern-Simons description of three-dimensional warped gravity based on boundary holonomies and monodromy sectors. Starting from the lower-spin SL(2, R) + U(1) gauge structure associated with warped AdS(3) holography and warped conformal field theories (WCFTs), we construct a discrete boundary framework in which ordered products of link holonomies replace continuous gauge connections along noncontractible cycles. In this setting, boundary monodromies become the primary gauge-invariant observables characterizing the physical sectors of the theory. We show that the hyperbolic, eliliptic, and parabolic sectors naturally arise from the conjugacy classes of the discrete SL(2, R) monodromy, while the additional U(1) holonomy supplies the warped contribution to the boundary charges. Using these monodromy invariants, we derive a discrete entropy relation entirely from boundary holonomy data without relying on a smooth geometric thermal background. The resulting entropy reproduces the characteristic warped black-hole and WCFT structure in the continuum limit. We further demonstrate that the continuum warped holonomy conditions are recovered from the large-lattice limit of the ordered boundary products, establishing a direct correspondence between discrete monodromies and continuous Wilson loops. Our analysis suggests that warped gravitational thermodynamics may be understood from a fundamentally holonomy-based perspective in which boundary monodromy sectors provide an alternative organizational description of the physical states within the discrete warped framework.
  Keywords: warped Chern-Simons theory, boundary monodromies, holonomy sectors, Wilson loops, warped thermodynamics.

</details>


### [65] [Hamiltonian formulation of Carrollian Maxwell theory in Deformed Light-cone Kaluza-Klein-like Null reduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22050)
*Limin Zeng*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs magnetic and electric Carrollian Maxwell theories via Kaluza-Klein-like null reduction of a complex Maxwell field in a Bargmann-deformed light-cone background, preserving U(1) gauge symmetry and revealing novel scalar-field couplings absent in prior approaches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically construct gauge-invariant Carrollian Maxwell theories—specifically magnetic and electric variants—while uncovering previously unobserved scalar-field degrees of freedom arising from Bargmann-deformed null reduction.

Method: Kaluza-Klein-like null reduction of a complex Maxwell field on a Bargmann-deformed light-cone background; careful scaling analysis to isolate magnetic/electric Carrollian limits; Hamiltonian formulation ensuring preservation of first-class U(1) Gauss constraint.

Result: Explicit gauge-invariant magnetic and electric Carrollian Maxwell theories; emergence of a scalar field either coupled or decoupled—novel compared to earlier constructions; demonstration that deformed light-cone null reduction yields broader class of Carrollian gauge theories.

Conclusion: The deformed light-cone Kaluza-Klein-like null reduction provides a robust, symmetry-preserving framework for generating diverse Carrollian gauge theories—including previously inaccessible configurations with scalar fields—thus extending the method’s applicability beyond prior limits.

Abstract: We construct magnetic and electric Carrollian Maxwell theories by performing Kaluza-Klein-like null reduction of a complex Maxwell field in a Bargmann deformed light-cone background with manifest gauge symmetry. The procedure preserves a first-class U(1) Gauss constraint throughout the Carrollian limit. Gauge invariance is therefore maintained in our Hamiltonian formulation. By choosing different scalings, we obtain standard magnetic Carrollian theory and electric Carrollian theory. However, a scalar field could appear in the Carrollian theory in a coupled or decoupled way, which has not been found by previous methods. This result fully reveals the diversity of Carrollian theories accessible through the deformed light-cone Kaluza-Klein-like null reduction method. Furthermore, our work provides an explicit example of the correct application of this approach, thereby broadening the scope of its applicability to gauge theories.

</details>


### [66] [Thermodynamic and Topological Phase Transitions of AdS Black Holes with Nonminimal $F^{αβ}F^{γλ}R_{αγ}R_{βλ}$ Coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22204)
*Mehdi Sadeghi,Faramarz Rahmani*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies how a non-minimal coupling term deforms the topology and phase structure of a 4D asymptotically AdS black hole, shifting it from W=+1 to W=0 topological class while preserving van der Waals-type phase transitions within the Hawking–Page framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how higher-curvature electromagnetic couplings affect both thermodynamic phase structure and topological classification of black holes in AdS space.

Method: Perturbative analysis to first order in coupling ε for a 4D asymptotically AdS black hole with non-minimal coupling F^{αβ}F^{γλ}R_{αγ}R_{βλ}; derivation of thermodynamic quantities; verification of first law and Smarr relation; topological classification via W-invariant.

Result: The non-minimal coupling induces a topological phase transition from W^{1+} (Reissner–Nordström–AdS limit) to W^{0−}, while retaining a van der Waals-type first-order phase transition coexisting with Hawking–Page behavior — forming a hybrid thermodynamic system.

Conclusion: The coupling ε serves as a topological deformation parameter that fundamentally alters the universal topological class, demonstrating that even perturbative corrections can induce qualitative changes in black hole classification and phase structure.

Abstract: The conventional and topological phase transitions of a four-dimensional asymptotically AdS black hole with a non-minimal coupling term $F^{αβ}F^{γλ}R_{αγ}R_{βλ}$ are investigated. Using a perturbative approach to first order in the coupling $ε$, the thermodynamic quantities are derived and the first law and Smarr relation are verified. Intriguingly, while the $ε= 0$ limit yields the standard Reissner--Nordström--AdS black hole belonging to the $W^{1+}$ topological class ($W = +1$), switching on the non-minimal coupling fundamentally transforms the topology to the $W^{0-}$ class ($W = 0$). This transition occurs while the van der Waals-type first-order phase transition survives in the intermediate region, embedded within the overall Hawking--Page pattern, rendering the system a hybrid black hole thermodynamic system. The coupling $ε$ thus acts as a topological deformation parameter that alters the universal classification of the system, despite the perturbative nature of the solution.

</details>


### [67] [Hilbert Functions and Line Bundle Cohomology on CICY Threefolds](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22275)
*Juntao Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper explains the empirical chamber-wise polynomial structure of line bundle cohomology dimensions on CICY threefolds using Hilbert functions of Koszul spectral sequence maps, enabling analytic derivations and finite-box certifications of cohomology formulae.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a theoretical explanation for empirically observed piecewise polynomial behavior of line bundle cohomology dimensions on complete intersection Calabi–Yau threefolds (CICYs), which are crucial for string phenomenology.

Method: Leverages the Koszul spectral sequence and Bott–Borel–Weil theory to identify ambient cohomology groups; analyzes rank defects of Koszul differentials via Hilbert functions of associated kernel/cokernel modules; combines analytic derivation with finite-box certification for infinite line bundle families.

Result: Analytically recovers nearly all known piecewise cohomology formulae; proves finite-box certificate theorems for remaining cases; discovers refined wall structures on some CICYs; constructs cohomology formula libraries for two previously unanalyzed CICY threefolds.

Conclusion: Hilbert-function methods offer a rigorous, systematic, and computationally efficient framework for deriving and certifying line bundle cohomology on CICYs, enhancing reliability and speed for string model-building scans.

Abstract: Line bundle cohomology on complete intersection Calabi--Yau threefolds is an important input in string phenomenology. Previous work, based on direct extrapolation and on machine-learning analyses of finite data sets, has shown that these cohomology dimensions often admit chamber-wise polynomial descriptions on the Picard lattice. In this paper we explain this structure through the Hilbert functions associated with the non-trivial maps in the Koszul spectral sequence. Once Bott--Borel--Weil theory determines the non-vanishing ambient cohomology groups, the rank defects of the relevant Koszul maps are governed by Hilbert functions of cokernel or kernel modules. This turns many empirical chamber formulae into explicit rank-defect statements, with proofs that are either analytic or certified on finite boxes. Using this approach, we recover analytically almost all of the piecewise formulae appearing in the existing literature. For the remaining cases, we prove finite-box certificate theorems for infinite families of line bundles in the specified regions. We also identify new wall structures on certain CICYs which refine the chamber decompositions suggested by finite-range data. Finally, we use the same framework to construct line bundle cohomology formula libraries for two CICY threefolds which had not previously been analysed in this way. These results suggest that Hilbert-function methods can provide promising faster inputs for future string model-building scans based on line bundle constructions.

</details>


### [68] [Eight loop form factors, amplitudes and patterns in planar $\mathcal{N}=4$ super-Yang-Mills theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22380)
*Lance J. Dixon,Zhenjie Li*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the three-point form factor for the operator trφ³ in planar N=4 SYM to 8 loops, leveraging shared polylogarithmic structure with the known trφ² form factor and identifying all-order patterns in symbol coefficients for its leading discontinuity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend insights from the well-studied trφ² form factor to the more complex trφ³ operator, probing universal structures in scattering amplitudes and form factors in planar N=4 SYM.

Method: Uses antipodal duality and symbolic computation within the space of iterated integrals (polylogarithms); analyzes symbol entries and leading discontinuities to extract all-order coefficient patterns.

Result: Eight-loop result for the trφ³ three-point form factor; identification of recurring patterns in symbol coefficients for its leading discontinuity; confirmation that it resides in the same function space as the trφ² form factor.

Conclusion: The trφ³ form factor shares structural constraints with trφ², suggesting deeper integrability- or duality-driven regularity in planar N=4 SYM form factors, enabling predictive all-order insights.

Abstract: The simplest nontrivial amplitude in planar $\mathcal{N}=4$ super-Yang-Mills theory is six-gluon scattering in the maximally-helicity-violating configuration. It has been computed to 8 loops with the help of antipodal duality, which relates it to the three-point form factor of a protected operator, the chiral stress tensor super-multiplet, represented also as ${\rm tr} φ^2$. In this talk, we describe the computation to 8 loops of another three-point form factor, for the operator ${\rm tr}φ^3$. This form factor lives in the same restricted space of polylogarithms as the ${\rm tr}φ^2$ form factor. We also report on all-order patterns for sequences of coefficients in the symbols of these polylogarithmic results, for the leading discontinuity of the ${\rm tr}φ^3$ form factor.

</details>


### [69] [Quiver Approach to Symmetry Theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.30354)
*Vivek Chakrabhavi,Mirjam Cvetič,Jonathan J. Heckman,Shani Meynet*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces an algebraic method using brane probe path algebras to extract global symmetry anomaly data for 5D SCFTs from M-theory on Calabi-Yau cones, complementing geometric approaches based on intersection numbers or η-invariants.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a computationally tractable, algebraic alternative to geometric methods (e.g., triple intersection numbers, η-invariants) for extracting global symmetry anomalies in 5D SCFTs engineered from M-theory on Calabi-Yau cones—especially where geometric computations are unknown or combinatorially intractable.

Method: Extracts anomaly data from the path algebra of D-branes probing the Calabi-Yau cone X, leveraging representation theory and quiver algebra associated with toric geometry; applies to both orbifold (C³/Γ) and non-orbifold Sasaki–Einstein five-manifold boundaries.

Result: Demonstrates equivalence of anomaly data obtained via path algebra with known geometric results in toric examples; successfully computes anomalies in cases where geometric methods fail or become unwieldy.

Conclusion: The path algebra approach provides a robust, generalizable, and computationally efficient framework for characterizing global symmetry anomalies in 5D SCFTs from M-theory, unifying algebraic and geometric perspectives.

Abstract: Global symmetry anomalies of a quantum field theory (QFT) can be packaged as specific couplings of a higher-dimensional symmetry theory (SymTh). In this work we show that for 5D superconformal field theories (SCFTs) engineered from M-theory backgrounds $X$ a Calabi-Yau cone, this data can be extracted from the path algebra of branes probing $X$. This provides a complementary algebraic approach compared with more geometric computations based on the explicit calculation of triple intersection numbers in a resolved geometry and / or $η$-invariants extracted from the boundary geometry $\partial X$. Our method applies in situations where the counterpart geometric computation is either unknown or combinatorially unwieldy. We illustrate with several toric threefold examples, including orbifolds $\mathbb{C}^{3} / Γ$ and more general non-orbifold Calabi-Yau cones of Sasaki-Einstein five-manifolds.

</details>


### [70] [Holographic s+p superconductors with nonlinear electrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22459)
*Ru-Qing Chen,Hui Zeng,Zhang-Yu Nie*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies a holographic s+p superconductor with nonlinear electrodynamics in the probe limit; it finds that increasing the nonlinearity parameter b suppresses both s- and p-wave condensates (s-wave more strongly), shrinks the pure s-wave phase region, lowers the charge-ratio threshold for s+p coexistence, and induces spontaneous horizon-external charge accumulation—requiring a revised definition of superconducting charge density and altering optical conductivity (e.g., introducing a frequency-dependent minimum in Im[σ]) that complicates gap identification.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how nonlinear electrodynamics affects multi-component (s+p) holographic superconductors, especially regarding phase structure, condensate competition, charge distribution, and optical response—addressing limitations of standard linear Maxwell treatments.

Method: Numerical solution of equations of motion in the probe limit for a holographic black hole background coupled to scalar (s-wave) and vector (p-wave) fields and nonlinear electrodynamics (parameterized by b); construction of condensate and grand potential curves, b–T phase diagrams, redefinition of superconducting charge density via background subtraction, and computation of optical conductivity.

Result: 1) Increasing b suppresses both s- and p-wave condensates, with stronger suppression on s-wave; 2) Pure s-wave phase region shrinks in b–T diagram; 3) Lower q_p/q_s ratio needed for s+p coexistence as b increases; 4) Spontaneous charge accumulation outside the horizon arises from nonlinear EM self-interaction—even in normal phase—necessitating a background-subtracted definition of superconducting charge density; 5) Finite-b modifies normal-phase optical conductivity: Im[σ] develops a finite-frequency minimum, persisting near criticality and obscuring the superconducting gap frequency.

Conclusion: Nonlinear electrodynamics qualitatively reshapes the phase diagram, condensate competition, and electromagnetic response of s+p holographic superconductors; the emergent horizon-external charge invalidates conventional charge-density definitions and introduces ambiguities in spectroscopic gap extraction, highlighting the need for careful bulk-field reinterpretation in nonlinear holographic models.

Abstract: We investigate a holographic s+p superconductor model coupled to nonlinear electrodynamics in the probe limit. The equations of motion are solved numerically, and the condensates as well as the grand potential curves for phase transitions at different values of the nonlinear parameter $b$ are illustrated. It is found that as $b$ increases, both the pure s-wave and p-wave condensates are suppressed. From the $b-T$ phase diagram, we observe that the region of the pure s-wave phase gradually shrinks with increasing $b$, which is attributed to the stronger suppression on the s-wave condensate compared to the one on the p-wave. Moreover, a smaller charge ratio $q_p/q_s$ is needed for the s+p coexistent phase to appear as $b$ grows. A particularly interesting feature is that, due to the nonlinear self-interaction of the electromagnetic field, charge accumulates spontaneously outside the event horizon from the bulk perspective even in the absence of scalar and vector condensates, thereby invalidating the conventional formula for the superconducting charge density. We improve the definition of the superconducting charge density as the background-subtracted value of the accumulated charge outside the horizon with respect to that in the normal phase. Furthermore, the optical conductivity in the normal phase is also modified by this accumulated charge for finite $b$, and its imaginary part develops a minimum at a finite frequency. This minimum persists in the superconducting phase near the critical point, confusing the extraction of the gap frequency.

</details>


### [71] [The conformal null string in $d+2$ and $d$ dimensions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22498)
*Ulf Lindström*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper confirms that the tensionless string with gauged scale symmetry is a Dirac reduction of the conformal string to Minkowski space, mapping its constraint algebra—Virasoro ⋉ su(1,1) Kac-Moody—to the Carrollian-Weyl symmetry in d dimensions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To corroborate the claim that the recently studied tensionless string with gauged scale symmetry arises as a reduction of the conformal string to Minkowski space.

Method: Dirac reduction of the conformal string defined in d+2-dimensional conformal (Dirac) embedding space, including explicit slicing and constraint algebra mapping.

Result: The d+2 dimensional conformal string's constraint algebra—Virasoro semidirect product with su(1,1) Kac-Moody—reduces precisely to the Carrollian-Weyl symmetry algebra in d-dimensional Minkowski space.

Conclusion: The tensionless string with gauged scale symmetry is rigorously established as the Dirac reduction of the conformal string, providing a geometric and algebraic foundation for its Carrollian symmetry structure.

Abstract: In \cite{Lindstrom:2026quz} it is pointed out how the tensionless string with a gauged scale symmetry discussed in the recent articles, \cite{Sheikh-Jabbari:2026cnj,Sheikh-Jabbari:2026vqh,Sheikh-Jabbari:2026tpf}, is a reduction of the conformal string \cite{Gustafsson:1994kr} to Minkowski space. Here we corroborate this by choices of slices in Dirac $d+2$ dimensional conformal space. We perform a Dirac reduction of the model and its algebra of constraints and see how they map to the constraints in $d$ dimensions, including how the semidirect product of the Virasoro algebra with a su(1,1) Kac-Moody algebra becomes the Corrollian-Weyl symmetry in $d$ dimensions.

</details>


### [72] [Nonlinear Geometrizability of State-Dependent Proto-Area in Approximate Holographic Codes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22598)
*Ruiliang Li*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper establishes exact finite-resolution criteria and necessary/sufficient conditions for when state-dependent proto-area data—arising from approximate quantum error correction in AdS/CFT—can be realized by a local bulk metric, particularly on time-reflection-symmetric asymptotically AdS₃ slices; it introduces geometric obstructions (e.g., a gauge-invariant quadratic obstruction from a metric-forced Jacobi equation), characterizes the geometric tangent space via the geodesic X-ray transform, and demonstrates explicit constructions and witnesses of nongeometric data using Hamiltonian-skewed codes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine when state-dependent geometric data reconstructed from boundary CFT information (via approximate recovery maps) genuinely correspond to a consistent local bulk metric—addressing foundational questions about emergence and geometry in holography.

Method: Uses fixed recovery maps calibrated on the code channel across logical-state families; derives exact finite-resolution criteria and necessary/sufficient two-jet compatibility conditions; analyzes polyhedral realization in finite networks; identifies geometric tangent space as range of rank-two geodesic X-ray transform; employs metric-forced Jacobi equation to compute normal Hessian of renormalized boundary length; applies split-regularity hypothesis to establish local graph structure; constructs Hamiltonian-skewed codes to realize controlled nongeometric responses.

Result: Provides unconditional two-jet geometricity criterion for regular proto-area data; identifies gauge-invariant quadratic obstruction to geometry; shows geometric tangent space equals range of geodesic X-ray transform; achieves stable reconstruction modulo boundary-fixing diffeomorphisms; furnishes primal/dual certificates and explicit nongeometric witnesses; demonstrates first-order nongeometry and higher-order obstruction scenarios via Hamiltonian-skewed codes.

Conclusion: Proto-area data need not reflect a single local bulk metric; geometric consistency is testable via exact jet-level criteria and obstructions; nongeometry is generic but structured, with controllable order of onset; the framework unifies discrete (network) and continuum (AdS₃ slice) perspectives while clarifying the role of quantum error correction in emergent geometry.

Abstract: State-dependent proto-area data produced by approximate recovery need not be compatible with a single local bulk metric. Using recovery maps calibrated on the code channel and held fixed along a logical-state family, we derive exact finite-resolution criteria and, near the hyperbolic disk, necessary and sufficient conditions for a regular proto-area two-jet to arise from a metric two-jet on a time-reflection-symmetric asymptotically AdS$_3$ slice. Finite networks give a polyhedral realization problem with primal and dual certificates, stable reconstruction, and explicit witnesses of nongeometry. In the continuum, the geometric tangent space is the range of the rank-two geodesic X-ray transform. A metric-forced Jacobi equation determines the normal Hessian of the renormalized boundary-length image and yields a gauge-invariant quadratic obstruction. Under a split-regularity hypothesis, nearby geometric data form a local graph; the two-jet criterion itself is unconditional for regular data. Hamiltonian-skewed codes realize both first-order nongeometry and a response whose first obstruction appears only at quadratic order. The compatible metric perturbation is reconstructed modulo boundary-fixing diffeomorphisms.

</details>


### [73] [Exact solution of the Seven-Vertex Model on a dynamical lattice](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22651)
*Mateus da Silva Junca,Ivan Kostov,Andre Alves Lima*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper fully solves the gravitational seven-vertex model—a one-parameter deformation of the six-vertex model on dynamical lattices—by mapping it to a loop gas on dynamical triangulations with curvature-dependent loop weights; it reformulates the model as a large-N matrix model (7vMM), solves it exactly using Jacobi theta functions, derives its non-algebraic spectral curve parametrically, and obtains the full phase diagram in cosmological constant–temperature space, including critical phases and the scaling limit near the dense–massive tricritical point.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To solve the gravitational seven-vertex model exactly and characterize its non-topological loop weight structure and critical behavior on dynamical lattices, extending prior partial results.

Method: Exact solution via reformulation as a large-N matrix model (7vMM); derivation of the full solution using Jacobi theta functions; parametric construction of the non-algebraic spectral curve; analysis of the phase diagram in the coupling space (cosmological constant vs. temperature); asymptotic extraction of the scaling solution near the tricritical point.

Result: Complete analytical solution expressed in Jacobi theta functions; explicit parametric form of the non-algebraic spectral curve of 7vMM; full phase diagram featuring massive, dilute, and dense critical phases; identification of critical boundaries and the tricritical point separating dense and massive phases; recovery of the earlier scaling solution as an asymptotic limit.

Conclusion: The gravitational seven-vertex model is exactly solvable beyond the scaling limit, revealing curvature-dependent, non-topological loop weights and rich critical structure distinct from the gravitational O(n) model; the exact solution confirms and generalizes prior scaling results and provides a new integrable framework for quantum gravity coupled to loop matter.

Abstract: We give the complete solution of the one-parameter deformation of the six-vertex model on dynamical lattice introduced in [1] and dubbed gravitational seven-vertex model. The statistical model in question is mapped to a gas of self- and mutually avoiding loops on dynamical triangulations, with a temperature coupling controlling the volume not occupied by loops. The phase diagram is characterised by massive, dilute and dense critical phases, similarly to the gravitational O(n) loop model. There is however an important difference -- in our model the weights of the loops are not topological but depend on the form of the loop and on the curvature defects of the lattice via lattice spin connection. The seven-vertex model on dynamical lattice is nevertheless exactly solvable after being reformulated as a large-N matrix model, which we will refer to as 7vMM, and the solution in the scaling limit was found in [1]. Here we derive the full solution in terms of Jacobi theta functions and present the (non-algebraic) spectral curve of 7vMM in a parametric form. We obtain the phase diagram in the space of the two coupling constants -- the cosmological constant and the temperature -- and identify the critical phases along the boundary of the physical domain. We derive the scaling solution of [1] as the asymptotic of the full solution in the vicinity of the tricritical point separating the phases of dense and massive loops.

</details>


### [74] [On the temperature dependence of quasinormal modes in SYK and holography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22679)
*Matthew Dodelson,Om Gupta,Márk Mezei,Diandian Wang*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper generalizes the quasinormal mode (QNM) analysis of the SYK model from infinite to finite temperature, bridging the infinite-temperature 'Christmas tree' spectrum to the low-temperature JT gravity dual; it shows monotonic increase of relaxation rate with temperature only in the strong-coupling (gravitational) regime, and yields new insights into operator growth.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how spectral features—specifically quasinormal modes (Ruelle–Pollicott resonances)—of the SYK model evolve with temperature, and to establish continuity between its infinite-temperature chaotic regime and the low-temperature semi-classical gravitational description (JT gravity), while comparing QNM dynamics across physical systems.

Method: Analytic and numerical computation of quasinormal modes (Ruelle–Pollicott resonances) of the SYK model at finite temperature; comparison with QNMs of AdS black holes, dynamical phase transitions, and the large-p SYK chain; analysis of relaxation rates and operator growth via spectral data.

Result: The finite-temperature QNM spectrum smoothly interpolates between the infinite-temperature Christmas tree structure and the low-temperature JT gravity spectrum; the relaxation rate increases monotonically with temperature only in the strong-coupling (gravitational) regime; new results on operator growth emerge as byproducts.

Conclusion: The QNM structure of SYK provides a spectral signature of the quantum-to-gravitational crossover; monotonicity of relaxation reflects strong-coupling dominance, supporting the emergence of semiclassical gravity only in that regime.

Abstract: It was recently found that the quasinormal modes (or Ruelle--Pollicott resonances) of the SYK model at infinite temperature form a Christmas tree shape, reminiscent of AdS black holes. We generalise this computation to finite temperature, allowing us to continuously connect the infinite temperature results to the low temperature regime dual to JT gravity. We contrast the movement of the quasinormal modes with a few examples: various AdS black holes, dynamical phase transitions, and the large $p$ SYK chain. We find that the relaxation rate increases monotonically with temperature only at strong coupling, corresponding to the gravitational regime. Byproducts of our investigations are new results on operator growth that may be of independent interest.

</details>


### [75] [Unified theory of oscillons and modes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22680)
*F. Blaschke,T. Romanczukiewicz,K. Slawinska,A. Wereszczynski*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper reinterprets oscillons as localized discrete resonant (non-normalizable) modes—specifically threshold or antibound modes—enabled by nonlinearity, and introduces 'wobblerons' as oscillon–kink bound states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a unified conceptual framework for oscillons by interpreting them as nonlinearly localized discrete resonant (non-normalizable) modes, and to extend this idea to kink backgrounds, predicting new excitations.

Method: Analytical and conceptual field-theoretic analysis of resonance structures in scalar field theories; identification of threshold and antibound modes as precursors to localized nonlinear excitations; construction of oscillon–kink composites (wobblerons).

Result: Oscillons are identified as nonlinear localizations of threshold (zero-energy) or antibound (positive-energy, non-normalizable) modes; wobblerons—stable oscillon–kink bound states—are predicted as a new class of nonlinear excitations.

Conclusion: Oscillons are not isolated phenomena but manifestations of underlying resonant spectral structures; their existence and properties are governed by the interplay between linear mode structure (threshold/antibound) and nonlinearity, enabling generalization to topological backgrounds like kinks.

Abstract: We show that an oscillon can be understood as a localized discrete resonant (non-normalizable) mode. Specifically, oscillon in the vacuum arises from the threshold mode, which because of nonlinearity gets localized. Following this idea, we find {\it wobblerons} - nonlinear excitations of kinks, that is, oscillons-kink bound state. Now, the oscillon can also originate in an antibound mode, i.e., a discrete, positive energy but non-normalizable mode.

</details>


### [76] [UV-finite Effective Field Theory from Quantized Irreversible Null-geometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22788)
*Si-xue Qin*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper constructs a UV-finite 4D effective field theory by embedding dynamics in a double-exponential measure from quantized irreversible null-geometry, yielding a natural proper-time cutoff that eliminates UV divergences; overlapping sub-divergences are shown to be pseudo-singularities from continuum approximation, corrected algebraically via BPHZ; QED and QCD yield finite bare parameters while preserving key symmetries (Ward-Takahashi, Slavnov-Taylor); the framework enables closed non-perturbative Schwinger-Dyson equations for chiral symmetry breaking.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve persistent UV and overlapping sub-divergences in standard perturbative QFT by constructing an intrinsically finite EFT without ad hoc regularization or dimensional continuation.

Method: Embedding the dynamical operator in a double-exponential capacity measure derived from quantized irreversible null-geometry; analytic expansion of the exact operator resolvent to derive a universal proper-time bound; reinterpretation of overlapping subdivergences as distributional pseudo-singularities arising from continuum oversmoothing; strict algebraic use of BPHZ as a mapping operator (not a subtraction scheme); derivation of closed non-perturbative Schwinger-Dyson equations.

Result: A manifestly UV-finite 4D EFT with absolute UV boundary defined topologically; finite bare electron mass (0.427 MeV) and strong coupling (α_{s,0} ≈ 0.0191); exact preservation of Ward-Takahashi and Slavnov-Taylor identities; anomaly-free, symmetry-preserving renormalization; analytically closed Schwinger-Dyson equations enabling treatment of chiral symmetry breaking and mass generation.

Conclusion: Ultraviolet finiteness and symmetry preservation in EFTs can be achieved natively in four dimensions through geometrically constrained operator embedding and algebraic renormalization—eliminating reliance on regularization schemes and providing a mathematically consistent foundation for non-perturbative dynamics.

Abstract: Standard perturbative quantum field theory is persistently challenged by ultraviolet (UV) divergences and overlapping sub-divergences. In this work, we construct an absolutely UV-finite effective field theory (EFT) natively in four-dimensional spacetime. We embed the complete dynamical operator within a double-exponential capacity measure derived from quantized irreversible null-geometry. By analytically expanding the exact operator resolvent, we demonstrate that a universal macroscopic proper-time bound ($T \ge τ_0$) natively emerges, structurally collapsing the high-order UV phase space via topological contraction. This mathematically defines the absolute UV boundary of the EFT. Consequently, overlapping subgraph divergences are rigorously identified as distributional pseudo-singularities caused by the continuous approximation analytically oversmoothing the discrete lattice bounds. These artifacts are systematically corrected by employing the Bogoliubov-Parasiuk-Hepp-Zimmermann (BPHZ) protocol strictly as an algebraic mapping operator. Evaluating Quantum Electrodynamics in 4D, we obtain a finite electron bare mass ($m_0 \approx 0.427$ MeV) and derive finite renormalization constants, analytically preserving the exact Ward-Takahashi identity ($Z_1=Z_2$). In Quantum Chromodynamics, the exact preservation of Slavnov-Taylor identities yields a calculable finite bare strong coupling ($α_{s,0} \approx 0.0191$). Generating analytically closed non-perturbative Schwinger-Dyson Equations, this framework provides a consistent, anomaly-free mathematical foundation for finite effective field theories to address dynamic chiral symmetry breaking and mass generation.

</details>


### [77] [A Linearized Obstruction to the Supersymmetric Extension of Conformal Boundary Conditions in Euclidean Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22810)
*Xingyang Yu*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper shows that Witten's conformal boundary condition for Euclidean gravity cannot be extended to preserve half-supersymmetry in linearized minimal supergravity, due to a pointwise, highest-derivative obstruction in the gravitino boundary conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether Witten's conformal boundary condition—which fixes boundary conformal class and mean curvature—can be supersymmetrized while preserving half of the supersymmetry in minimal supergravity.

Method: Analysis of linearized minimal supergravity with boundary conditions: examination of possible local/pseudodifferential (including APS-type) gravitino boundary conditions compatible with chiral supersymmetry, BRST symmetry, and LS-ellipticity; systematic study of how supersymmetry acts on boundary data and responses.

Result: No half-dimensional gravitino boundary condition preserves chiral supersymmetry when bosonic conformal data is fixed; supersymmetry maps the natural chiral gravitino datum to the trace-free extrinsic curvature—the very quantity left free by Witten’s prescription—creating an irreconcilable conflict at the highest-derivative level.

Conclusion: A linearized, pointwise, highest-derivative obstruction prevents any supersymmetric extension of Witten’s conformal boundary condition in minimal supergravity; nonlinear supercovariant boundary terms involving gravitino bilinears may circumvent it.

Abstract: Witten's conformal boundary condition \cite{Witten:2018lgb} provides an elliptic boundary-value problem for the finite-boundary perturbative Euclidean gravitational path integral: one fixes the boundary conformal class and the mean curvature, while the trace-free extrinsic curvature is left free as the conjugate response. We show that this perturbative construction admits no half-supersymmetric extension in linearized minimal supergravity. For fixed conformal bosonic data, no half-dimensional gravitino boundary condition (local or pseudodifferential, APS-type included, with any compatible ghost condition at highest-derivative order) closes the full preserved chiral supersymmetry. Supersymmetry first selects the natural local chiral gravitino datum. Acting back on this datum then produces the trace-free extrinsic curvature, precisely the response that the conformal prescription leaves unfixed. The obstruction is therefore not the failure of a particular elliptic ansatz: even the chiral/Robin completion that is LS-elliptic and BRST-compatible at highest-derivative order would impose Dirichlet control on a Neumann response. The obstruction is pointwise in tangential momentum and survives compensating gauge transformations. It is a linearized, highest-derivative obstruction, not a global or nonlinear no-go; nonlinear supercovariant boundary terms may evade it by tying the trace-free extrinsic curvature to gravitino bilinears.

</details>


### [78] [Extended Thermodynamics and Renyi Entropy Beyond Fixed Central Charge](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22849)
*Chatchai Promsiri,Phuwadon Chunaksorn,Ratchaphat Nakarachinda,Ekapong Hirunsirisawat*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper introduces a novel central-charge Rényi entropy for thermal CFTs on a hyperbolic cylinder in a grand canonical ensemble with fixed charge, volume, and central charge potential. It satisfies all Rényi entropy inequalities, extends entanglement quantification to ensembles of fluctuating holographic CFTs, and identifies a critical Rényi index n_* separating two statistical regimes—dominant-theory (n > n_*) vs. multi-theory (n < n_*)—linked to the mass gap and residual entropy in near-extremal conformal thermodynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the physical interpretation of central charge variations in conformal thermodynamics and extend Rényi entropy to account for statistical fluctuations across an ensemble of holographic CFTs with dynamical central charge.

Method: Constructs a central-charge Rényi entropy using the Casini-Huerta-Myers (CHM) map applied to thermal CFTs on a hyperbolic cylinder within a (\tilde{Q}, \mathcal{V}, \mu_C) grand canonical ensemble; verifies consistency via the four fundamental Rényi entropy inequalities; analyzes near-extremal configurations to identify residual entropy origin and derive the characteristic Rényi index n_*.

Result: The constructed entropy is a mathematically consistent Rényi measure; residual entropy originates from the central charge sector, not thermal excitations; a mass gap induces a characteristic temperature \tilde{T}_*, mapped to a critical Rényi index n_* that demarcates two statistical regimes in theory space.

Conclusion: Central charge fluctuations constitute an independent thermodynamic degree of freedom; the central-charge Rényi entropy provides a physically meaningful, ensemble-aware generalization of entanglement entropy in holographic CFTs; the emergence of n_* signals a phase-like transition in statistical dominance across the CFT landscape.

Abstract: An outstanding problem in the framework of conformal thermodynamics concerns the interpretation of variations in the central charge $C$. In this paper, we construct a novel central-charge Rényi entropy via the Casini-Huerta-Myers (CHM) map by considering thermal CFTs on a hyperbolic cylinder within a fixed charge, field theory volume and central charge potential $(\tilde{Q},\mathcal{V},μ_C)$ grand canonical ensemble. We demonstrate that the resulting entropy satisfies all four fundamental Rényi entropy inequalities throughout the admissible range of $μ_C$, establishing its consistency as a genuine Rényi measure. Physically, this novel measure extends conventional Rényi entropy by capturing the degree of entanglement across a statistical ensemble of holographic CFTs with fluctuating degrees of freedom. Furthermore, our conformal thermodynamic analysis of near-extremal configurations reveals that residual entropy arises from the central charge sector rather than thermal excitations. The mass gap that separates the extremal state and the first thermal excitation introduces a characteristic temperature scale $\tilde{T}_*$, which translates via the CHM map into a distinguished characteristic Rényi index $n_*$. Crucially, we propose that $n_*$ separates the theory space into two qualitatively distinct statistical regimes: a dominant-theory regime ($n > n_*$) governed by the most probable CFT realizations, and a multi-theory regime ($n < n_*$) where a broader spectrum of fluctuating theories and higher-energy modular excitations becomes increasingly relevant.

</details>


### [79] [On background fields and a cutoff in sigma models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23088)
*N. V. Kharuk*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper analyzes chiral field decomposition in a 2D nonlinear sigma model with Heisenberg group symmetry, identifies the background-field-consistent decomposition method, performs one-loop renormalization, computes two-loop power-law singularities, examines extension to classical action, and assesses regulator compatibility with functional identities in the background field formalism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine which chiral field decomposition variant is compatible with the background field method for generating functional construction, and to examine consistency of regularization with functional relations in quantum field theory.

Method: Analytical field-theoretic computation: comparison of two chiral field decomposition schemes; background field method applied to generating functional; dimensional regularization or similar for one-loop renormalization; perturbative expansion for two-loop power-law singularity analysis; functional identity verification under cutoff schemes.

Result: Only one decomposition preserves background field method consistency; explicit one-loop renormalized quantum action obtained; power-law divergences identified at two loops; extended classical action formulation derived; cutoff compatibility with Slavnov–Taylor or Ward-type identities confirmed within background field formalism.

Conclusion: The background field method imposes stringent constraints on chiral field decomposition; consistency requires adherence to specific geometric/field-algebraic structure; regulator choices must respect functional identities, limiting admissible cutoff schemes in nonlinear sigma models.

Abstract: In this paper, using the example of a two-dimensional nonlinear sigma model with the Heisenberg group, we compare two variants of chiral field decomposition into a background part and a fluctuation. It is shown that only one of these methods is consistent with the construction of the generating functional by introducing a background field. Furthermore, we perform a one-loop renormalization of the quantum action, calculate power-law singularities in the two-loop approximation, and consider transition to an extended classical action. Finally, we study the consistency of the cutoff with special functional relations within the framework of the background field method.

</details>


### [80] [Bounds on nonlinear electrodynamics via resummed relative entropy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23323)
*Pietro Conzinu,Daiki Ueda*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper uses relative entropy in electromagnetic backgrounds to systematically probe nonlinear electrodynamic EFTs, deriving sign constraints on higher-dimensional operators and distinguishing perturbative breakdown from physical instabilities (e.g., Schwinger effect) via non-negativity violations; it unifies consistency and stability diagnostics across fermionic/scalar QED and Dirac-Born-Infeld theory using perturbative, resummed (Borel–Laplace), and non-perturbative (Schwinger proper-time) methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To develop a systematic, unified diagnostic for both perturbative consistency and non-perturbative stability in nonlinear electrodynamic EFTs by leveraging relative entropy as a probe sensitive to the full tower of higher-dimensional operators.

Method: Computes relative entropy in background electromagnetic fields for fermionic QED, scalar QED, and Dirac-Born-Infeld theory using perturbative expansions, Borel–Laplace resummation, and the Schwinger proper-time method; analyzes non-negativity conditions to constrain EFT coefficients and interpret violations.

Result: Non-negativity of perturbative relative entropy yields generalized positivity bounds on truncated EFT operators; violations in perturbative vs. resummed/non-perturbative regimes distinguish EFT breakdown from physical instabilities (e.g., Schwinger pair production); resummed relative entropy imposes general constraints for UV completions with factorial or power-law coefficient growth.

Conclusion: Relative entropy serves as a unified diagnostic tool: its non-negativity enforces consistency bounds in weak coupling and signals physical instabilities in strong coupling, bridging perturbative EFT validity and non-perturbative quantum vacuum stability.

Abstract: We investigate nonlinear electrodynamic effective field theories (EFTs) through the relative entropy evaluated in suitable background electromagnetic fields. In this setup, the relative entropy encodes information about the infinite tower of higher-dimensional operators and provides a systematic probe of nonlinear EFT effects. We study these features in fermionic QED, scalar QED, and Dirac-Born-Infeld theory using perturbative analyses, resummation techniques such as Borel--Laplace resummation, and non-perturbative approaches including the Schwinger proper-time method. In the weak-coupling regime, we show that the non-negativity of the perturbative relative entropy imposes sign constraints on finite truncations of higher-dimensional operators, generalizing familiar positivity bounds on leading EFT coefficients. We further show that violations of non-negativity in the strong-coupling regime admit qualitatively different interpretations depending on the framework: perturbatively analyzed violations diagnose the breakdown of the truncated EFT expansion, whereas violations in resummed or genuinely non-perturbative relative entropy signal physical instabilities of the system, such as the Schwinger effect. Extending the analysis to broader classes of UV completions, including theories with factorial or power-law growth of EFT coefficients, we derive general constraints on nonlinear electrodynamic EFT effects from the non-negativity of the resummed relative entropy. Our results suggest that relative entropy provides a unified diagnostic of perturbative consistency and non-perturbative stability in nonlinear EFTs.

</details>


### [81] [NLO Angular Impulse and Leading Singularities to all orders in spin for Kerr Black Holes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23450)
*Lucas Haiashi,Gabriel Menezes*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes the next-to-leading order (NLO) spin-dependent angular impulse in Kerr black hole scattering using the KMOC formalism and on-shell amplitudes, yielding compact all-order-in-spin expressions, verifying consistency with spin constraints and radial-action results, and deriving a spin-resummed non-relativistic gravitational potential from triangle leading singularities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To advance amplitude-based methods for classical gravitational dynamics by computing spin-dependent observables—specifically the NLO angular impulse (spin kick)—in binary Kerr black hole scattering, bridging quantum amplitudes and classical GR predictions.

Method: Uses the Kosower–Maybee–O'Connell (KMOC) formalism grounded in on-shell scattering amplitudes and leading singularities; derives spin-dependent angular impulse integrands valid to all orders in spin; performs consistency checks including covariant spin supplementary condition preservation and comparison with radial-action results; extracts non-relativistic potential from triangle leading singularities.

Result: Compact, all-order-in-spin expressions for the NLO angular impulse; verification that conservative dynamics preserve spin magnitude and covariant spin supplementary condition through 2PM order; agreement of quadratic-in-spin result with radial-action calculations; derivation of a spin-resummed gravitational potential reproducing known spin-orbit interaction at linear order.

Conclusion: Amplitude-based methods—particularly KMOC—are highly efficient and reliable for computing spin-dependent classical observables in black hole scattering; this work strengthens the theoretical foundation for applying scattering amplitudes to gravitational waveform modeling and effective-one-body frameworks.

Abstract: We compute the next-to-leading order (NLO) angular impulse (spin kick) in the scattering of Kerr black holes using the Kosower--Maybee--O'Connell (KMOC) formalism. Our approach is based on on-shell scattering amplitudes and leading singularities, allowing for a direct extraction of classical observables from quantum amplitudes. We derive compact expressions for the spin-dependent angular impulse valid to all orders in the spin variables at the integrand level, and show that these results reduce to known expressions in the appropriate limits. We perform detailed consistency checks: the conservative result preserves both the covariant spin supplementary condition and the spin magnitude through 2PM order, and the quadratic-in-spin conservative result agrees with existing radial-action results after translating between the direct KMOC spin kick and the radial-action observable. In addition, we extract the corresponding non-relativistic gravitational potential from the triangle leading singularities, obtaining a representation that resums spin effects and reproduces the known spin-orbit interaction at linear order. Our results provide further evidence for the efficiency of amplitude-based methods in classical gravitational dynamics, and highlight the KMOC formalism as a powerful framework for computing spin-dependent observables in binary black hole scattering.

</details>


### [82] [Geometric and Statistical Thermo Field Dynamics in de Sitter Spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23484)
*D. S. Cabral,L. A. S. Evangelista,J. C. R. de Souza,A. F. Santos*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper investigates quantum field dynamics for a massive scalar field non-minimally coupled to gravity in de Sitter spacetime, revealing observer-dependent vacuum interpretations (Bunch–Davies as vacuum for comoving observers, thermal state for static ones), and constructs a unified thermal framework using Thermo Field Dynamics that incorporates both geometric (cosmological horizon) and statistical (intrinsic temperature) doubling; it analyzes Bogoliubov evolution and particle production, showing conservation in comoving frame (matching CMB thermodynamics) and stimulated creation in static frame, with a characteristic thermal scale emerging for massive, non-minimally coupled fields.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To reconcile observer-dependent thermal perceptions of the Bunch–Davies vacuum in de Sitter space—specifically, the discrepancy between comoving (vacuum) and static (thermal Gibbons–Hawking bath) observers—and to construct a consistent quantum field-theoretic framework incorporating both horizon-induced and intrinsic finite-temperature effects.

Method: Applies Thermo Field Dynamics (TFD) to de Sitter spacetime, combining geometric doubling (from cosmological horizon) with statistical doubling (from intrinsic thermal bath); computes Bogoliubov transformations between frames; evaluates particle number densities in comoving and static coordinates; analyzes temporal evolution of Bogoliubov angle; considers limits (radiation, massive/non-minimally coupled cases).

Result: Demonstrates that TFD doubling reflects genuine physical structure—global causality plus finite-temperature effects; shows comoving particle number conserved in radiation limit (consistent with CMB thermodynamics); reveals Parker particle creation stimulated by finite-temperature effects in static frame; identifies a characteristic thermal scale and initial-condition dependence for massive, non-minimally coupled fields.

Conclusion: A unified TFD-based framework successfully integrates geometric and statistical thermal aspects of quantum fields in de Sitter space, showing that observer-dependent thermal behavior arises from fundamental spacetime structure and intrinsic thermality—not merely mathematical artifact—and provides physically distinct predictions across reference frames.

Abstract: The dynamics of a massive scalar field non-minimally coupled to gravity in an expanding de Sitter universe are investigated. It is shown that a comoving observer identifies the Bunch--Davies state as the vacuum, whereas a static observer perceives the same state as a thermal bath at the Gibbons--Hawking temperature. Motivated by this observer dependence, a thermal formulation based on Thermo Field Dynamics is developed by combining the geometric doubling associated with the cosmological horizon with the statistical doubling induced by a intrinsec thermal bath. The resulting construction reveals that the doubling procedure is not merely a mathematical artifact, but rather a manifestation of the global causal structure of spacetime together with finite-temperature effects. The temporal evolution of the Bogoliubov angle is analyzed and the corresponding particle number densities are evaluated in both comoving and static frames. In the radiation limit, the comoving number density remains conserved, providing a thermodynamic evolution consistent with that of the Cosmic Microwave Background, whereas in the static frame finite-temperature effects sti\-mulate Parker particle creation. For massive and non-minimally coupled fields, the interplay between geometric and statistical temperatures gives rise to a characteristic thermal scale and a nontrivial dependence on the initial conditions. These results provide a unified framework for describing quantum fields in de Sitter spacetime in the presence of both apparent horizon-induced and intrinsec thermal effects.

</details>


### [83] [Microscopic entropy of de Sitter spacetime and entropic solution to the old cosmological constant problem](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23522)
*Mariano Cadoni,Lorenzo Herres,Lorenzo Orlando,Mirko Pitzalis*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper proposes an entropic solution to the cosmological constant problem by interpreting the dimensionless coupling α — the ratio of de Sitter to Planck scales — as the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy of de Sitter spacetime and as a measure of horizon degrees of freedom; its monotonic IR-increasing RG flow yields a cosmological constant matching observation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the old cosmological constant problem by explaining the observed tiny value of Λ as a natural consequence of horizon entropy and microscopic degrees of freedom, rather than fine-tuning.

Method: Combines functional renormalization group (FRG) techniques, holographic principles, and emergent gravity ideas to interpret α as horizon entropy and analyze its scale-dependent RG flow α(k), imposing monotonicity (IR increase) as a physical constraint.

Result: The RG flow of α(k) is constrained to increase monotonically toward the infrared, leading to a predicted cosmological constant Λ ∼ H² ∼ (Mₚₗ² / N_dS), where N_dS ∼ α is the de Sitter horizon entropy; this yields Λ consistent with observations.
