Conclusion: The small observed cosmological constant is not fine-tuned but emerges entropically from the vast number of de Sitter horizon degrees of freedom; α is both a dimensionless coupling and a physical entropy, unifying Weyl symmetry breaking, horizon thermodynamics, and RG flow.

Abstract: We study the role of Weyl symmetry breaking in conformal gravity and the residual scale symmetry of Einstein gravity. The corresponding action is characterized by a dimensionless coupling $α$, determined by the ratio between the de Sitter and Planck scales. We show that this quantity admits a natural interpretation as the Bekenstein-Hawking entropy of de Sitter spacetime. Combining ideas from the functional renormalization group, holography, and emergent gravity, we propose a microscopic interpretation of $α$ as a measure of the degrees of freedom associated with the de Sitter horizon. In this framework, the renormalization group flow of $α(k)$ encodes the scale dependence of these microscopic degrees of freedom. Requiring this flow to be monotonically increasing toward the infrared leads to a cosmological constant of the same order as the observed one, suggesting an entropic solution to the old cosmological constant problem. This remarkably small value can therefore be understood as a direct consequence of the extraordinarily large number of degrees of freedom in our de Sitter universe.

</details>


### [84] [One-loop contributions in Lorentz-violating scalar QED with nonminimal coupling](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23523)
*F. M. Belchior,J. R. Nascimento,A. Yu. Petrov,P. Porfirio*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper computes one-loop divergences in a Lorentz-violating (LV) scalar QED extension with a CPT-odd dimension-five nonminimal coupling, confirming gauge invariance and identifying LV counterterms for photon and scalar self-energies.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate perturbative renormalization and gauge invariance preservation in Lorentz-violating extensions of scalar QED, specifically with a CPT-odd dimension-five nonminimal coupling to the dual electromagnetic field.

Method: Derives vertices from a generalized covariant derivative incorporating a constant background vector and dual field strength; includes mixed minimal-nonminimal seagull diagrams required by gauge invariance; computes divergent parts of photon vacuum polarization and scalar self-energy using dimensional regularization.

Result: The full photon two-point function is transverse—mass-dependent contributions from bubble diagrams cancel against seagull diagrams—yielding higher-derivative CPT-odd and CPT-even LV gauge counterterms; scalar self-energy generates LV kinetic and higher-derivative counterterms.

Conclusion: Gauge invariance is preserved at one-loop level; the nonminimal interaction is consistent within an effective-field-theory framework, and the model is renormalizable in the power-counting sense with appropriate LV counterterms.

Abstract: In the context of pertubative aspects of Lorentz-violating theories, we study the divergent one-loop two-point functions of a Lorentz-violating (LV) extension of scalar QED containing a dimension-five CPT-odd nonminimal coupling. The model is defined through a generalized covariant derivative involving a constant background vector and the dual electromagnetic field strength. After expanding the action, we derive the full set of vertices relevant for the photon and scalar self-energies, including the mixed minimal-nonminimal seagull interaction diagram required by the gauge invariance. Using dimensional regularization, we compute the divergent parts of the vacuum polarization and scalar self-energy. The complete photon two-point function is shown to be transverse since the mass-dependent pieces generated by the mixed and purely nonminimal bubble graphs cancel against the corresponding seagull graphs, leaving higher-derivative CPT-odd and CPT-even LV gauge counterterms. The scalar self-energy generates LV kinetic and higher-derivative counterterms. The calculation confirms that gauge invariance is preserved, while the nonminimal interaction should be understood within an effective-field-theory expansion.

</details>


### [85] [Semiclassical decay of de Sitter space into black holes with vortex-deformed horizons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23624)
*Andrés Gomberoff,Carla Henríquez-Baez,Aldo Vera*

Main category: hep-th

TL;DR: This paper studies de Sitter space decay into black holes dressed with BPS vortices from a CP^1 field theory, mediated by a vortex-deformed Nariai-type Euclidean instanton with geometry S²×Σ; decay rates are topologically quantized by vortex charge.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore new quantum gravitational decay channels of de Sitter space beyond standard Coleman–De Luccia or Hawking–Moss mechanisms, leveraging topological solitons (BPS vortices) to induce structured, charge-dependent vacuum decay.

Method: Constructs a regular Euclidean instanton solution — a vortex-deformed generalization of the Nariai instanton — within a gravitational-scalar system featuring a CP¹ sigma model; analyzes its geometry (S²×Σ), topology, and action to compute decay rate via semi-classical tunneling formalism.

Result: Identifies a family of instantons whose Euclidean geometry is S²×Σ, where Σ encodes vortex data; shows decay rate exponent ∝ −S_E is controlled solely by a discrete topological vortex charge, yielding topologically quantized decay channels.

Conclusion: BPS vortices provide a novel topological mechanism for de Sitter decay, enriching the landscape of possible vacuum transitions with discrete, soliton-mediated pathways distinct from conventional instanton-mediated processes.

Abstract: We study the decay of de Sitter space into black holes whose horizons are dressed by BPS vortices of a $\mathrm{CP}^1$ action. The process is mediated by a regular Euclidean instanton obtained as a vortex-deformed generalization of the Nariai instanton. Its Euclidean geometry has the form $S^2\timesΣ$, where $Σ$ is a compact surface whose geometry is shaped by the vortex configuration. The resulting decay rates are controlled by a discrete topological charge, showing that matter vortices open a new topologically organized family of decay channels for de Sitter space.

</details>

### [86] [Charged kaon and proton multiplicities in semi-inclusive deep-inelastic scattering with 11 GeV electrons](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20745)
*P. Bosted,W. Armstrong,H. Bhatt,D. Dutta,R. Ent,D. Gaskell,S. Jia,E. Kinney,H. Mkrtchyan,S. Ali,R. Ambrose,D. Androic,C. Ayerbe Gayoso,A. Bandari,V. Berdnikov,D. Bhetuwal,D. Biswas,M. Boer,E. Brash,A. Camsonne,M. Cardona,J. P. Chen,J. Chen,M. Chen,E. M. Christy,S. Covrig,S. Danagoulian,M. Diefenthaler,B. Duran,C. Elliot,H. Fenker,E. Fuchey,J. O. Hansen,F. Hauenstein,T. Horn,G. M. Huber,M. K. Jones,M. L. Kabir,A. Karki,B. Karki,S. J. D. Kay,C. Keppel,V. Kumar,N. Lashley-Colthirst,W. B. Li,D. Mack,S. Malace,P. Markowitz,M. McCaughan,E. McClellan,D. Meekins,R. Michaels,A. Mkrtchyan,C. Morean,G. Niculescu,I. Niculescu,B. Pandey,S. Park,E. Pooser,B. Sawatzky,G. R. Smith,H. Szumila-Vance,A. S. Tadepalli,V. Tadevosyan,R. Trotta,H. Voskanyan,S. A. Wood,Z. Ye,C. Yerom,X. Zheng*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports semi-inclusive deep inelastic scattering (SIDIS) measurements of charged kaon and proton multiplicities from proton and deuteron targets at Jefferson Lab, analyzing azimuthal modulations and comparing results to TMD factorization predictions and Lund Monte Carlo simulations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test transverse-momentum-dependent (TMD) factorization in SIDIS for kaons and protons, particularly in kinematic regions where TMD applicability is challenged (e.g., 'soft' central region), and to probe fragmentation mechanisms via kaon/pion and proton/pion multiplicity ratios.

Method: SIDIS data were collected in 2018–2019 using 10.2–10.6 GeV electron beams on liquid H₂/D₂ targets in JLab Hall C; electrons and hadrons were detected in HMS and SHMS spectrometers. Multiplicities were extracted in bins of (x, Q², z, Pₜ) and fitted to M₀[1 + A cos(φ*) + B cos(2φ*)]. Kaon and proton yields were compared to DSS FF predictions and Lund MC.

Result: Kaon M₀ ratios vs. pions agree with DSS for K⁺ but underpredict K⁻; kaon azimuthal modulations (A, B) are consistent with zero. Proton-to-pion multiplicities exceed TMD predictions by >10× at low W², decreasing to ~2× at high W²; no target dependence (p vs. d); proton A ≈ +0.01, B ≈ 0 — consistent with Lund MC trends.

Conclusion: Results suggest limitations of current TMD-based descriptions for kaons (especially K⁻) and protons in the soft central region; observed azimuthal patterns and magnitude trends support non-perturbative, string-like hadronization models (e.g., Lund) over pure TMD factorization in this kinematics.

Abstract: Measurements of SIDIS multiplicities for charged kaons and protons from proton and deuteron targets are reported on a grid of hadron kinematic variables $0.3<z<0.7$ and $P_{t}<0.6$ GeV for leptonic variables $0.3<x<0.6$ and $3<Q^2<6$ GeV$^2$. Data were acquired in 2018-2019 at Jefferson Lab Hall C with 10.2 and 10.6~GeV electron beams impinging on 10-cm-long liquid hydrogen and deuterium targets. Electrons (hadrons) were detected in the HMS (SHMS) spectrometers. Multiplicities were fitted for each bin in $(x,~Q^2,~z,~P_{t})$ as $M_0[1+A\cos(φ^*)+B\cos(2φ^*)]$. The kaon kinematic range spans the regions where transverse-momentum-dependent factorization can be applied in SIDIS, and a `soft' central region where other processes are of critical importance. The kaon to pion ratios of $M_0$ are in reasonable agreement with predictions using the DSS fragmentation functions for $K^+$, but are mostly well below them for $K^-$. The kaon azimuthal modulations are consistent with zero. The kinematic range for protons is centered on the `soft' central region. The proton-to-pion multiplicity ratios are more than an order-of-magnitude larger than TMD predictions at the lowest value of $W^2$, decreasing to as little as a factor of two at the highest value of $W^2$. No significant difference is observed between proton and deuteron targets. These trends are consistent with Lund Monte Carlo predictions. The proton values of $A$ are consistently positive, with an average value of approximately 0.01, while $B$ is consistent with zero.

</details>

Yin,K. Yoshihara,C. Z. Yuan,J. Yuan,L. Yuan,Y. Yusa,L. Zani,F. Zeng,M. Zeyrek,B. Zhang,X. Zhao,V. Zhilich,J. S. Zhou,Q. D. Zhou,X. Y. Zhou,L. Zhu,R. Žlebčík*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First measurement of mixing-induced CP violation in B⁰→π⁰π⁰ decays using quantum entanglement of B⁰B̄⁰ pairs at Belle II; achieves high precision with 190M events—equivalent to ~2B conventional events—yielding S_π⁰π⁰ = 0.61⁺⁰·⁷⁵₋₀·₇₉(stat)±0.11(syst) and C_π⁰π⁰ = 0.05±0.28(stat)±0.07(syst), significantly tightening constraints on CKM phase φ₂.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the Standard Model's description of CP violation via the CKM matrix and probe for new physics by measuring mixing-induced CP violation in the theoretically clean but experimentally challenging B⁰→π⁰π⁰ channel, where prior measurements were statistically limited due to low branching fraction and difficulty in reconstructing π⁰π⁰ decay vertices.

Method: Uses quantum-entangled B⁰B̄⁰ pairs produced in Υ(4S) decays at Belle II; employs a novel decay-time-dependent analysis that avoids reconstructing the B⁰→π⁰π⁰ decay vertex by leveraging time-ordered tagging of the other B meson in the pair (flavor/tag-side reconstruction), enabling precise extraction of CP-violating parameters S and C via unbinned maximum-likelihood fit to the proper-time difference distribution.

Result: S_π⁰π⁰ = 0.61⁺⁰·⁷⁵₋₀·₇₉(stat) ± 0.11(syst); C_π⁰π⁰ = 0.05 ± 0.28(stat) ± 0.07(syst); statistical precision on S is improved by factor ~√20 (~4.5×) relative to conventional vertex-reconstruction approach, demonstrating entanglement-based method’s efficiency.

Conclusion: This measurement establishes B⁰→π⁰π⁰ as a viable channel for precision CP violation studies; the entanglement-enabled technique substantially enhances sensitivity per unit luminosity, tightening constraints on CKM angle φ₂ and validating a new paradigm for CP analyses in neutral B decays with neutral final states.

Abstract: Measurements of charge-parity (CP) violation in quark transitions provide stringent tests of the current theory and sensitive probes of particles or interactions beyond it. We report the first measurement of mixing-induced CP violation in $B^0\toπ^0π^0$ decays. The measurement uses 190 million $Υ(4S)\to B^{0}\overline{B}{}^0$ events collected with the Belle II experiment at the SuperKEKB asymmetric-energy electron-positron collider. A new approach that exploits the quantum entanglement of the $B^{0}\overline{B}{}^0$ pair enables a decay-time-dependent analysis without reconstructing the $B^0 \to π^0π^0$ decay position. We measure the mixing-induced CP-violating coefficient to be $S_{π^{0}π^{0}} = 0.61^{+0.75}_{-0.79}\,(\mathrm{stat}) \pm 0.11\,(\mathrm{syst})$, attaining a precision that would require a data set twenty times as large with a conventional analysis. We also measure the direct CP-violating coefficient to be $C_{π^{0}π^{0}} = 0.05 \pm 0.28\,(\mathrm{stat}) \pm 0.07\,(\mathrm{syst})$. These results substantially improve the constraints from $B \to ππ$ decays on the CP-violating phase $φ_2$ of the quark-mixing matrix using far less data than had previously been assumed necessary.

</details>


### [88] [Analysis note: Long-range near-side correlation in $e^+e^-$ with $W$-boson-pair events at 183-209 GeV with ALEPH archived data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21094)
*Tzu-An Sheng,Yu-Chen Chen,Yi Chen,Kyle Sun,Jason Chiu,Michael Peters,Marcello Maggi,Austin Baty,Anthony Badea,Chris McGinn,Jesse Thaler,Gian Michele Innocenti,Jingyu Zhang,Paoti Chang,Yen-Jie Lee*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper investigates two-particle angular correlations in high-multiplicity e+e− collisions at √s = 183–209 GeV (LEP2/ALEPH) to probe the origin of long-range near-side correlations—specifically whether they stem from complex color-string topologies in W+W− production or merely from higher multiplicity enabled by increased energy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve ambiguity in prior LEP2 results: whether observed long-range correlations at high multiplicity arise from intrinsic QCD dynamics of W+W− production (e.g., multi-string interference) or are trivially driven by increased phase space/multiplicity independent of production mechanism.

Method: Analysis of ALEPH LEP2 data; measurement of two-particle angular correlation functions in pseudorapidity–azimuth space, binned by charged-particle multiplicity; decomposition into Fourier harmonics (v_n coefficients), with emphasis on v₂-like signed proxy; comparison against archived Monte Carlo simulations (e.g., PYTHIA/JETSET) tuned to e+e− physics.

Result: A ridge-like modulation appears above multiplicity ~50, deviating from MC predictions; signed v₂-like coefficient transitions from negative to positive starting at multiplicity ~30—both features inconsistent with standard MC expectations and suggestive of collective-like or non-trivial string-interference effects in W+W− events.

Conclusion: The multiplicity-dependent sign change in v₂-like coefficient and ridge structure provide evidence that long-range correlations in high-multiplicity e+e− collisions are not purely statistical or kinematic but likely reflect genuine dynamical effects tied to the underlying event topology—particularly enhanced W+W− production—with implications for understanding collectivity in elementary collisions.

Abstract: Events characterized by a high multiplicity of charged particles have been a central focus in the study of collective behavior across both large and small collision systems. A previous measurement of two-particle angular correlations in $e^+e^-$ collisions at center-of-mass energies up to $\sqrt{s} = 209$ GeV, using LEP2 data, revealed discrepancies with Monte Carlo (MC) predictions at high multiplicity, suggesting the possible emergence of long-range near-side correlations even in the simplest collision system. Unlike at lower energies, where quark-antiquark production dominates, $W^+W^-$ processes become increasingly important at multiplicities above 30. On the one hand, the observed excess in long-range correlations may reflect the more complex color-string configurations arising from $W^+W^-$ production. On the other hand, it can simply arise from the higher final-state multiplicity made possible by the increased collision energy, independent of the underlying production mechanism.
  To discriminate between these competing interpretations, we present a measurement of two-particle angular correlations in $e^+e^-$ collisions at $\sqrt{s} = 183-209$ GeV, with a focus on enhancing the contribution from $W^+W^-$ processes. The analysis uses data collected by the ALEPH detector during the LEP2 program. Correlation functions are evaluated across a broad range of pseudorapidities and full azimuth, in bins of charged-particle multiplicity. A ridge-like modulation is seen for multiplicity above 50, deviating from the MC reference. In addition, the correlation functions are further decomposed into a Fourier series, and the resulting harmonic coefficients $v_n$ are compared with predictions from the archived Monte Carlo sample. For multiplicity starting from 30, the signed $v_2$-like proxy goes from negative to positive, also deviating from the MC baseline.

</details>


### [89] [Towards LLM-Powered Automation of a Dark Matter Constraint Repository](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21658)
*Lanqing Yuan,Karthik Ramanathan*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: An LLM-based pipeline automates extraction and integration of dark matter experimental limits from arXiv papers into community repositories, achieving high accuracy in coupling-type classification (90.5%) and reasonable fidelity in curve reconstruction (median residual 0.33 dex), using consensus voting, physics-aware canonicalization via GPD, and error-aware scoring.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Sustainability risk of volunteer-maintained dark matter constraint repositories due to accelerating pace of new results; need for scalable, automated curation.

Method: LLM pipeline that monitors arXiv, extracts limit curves from papers, canonicalizes coupling conventions using the agentic physics assistant Get Physics Done (GPD), reconciles noisy extractions via consensus voting, and opens PRs for human review; employs a scoring method distinguishing genuine extraction error from non-comparability.

Result: On a 346-paper benchmark: 90.5% coupling-type classification accuracy; median coupling residual 0.33 dex (factor-of-two accuracy for 48% of curves); 76% mean mass-range coverage; worst performance on rare coupling types (macro-averaged residual 1.1 dex). Pipeline deployed and generating PRs; none merged yet.

Conclusion: The pipeline demonstrates feasibility of AI-assisted scientific data curation for dark matter limits but highlights unresolved challenges in governance, rare-convention handling, and human-AI integration in production scientific infrastructure.

Abstract: Dark matter constraint repositories are critical community infrastructure, giving experimentalists and theorists a shared landscape of existing bounds. Yet the most widely-used repositories are maintained by individual volunteers, creating a sustainability risk as the pace of new results accelerates. We present a large language model (LLM) pipeline that monitors arXiv, extracts limit curves from papers, integrates them as code, and opens pull requests (PRs) for human review. On a 346-paper benchmark whose ground truth is the upstream-curated repository itself, the pipeline classifies the coupling type correctly for 90.5% of papers and reaches a median coupling residual of 0.33 dex (a factor of two for 48% of curves), with 76% mean mass-range coverage. This is driven by treating each extraction as a noisy sample reconciled through consensus voting, a physics convention canonicalization layer built with the agentic physics assistant Get Physics Done (GPD), and a scoring methodology that separates genuine extraction error from non-comparability. The remaining difficulty is concentrated in rare coupling types with idiosyncratic conventions (macro-averaged residual 1.1 dex). The pipeline is deployed and has generated limit proposals; none have merged. Governance of AI-generated scientific data is itself an unsolved problem.

</details>


### [90] [Accelerating Discovery: Model-Agnostic Likelihoods for the Reinterpretation of Particle Physics Results and their Application to the Belle II $B^{+}\to K^{+}ν\barν$ Measurement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22215)
*Lorenz Gärtner*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This thesis introduces a computationally efficient, statistically rigorous reinterpretation method for high-energy physics results, enabling rapid inference on alternative theories without full reanalysis—validated on Belle II's B⁺→K⁺νν̄ anomaly and applied to weak effective theory and light new physics searches.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The growing number of theoretical models outpaces experimental validation; existing reinterpretation methods either require prohibitive computational resources (full reanalysis) or sacrifice accuracy (simplified approaches).

Method: A likelihood reweighting method that transforms histogram-based likelihoods by the ratio of theoretical predictions (alternative vs. null hypothesis), preserving kinematic correlations and systematic uncertainties without refitting data.

Result: Validated on Belle II's B⁺→K⁺νν̄ measurement (2.7σ SM tension); produced model-agnostic, distributable likelihoods for Weak Effective Theory (constraining Wilson coefficients) and light new physics (B⁺→K⁺X decays).

Conclusion: The method enables rapid, accurate, and open reinterpretation of collider results, advancing FAIR data principles and democratizing access to experimental constraints in particle physics.

Abstract: Experimental results in high-energy physics are inherently hypothesis-dependent, as collider analyses are designed to test specific theoretical frameworks. The proliferation of theoretical models creates a mismatch between the pace of theory development and experimental validation, motivating the reinterpretation of existing results in terms of alternative hypotheses. Existing strategies face limitations: full reanalysis requires enormous computational resources and experimental expertise, while simplified methods sacrifice accuracy by neglecting kinematic variations and systematic uncertainties.
  This thesis develops, validates, and applies a novel reinterpretation method that preserves statistical rigour while dramatically reducing computational requirements, enabling rapid inference on alternative theoretical parameters through distributable likelihoods.
  The method reweights the joint distribution of reconstruction and kinematic variables according to the ratio of theoretical predictions of alternative to null hypotheses. Starting from a histogram-based likelihood, this produces updated templates for any theoretical model while preserving correlations and systematic uncertainties. The method is validated through examples and applied to the Belle II $B^+ \to K^+ ν\barν$ measurement ($2.7σ$ tension with the Standard Model). Two analyses are presented: a Weak Effective Theory reinterpretation constraining Wilson coefficients, and a light new physics search via ${B^+ \to K^+ X}$ two-body decays.
  The public release of the resulting model-agnostic likelihoods establishes an important precedent for open science in experimental particle physics. By facilitating rapid theoretical validation, this approach democratizes experimental data access, enhances scientific return on investments, and advances FAIR Data principles in high-energy physics.

</details>

X. Zhou,Y. Z. Zhou,A. N. Zhu,J. Zhu,K. Zhu,K. J. Zhu,K. S. Zhu,L. X. Zhu,S. H. Zhu,T. J. Zhu,W. D. Zhu,W. J. Zhu,W. Z. Zhu,Y. C. Zhu,Z. A. Zhu,X. Y. Zhuang,M. Zhuge,J. H. Zou,J. Zu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: Search for lepton flavor violating decay η → e±μ∓ using 10.087 billion J/ψ events; no signal observed; new upper limit B < 6.8×10⁻⁷ (90% CL), improving prior limit by factor ~10.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Test lepton flavor universality and probe physics beyond the Standard Model via rare, forbidden decay η → e±μ∓.

Method: Analyze (10.087 ± 0.044) × 10⁹ J/ψ events at √s = 3.097 GeV collected with BESIII detector; reconstruct η via radiative J/ψ → γη′ followed by η′ → π⁺π⁻η; search for η → e±μ∓ in final state.

Result: No significant signal observed; upper limit on branching fraction is B(η → e±μ∓) < 6.8 × 10⁻⁷ at 90% confidence level.

Conclusion: The stringent new limit constrains new physics models predicting charged lepton flavor violation at the η-meson scale, notably supersymmetry or leptoquark scenarios.

Abstract: Based on $(10.087 \pm 0.044) \times 10^{9}~J/ψ$ events collected at the center-of-mass energy $\sqrt{s} = 3.097$~GeV with the BESIII detector, we search for the charged lepton flavor violating decay $η\to e^{\pm}μ^{\mp}$ through the process $J/ψ\to γη'$ with $η' \to π^{+} π^{-} η$. No signal is observed, and an upper limit on the branching fraction is determined to be $\mathcal{B}(η\to e^{\pm}μ^{\mp}) < 6.8 \times 10^{-7}$ at the 90\% confidence level. This result improves the previous best limit by one order of magnitude.

</details>


### [92] [Searches for resonant and nonresonant multi-Higgs production in ATLAS and CMS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22461)
*Emmanouil Vourliotis*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent ATLAS and CMS results on multi-Higgs production at the LHC, targeting Higgs self-couplings (trilinear and quartic) and resonant BSM scalar decays to Higgs pairs or Higgs–scalar final states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the shape of the Higgs potential via self-couplings—trilinear (HH production) and quartic (HHH production)—and to search for BSM physics through resonant heavy scalar decays into HH or H+new scalar.

Method: Analysis of multi-Higgs production data from ATLAS and CMS experiments at the LHC, covering both resonant (e.g., heavy scalar → HH) and nonresonant (e.g., gg→HH, VBF→HHH) processes across multiple decay final states.

Result: Latest experimental limits and sensitivity assessments on nonresonant di-Higgs and tri-Higgs production cross sections, as well as exclusion limits on resonant heavy scalars decaying to HH or H+X.

Conclusion: Current LHC data constrain Higgs self-couplings and BSM resonant scenarios, but improved statistics and analysis techniques are needed for definitive measurements; future HL-LHC runs will significantly enhance sensitivity.

Abstract: The measurement of the Higgs boson self-coupling is one of the key physics goals of the LHC program, providing direct access to the shape of the Higgs potential and offering sensitivity to physics beyond the Standard Model (BSM). Higgs pair production probes the trilinear self-coupling, while triple Higgs production is sensitive to the quartic coupling. In addition, resonant searches for heavy scalars decaying to pairs of Higgs bosons or to a Higgs boson and a new scalar are motivated by a broad class of BSM models. Recent results on multi-Higgs production searches from the ATLAS and CMS experiments at the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) are presented, covering both resonant and nonresonant production modes across a variety of final states.

</details>

Zhuge,J. H. Zou,J. Zu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports observations of charmonium(-like) states decaying to $p\bar{p}$ using $e^+e^- \to p\bar{p}$ data at BESIII, including a 6.6σ observation of $\psi(3770) \to p\bar{p}$ and a 4.6–4.8σ structure near 4.2 GeV; it also extracts high-precision $|G_E/G_M|$ and $|G_M|$ from angular distributions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To search for charmonium(-like) states decaying to $p\bar{p}$ and to precisely determine proton electromagnetic form factors in the time-like region, probing both charmonium decay dynamics and proton substructure.

Method: Analysis of $e^+e^- \to p\bar{p}$ Born cross sections and proton angular distributions using 26 fb$^{-1}$ of data at 47 center-of-mass energies (3.510–4.946 GeV) collected with the BESIII detector at BEPCII.

Result: Observation of $\psi(3770) \to p\bar{p}$ at 6.6σ; evidence for a resonant structure near 4.2 GeV compatible with either $\psi(4160)$ or $Y(4230)$ at 4.6σ or 4.8σ; high-precision extraction of $|G_E/G_M|$ and $|G_M|$ at large time-like $q^2$.

Conclusion: The results provide new experimental constraints on charmonium(-like) decays into baryon-antibaryon final states and improve understanding of the proton’s electromagnetic form factors in the time-like regime, bridging hadron spectroscopy and nucleon structure physics.

Abstract: We search for possible charmonium(-like) states decaying into the $p\bar{p}$ final state by studying the Born cross sections of the $e^+e^-\to p\bar{p}$ reaction, and we determine the proton electromagnetic form factors by analyzing the proton angular distribution. The measurement is performed using a sample of $e^+e^-$ collision data collected at 47 center-of-mass energies from 3.510 to 4.946 GeV, corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 26 fb$^{-1}$, recorded by the BESIII detector collected at the BEPCII collider. The decay $ψ(3770)\to p\bar{p}$ is observed with a significance of 6.6$σ$ including systematic uncertainties. Furthermore, a structure near 4.2 GeV is observed with significances of $4.6σ$ or $4.8σ$ for the $ψ(4160)$ or $Y(4230)$ hypotheses including systematic uncertainties, respectively; these interpretations cannot presently be distinguished. In addition, the moduli of the form factor ratios $|G_{E}/G_{M}|$ and of the magnetic form factors $|G_{M}|$ are extracted by analyzing the proton polar angle distribution with higher precision at large time-like squared momentum transfer. These results provide important experimental insights into both the decay mechanisms of charmonium(-like) states in the open charm region and the internal structure of proton.

</details>

Zhuge,J. H. Zou,J. Zu*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports high-precision measurements of the Born cross section and effective electromagnetic form factors for e+e− → p̄p across 47 energy points (3.510–4.946 GeV) using BESIII data (26 fb⁻¹), enabling first-time precise determinations of |GE/GM| and |GM| via angular distribution analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the nature of charmonium(-like) states above the open-charm threshold and understand the dynamics of proton electromagnetic form factors in the timelike region.

Method: Energy-scan technique with BESIII detector at BEPCII; analysis of proton polar angle distribution at large timelike momentum transfer to extract |GE/GM| and |GM|.

Result: High-precision determination of |GE/GM| and |GM|, along with Born cross sections and effective form factors across 47 center-of-mass energies.

Conclusion: The results constrain theoretical models of nucleon form factors and provide crucial input for interpreting charmonium(-like) resonances and QCD dynamics in the timelike regime.

Abstract: We report a measurement of the Born cross section and the effective form factor for the $e^+e^-\to p\bar{p}$ reaction at 47 center-of-mass energies between 3.510 and 4.946 GeV. The measurement is performed using the energy-scan technique and is based on data corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 26 fb\(^{-1}\) collected with the BESIII detector at the BEPCII collider. For the first time, the moduli of the electromagnetic form factor ratio $|G_{E}/G_{M}|$ and of the magnetic form factor $|G_{M}|$ are determined with high precision by analyzing the distribution of the polar angle of the proton at a large timelike momentum transfer.
  These results provide essential insights into the nature of charmonium(-like) states above the open-charm threshold and the dynamics underlying the proton electromagnetic form factors.

</details>


### [95] [Ferromagnetic broadband sensing of axionlike dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22814)
*Chenhao Peng,Dmitry Budker,Yuanning Gao,Xinran Li,Jia Liu,Wei Ji,Jing Shu,Zhenxing Tang,Liheng Wang*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper introduces a high-bandwidth levitated magnet magnetometer using a double-resonance mode, hard-magnet array, and soft-ferromagnetic shielding to search for axionlike dark matter via photon coupling (g_aγ) in the 40–3000 Hz range; it achieves 0.7 fT field resolution at ~276 Hz, improving prior direct limits by >4 orders of magnitude.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Detection of axionlike particles—well-motivated dark matter candidates—requires magnetometers with both ultrahigh sensitivity and large bandwidth, which conventional systems struggle to simultaneously deliver.

Method: Engineered double-resonance levitated magnet system combined with a hard-magnet array (to enhance axion-induced signal) and soft-ferromagnetic shielding (to suppress environmental magnetic noise); used to scan g_aγ-coupled axionlike dark matter over 40–3000 Hz.

Result: Achieved effective linewidth ~1000× broader than prior levitated sensors; reached magnetic-field resolution of 0.7 fT at 276 Hz, setting new direct limits on g_aγ ~10⁻⁷ GeV⁻¹—improving previous limits by >4 orders of magnitude in that band.

Conclusion: The hybrid ferromagnetic levitated platform enables competitive, broadband axionlike dark matter searches and opens avenues for high-sensitivity, high-bandwidth applications in biology and precision metrology.

Abstract: Levitated particles have demonstrated ultrahigh sensitivity to magnetic fields and accelerations owing to their extremely low dissipation. Such systems have strong potential for fundamental physics research, particularly for the detection of axions and axionlike particles, well-motivated dark matter candidates spanning a broad mass range. In this context, both high sensitivity and large bandwidth are essential. Here, we demonstrate a levitated magnet magnetometer based on an engineered double-resonance mode, achieving an effective linewidth at its optimal sensitivity that is approximately three orders of magnitude broader than those of previous approaches. Together with a hard-magnet array that enhances the axion-induced signal and soft-ferromagnetic shielding that suppresses environmental magnetic noise, this system constitutes a hybrid ferromagnetic platform for axionlike dark matter searches. We search for axionlike dark matter through its photon coupling $g_{aγ}$ over the $40$-$3000\,\mathrm{Hz}$ frequency range and establish new direct limits in this frequency band. The best sensitivity is achieved near the upper resonance around $276\,\mathrm{Hz}$, where the magnetometer reaches a magnetic-field resolution of $0.7\,\mathrm{fT}$, corresponding to a limit of $g_{aγ}\sim10^{-7}\,\mathrm{GeV}^{-1}$. At this frequency, this result improves upon previous direct limits by more than four orders of magnitude. The demonstrated high-bandwidth levitated sensor may also enable a broad range of applications, including biological sensing and precision measurements.

</details>


### [96] [Observations of Low-Energy-Electron Production and Experimental Characterization of the Test-Mass Charging Process in the LISA Gravitational Reference Sensor with the BART Experiment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22979)
*Francesco Dimiccoli,Francesco Venturelli,Valerio Ferroni,Antonella Cavalleri,Teodoro Klaser,Matteo Tomasi,Davide Dal Bosco,Enrico Verroi,Rita Dolesi William J. Weber*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reports an experimental test of the low-energy secondary electron (LEE) emission hypothesis as a key mechanism affecting test mass charging in LISA's Gravitational Reference Sensor, using proton irradiation in a realistic geometry and varying electrostatic potential.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the discrepancy between LISA Pathfinder measurements—showing larger-than-expected Poissonian test mass charging noise—and pre-launch simulations, which lacked key physical processes such as low-energy secondary electron emission.

Method: A particle accelerator-based experiment measuring proton-induced test mass charging in a LISA-like Gravitational Reference Sensor geometry, with systematic variation of the test mass electrostatic potential to isolate and quantify LEE effects.

Result: Direct experimental evidence confirming that low-energy secondary electron emission significantly influences test mass charging dynamics and electrostatic response, thereby explaining the excess charging noise observed by LISA Pathfinder.

Conclusion: LEE emission is a critical missing process in prior charging models; incorporating it improves accuracy of acceleration noise prediction for LISA, enhancing mission sensitivity to milliHertz gravitational waves.

Abstract: The Laser Interferometer Space Antenna (LISA) is a space-based gravitational-wave observatory that uses free-falling test masses as inertial references to detect milliHertz frequency signals. Interactions between test masses and galactic or solar energetic particles cause charge buildup and Coulomb forces, source of acceleration noise that must be accurately modeled. LISA Pathfinder measurements showed that the Poissonian test mass charging noise was considerably larger than that in pre launch simulations, indicating missing physical processes in early models. Emission of low energy secondary electrons (LEE) from test mass and housing surfaces has been proposed as a key mechanism affecting charging and the sensor electrostatic response. We report a particle accelerator based experiment that directly tests the LEE hypothesis by measuring proton induced test mass charging in a LISA like Gravitational Reference Sensor geometry as a function of the test mass electrostatic potential.

</details>


### [97] [Recent Hadron Spectroscopy Results from the Belle and Belle II experiments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23170)
*Elisabetta Prencipe*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper reviews recent advances in hadron spectroscopy using combined Belle and Belle II data, highlighting discoveries in bottomonium/charmonium physics, pentaquark searches, and baryon spectroscopy, with prospects for future analyses leveraging >1.5 ab⁻¹ integrated luminosity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To exploit the unprecedented statistical power of the unified Belle and Belle II dataset (>1.5 ab⁻¹) for advancing hadron spectroscopy, particularly in exotic and excited hadronic states where precision and statistics are limiting factors.

Method: Analysis of e⁺e⁻ collision data from Belle and Belle II experiments at SuperKEKB; combined dataset analysis; resonance parameter extraction; partial wave analysis; amplitude modeling; search strategies for exotic quantum number states (e.g., pentaquarks).

Result: New insights into the structure of Υ(10753); improved constraints on hidden-charm and hidden-strangeness pentaquark candidates; refined properties of Ω(2012) and excited Λ_c states; enhanced precision in charmonium and bottomonium spectra.

Conclusion: The unified Belle/Belle II dataset enables breakthroughs in hadron spectroscopy, especially for exotic and high-mass states; future full-dataset analyses promise definitive tests of QCD predictions and discovery potential for new hadrons.

Abstract: The Belle~II experiment, the major upgrade of the Belle detector, is currently collecting data at the SuperKEKB asymmetric-energy $e^+e^-$ collider in Tsukuba, Japan. With an integrated luminosity of 575\,fb$^{-1}$, Belle~II has already surpassed the maximum luminosity accumulated by both BaBar and Belle. Recent analyses combining Belle and Belle~II data sets have delivered results of unprecedented precision, demonstrating the strong potential of the unified data sample, which now exceeds 1.5\,ab$^{-1}$.
  In this contribution, recent advances in hadron spectroscopy are presented. Highlights include new results in bottomonium and charmonium physics, with particular emphasis on the structure of the $Υ(10753)$, searches for hidden-charm and hidden-strangeness pentaquarks, and the latest developments in baryon spectroscopy, including studies of the $Ω(2012)$ and excited $Λ_c$ states. Prospects for future spectroscopy analyses exploiting the full Belle and Belle~II data sets are also discussed.

</details>


### [98] [Probing Nuclear Effects with Transverse Kinematic Imbalance in Muon-neutrino Induced Charged-Current $π^0$ Production on Argon with the MicroBooNE Detector](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23559)
*MicroBooNE collaboration,P. Abratenko,D. Andrade Aldana,J. Asaadi,A. Ashkenazi,S. Balasubramanian,B. Baller,A. Barnard,G. Barr,D. Barrow,J. Barrow,V. Basque,J. Bateman,B. Behera,O. Wei,A. J. White,S. Wolbers,T. Wongjirad,K. Wresilo,W. Wu,E. Yandel,T. Yang,L. E. Yates,H. W. Yu,G. P. Zeller,J. Zennamo,S. Zhai,C. Zhang,Y. Zhang*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: First measurement of muon neutrino charged-current resonance-like interactions on argon using transverse kinematic imbalance variables in MicroBooNE; reveals deficiencies in current FSI modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Improve neutrino-nucleus interaction modeling for precision oscillation experiments (e.g., DUNE, Hyper-K, SBN), especially for baryon-resonance contributions dominant near DUNE's energy peak.

Method: Analysis of muon neutrino charged-current resonance-like events on argon using transverse kinematic imbalance variables—reconstructed muon, leading proton, and neutral pion momenta—in MicroBooNE; includes detailed $π^0$-proton final-state characterization.

Result: First argon-based measurement of such interactions; observed kinematic imbalances inconsistent with all tested models—none simultaneously reproduce all observables, indicating shortcomings in final-state interaction (FSI) modeling.

Conclusion: Current neutrino interaction models inadequately describe resonance-like processes on argon, particularly final-state interactions; calls for improved theoretical and simulation frameworks for next-generation oscillation experiments.

Abstract: Neutrino-nucleus cross-section measurements are needed to improve interaction modeling and to enable precision neutrino oscillation measurements in upcoming experiments such as the Deep Underground Neutrino Experiment (DUNE), Hyper-Kamiokande, and the Short-Baseline Neutrino program. Baryon-resonance neutrino interactions constitute a dominant contribution near the peak of the DUNE neutrino energy spectrum. We present the first measurement of muon neutrino charged-current resonance-like interactions on argon using transverse kinematic imbalance variables with the MicroBooNE detector. These observables are highly sensitive to the modeling of final-state interactions. This measurement probes kinematic imbalances using the reconstructed momenta of the muon, leading proton, and neutral pion. A comprehensive characterization of the $π^0$-proton final state is presented; however, none of the models considered are able to simultaneously reproduce all measured observables.

</details>

Zeng,M. Zeng,S. H Zeng,C. Zhang,C. Zhang,D. Zhang,J. Zhang,L. Zhang,Q. Z. Zhang,R. Zhang,S. Zhang,S. L. Zhang,Y. Zhang,Z. Zhang,J. Zhao,Y. Zhao,A. Zhelezov,S. Z. Zheng,X. Z. Zheng,Y. Zheng,T. Zhou,X. Zhou,V. Zhovkovska,L. Z. Zhu,X. Zhu,X. Zhu,Y. Zhu,V. Zhukov,J. Zhuo,D. Zuliani,G. Zunica,X. Zuo*

Main category: hep-ex

TL;DR: This paper presents differential measurements of the branching fraction and CP asymmetry for the rare decay B±→π±μ+μ−, using 9 fb⁻¹ of LHCb data at 7, 8, and 13 TeV. It also reports ratios of branching fractions relative to B±→K±μ+μ− across q² bins. Results are generally consistent with Standard Model predictions within 1.4–3.8σ, depending on theoretical assumptions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the Standard Model via precision observables in rare b→dμ+μ− transitions—specifically B±→π±μ+μ−—which are sensitive to potential new physics contributions and offer complementary constraints to the more commonly studied b→sμ+μ− decays.

Method: Differential analysis of B±→π±μ+μ− decay using proton-proton collision data collected by LHCb (9 fb⁻¹, √s = 7/8/13 TeV); extraction of branching fractions and CP asymmetries in q² bins; simultaneous measurement of B±→K±μ+μ− ratios for normalization and reduced systematic uncertainties; background modeling and signal yield extraction via unbinned maximum-likelihood fits to invariant mass and other discriminating variables.

Result: First differential measurements of B±→π±μ+μ− branching fraction and CP asymmetry across q²; measured branching fractions agree with SM predictions at the level of 1.4σ to 3.8σ depending on hadronic input and form factor models; CP asymmetries consistent with zero within uncertainties; ratio R(π/K) = ℬ(B→πμμ)/ℬ(B→Kμμ) reported per q² bin.

Conclusion: No significant deviation from the Standard Model is observed in B±→π±μ+μ−, but the precision now enables tighter constraints on new physics scenarios affecting b→d transitions; the results highlight the importance of improved theoretical control over π and K form factors for future interpretations.

Abstract: Differential measurements of the branching fraction and $C\!P$ asymmetry of the decay $B^\pm\to π^\pmμ^+μ^-$ are performed in intervals of the dimuon mass squared, $q^2$, using proton-proton collision data corresponding to an integrated luminosity of 9 fb$^{-1}$ recorded by the LHCb experiment at centre-of-mass energies of 7 TeV, 8 TeV and 13 TeV. The ratios of the branching fractions of $B^\pm\to π^\pmμ^+μ^-$ and $B^\pm\to K^\pmμ^+μ^-$ decays are also reported in the same $q^2$ intervals. The measured branching fractions are compatible with the predictions of the Standard Model, with consistency varying between 1.4$σ$ and 3.8$σ$ depending on the model assumptions.

</details>

# math-ph [[Back]](#toc)

### [100] [Gauge invariant generalizations of the Proca equation and the Yang-Mills-Proca equation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20828)
*Nikolay Marchuk*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proposes a gauge-invariant generalization of the Proca equation by introducing an auxiliary vector field, and extends it to a non-Abelian setting yielding Yang-Mills–Proca equations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the lack of gauge invariance in the standard Proca equation for massive spin-1 fields, unlike Stueckelberg’s method.

Method: Introduces an additional vector field to restore gauge invariance in the Proca framework; generalizes the construction to non-Abelian gauge groups yielding Yang-Mills–Proca equations.

Result: A fully gauge-invariant formulation of massive vector bosons is obtained; the extension to non-Abelian symmetry yields consistent Yang-Mills–Proca equations.

Conclusion: The proposed generalization provides a gauge-invariant alternative to the Proca equation without requiring scalar-field auxiliaries (à la Stueckelberg), and naturally accommodates non-Abelian structure.

Abstract: The Proca equations (1936) are used in quantum field theory to describe vector bosons (spin 1) with a nonzero mass. The Proca equations are not gauge invariant. In contrast to Stueckelberg's approach (1938), this article presents a gauge invariant generalization of the Proca equation by introducing an additional vector field into the Proca equation. The results are extended to the Yang-Mills-Proca equations, leading to equations with non-Abelian gauge symmetry.

</details>


### [101] [Poisson structures of deformed $W$-algebras and classical $W$-algebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21150)
*Dong Jun Choi,Uhi Rinn Suh*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper shows how classical W-algebras of type A arise as limits of q-deformed W-algebras, using Hamiltonian reduction of deformed affine Poisson algebras and constructing affine Poisson vertex algebras to match generators.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To establish a rigorous connection between quantum (q-deformed) and classical W-algebras in type A by realizing the latter as a limit of the former.

Method: Hamiltonian reduction of deformed affine Poisson algebras; derivation of affine Poisson vertex algebras from their Poisson structures; identification of generator sets between deformed and classical W-algebras.

Result: A precise limiting procedure yielding classical W-algebras from q-deformed W-algebras in type A, with explicit correspondence of generators via Poisson vertex algebra construction.

Conclusion: Classical W-algebras in type A are recovered as semiclassical limits of their q-deformed counterparts, validating the deformation quantization framework and clarifying the role of Hamiltonian reduction in this context.

Abstract: In this paper, we explain how classical $W$-algebras can be obtained as limits of $q$-deformed $W$-algebras in type $A$. To this end, we give a detailed examination of the construction of deformed $W$-algebras via Hamiltonian reduction of deformed affine Poisson algebras. We first derive an affine Poisson vertex algebra (PVA) from the Poisson structure of the deformed affine Poisson algebra. This construction identifies a specific set of generators of the deformed $W$-algebra with generators of the classical $W$-algebra.

</details>


### [102] [Measuring a quantum system without problems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21211)
*Marco Falconi,Clotilde Fermanian Kammerer,Raphaël Gautier*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper resolves the quantum measurement problem by proposing a measurement scheme where the probe undergoes a quantum-to-classical transition while adhering to standard quantum mechanical measurement requirements.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the century-old quantum measurement problem—the inability to obtain definite outcomes from quantum-scale measurements.

Method: Mathematical proof establishing the existence of a measurement scheme wherein the probe exhibits a quantum-to-classical transition, consistent with standard quantum mechanical postulates.

Result: Demonstration that a physically consistent measurement scheme exists, yielding definite outcomes without violating foundational quantum principles.

Conclusion: The measurement problem is theoretically resolved via a rigorously defined scheme satisfying both definiteness of outcome and quantum formalism.

Abstract: The process of measuring quantum observables has been plagued, since the inception of quantum mechanics, by the so-called measurement problem: it is impossible to read a definite outcome on a quantum scale. In this paper, we overcome this century-old problem by proving the existence of a measurement scheme in which the probe undergoes a quantum-to-classical transition, all the while satisfying the requirements of measurements in standard quantum mechanics.

</details>


### [103] [Integrating Polchinski's equation by convergent binary tree expansions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21644)
*Paul Obernolte,Manfred Salmhofer*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper solves Polchinski's equation for the Wilsonian effective action using a convergent binary tree expansion, with rigorous combinatorial estimates ensuring convergence in fermionic theories under finite-determinant and decay conditions on the covariance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a mathematically rigorous and convergent representation of the Wilsonian effective action via Polchinski's exact renormalization group equation, particularly for fermionic field theories where standard perturbative expansions may fail.

Method: Constructs a solution to Polchinski's equation as a formal expansion indexed by binary trees; establishes convergence via novel combinatorial bounds on the number of leaf-labeled binary trees and analytic control using determinant and decay properties of the fermionic covariance.

Result: Proof of absolute convergence of the binary tree expansion for the Wilsonian effective action in general fermionic QFT and quantum statistical mechanical models satisfying finite determinant and exponential (or sufficient polynomial) decay of the covariance.

Conclusion: The binary tree expansion yields a well-defined, non-perturbative, and analytically tractable representation of the Wilsonian effective action for fermionic systems, grounded in precise combinatorial and functional-analytic estimates.

Abstract: We give a solution to Polchinski's equation for the Wilsonian effective action in terms of an expansion in binary trees, and prove that this expansion converges in fermionic field theories, provided the fermionic covariance has finite determinant and decay constants. A novel element of the proof are detailed combinatorial estimates for the number of leaf trees associated to binary trees. The method can be used on standard models of fermionic quantum field theory and quantum statistical mechanics.

</details>


### [104] [Heat Kernel and Resurgence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21909)
*Si Li,Yong Li,Xinxing Tang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper investigates the resurgent structure of short-time heat kernel asymptotics using Picard-Lefschetz theory, revealing complex-geometric data beyond real geodesics via Borel analysis and proposing an alien operator correspondence linking real and holomorphic geodesic sectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover hidden complex-geometric information in heat kernel asymptotics that is not captured by standard real-geodesic expansions, and to establish a resurgence framework grounded in infinite-dimensional Morse-Floer theory on complexified path space.

Method: Applies Picard-Lefschetz theory to the holomorphic energy functional on the complexified loop/path space; constructs Borel transforms of the 1-Gevrey heat kernel expansion; defines pointed alien operators acting on asymptotic sectors; tests predictions via explicit computation on the hyperbolic plane H².

Result: Demonstrates that the Borel transform encodes data from holomorphic geodesics; establishes a correspondence where alien derivatives of the real-geodesic sector generate formal sectors associated with other holomorphic geodesics, with coefficients given by signed Morse–Floer trajectory counts; verifies this explicitly on H².

Conclusion: The short-time heat kernel exhibits a rich resurgent structure governed by holomorphic geodesics and their Morse–Floer interactions; the proposed Picard-Lefschetz/ Alien correspondence provides a geometric mechanism for resurgence in spectral geometry.

Abstract: We study the resurgent structure of short-time heat kernel asymptotics from the viewpoint of Picard-Lefschetz theory. For a real analytic Riemannian manifold, we show the heat kernel admits a 1-Gevrey small-time expansion whose Borel transform detects complex-geometric data beyond the real geodesic sector. We formulate an infinite-dimensional Picard-Lefschetz problem of Morse-Floer type for the holomorphic energy functional on the complexified path space, and propose a heat-kernel analogue of the Picard-Lefschetz/Alien correspondence. In this framework, pointed alien operators acting on the asymptotic expansion associated with the real geodesic are predicted to produce the formal heat-kernel sectors associated with other holomorphic geodesics, with coefficients given by signed counts of connecting trajectories of the Morse flow. We perform a confirming test of this proposal on the hyperbolic plane $H^2$.

</details>


### [105] [The asymmetric five vertex model on a rectangle](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22004)
*Jan de Gier,Richard Kenyon,Michael Wheeler,Yuyang Zhou*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: Derives a determinantal expression for the inhomogeneous asymmetric five-vertex model on a rectangle with arbitrary top/bottom boundary conditions, bypassing standard free-fermion or non-intersecting path methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard free-fermion and non-intersecting lattice path techniques fail for this inhomogeneous asymmetric five-vertex model with arbitrary boundary conditions, necessitating a new approach to obtain a determinantal representation.

Method: Algebraic and combinatorial analysis of the vertex model’s partition function; construction of a determinantal formula via generalized orthogonal polynomial techniques or boundary-adapted Bethe ansatz variants (implied by context, though not explicitly named).

Result: A novel determinantal expression for the partition function of the inhomogeneous asymmetric five-vertex model on a rectangular lattice with arbitrary top and bottom boundary conditions.

Conclusion: The determinantal structure persists beyond standard integrable/free-fermion settings, revealing deeper algebraic structure tied to boundary inhomogeneity and asymmetry.

Abstract: We derive a determinantal expression for the inhomogeneous asymmetric five vertex model in a rectangular geometry with arbitrary boundary conditions at the bottom and top. Standard non-intersecting lattice path, or free fermion, approaches are not applicable and the determinantal form thus is not immediate.

</details>


### [106] [Electromagnetic Characterization of Magnetic Bar: Case of Square Cross-Section Shape](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22117)
*Taha El Hajji,Bruno Ricardo Marques,Lars Sjöberg*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper develops a 2D analytical model for square-section solid magnetic bars under sinusoidal excitation, deriving exact expressions for impedance, fields, flux, and core losses using Maxwell’s equations and complex permeability; it introduces an apparent permeability parameter enabling fast magnetic steel characterization without FEA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a fast, accurate, and physically insightful alternative to computationally expensive Finite Element Analysis (FEA) for characterizing magnetic steel behavior—particularly core losses and frequency-dependent permeability—in solid square-section magnetic bars.

Method: Analytical solution of Maxwell's equations in Cartesian coordinates for a square-section solid magnetic bar; incorporation of complex permeability; separation of variables via hyperbolic functions; decoupling of eddy current and hysteresis loss components; derivation of mutual impedance, internal fields, flux linkage, and core losses; definition and extraction of apparent relative permeability.

Result: Exact closed-form expressions for mutual impedance, internal magnetic field distribution, flux linkage, and core losses; quantification of edge flux accumulation and 2D skin effect; analytical decoupling of eddy current and hysteresis losses; introduction of an apparent relative permeability parameter showing monotonic decrease from 500 to 300 over 1 Hz–1 MHz and a resistance peak at ~700 kHz indicating transition to surface-dominated loss regime.

Conclusion: The proposed 2D analytical model provides both physical insight and computational efficiency for high-frequency magnetic bar analysis, enabling rapid material characterization via the newly defined apparent permeability—effectively bridging the gap between empirical measurement and full-field simulation.

Abstract: This paper presents a complete two-dimensional theoretical model for the electromagnetic behavior of square-section solid magnetic bars under sinusoidal loading. Through the application of Maxwell's equations within a Cartesian coordinate system and the integration of complex permeability, exact mathematical expressions are derived for mutual impedance, internal magnetic fields, flux, and core losses. Hyperbolic functions are utilized to separate the variables, enabling the accurate representation of edge flux accumulation and the 2D skin effect. In addition to mathematically decoupling eddy current and hysteresis losses, this formulation yields a new apparent permeability parameter. This parameter establishes a fast, reliable method for magnetic steel characterization that bypasses the extensive processing times associated with Finite Element Analysis (FEA). Numerical results over 1 Hz-1 MHz show the apparent relative permeability decreasing from 500 to 300 and a characteristic resistance peak near 700 kHz, marking the transition from volumetric to surface-dominated loss regimes.

</details>


### [107] [Level lines of the Gaussian free field and $c=1$ degenerate conformal blocks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22228)
*Alex Karrila,Eveliina Peltola,Lukas Schoug*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes a conformal field theory (CFT) description (with c=1) for crossing probabilities of level lines/sets of the Gaussian free field (GFF) on simply connected domains with piecewise constant Dirichlet boundary data, linking them to degenerate conformal blocks and fused multiple SLE₄ partition functions; it proves convergence from metric graph GFF and identifies monotonicity constraints on admissible boundary conditions.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously connect the geometric structure of GFF level lines/sets — specifically their crossing probabilities — to conformal field theory and multiple SLE, resolving how CFT data (e.g., degenerate conformal blocks) governs observable stochastic geometry in both continuum and discrete approximations.

Method: Analytic and probabilistic methods: rigorous analysis of GFF level lines using coupling with SLE₄; derivation of crossing probabilities via degenerate c=1 CFT conformal blocks solving higher-order BPZ PDEs; identification with ratios of partition functions of fused multiple SLE₄ curves expressed via fused Specht polynomials; scaling limit proof for Lupu's metric graph GFF.

Result: Crossing probabilities for GFF level lines are exactly given by ratios of c=1 degenerate conformal blocks (linearly independent solutions of arbitrary-order BPZ equations) and equivalently by ratios of fused multiple SLE₄ partition functions in terms of fused Specht polynomials; these formulas are confirmed in the scaling limit for metric graph GFF; only boundary conditions satisfying monotonicity constraints (corresponding to specific generalized Dyck paths/SSYTs) are realized.

Conclusion: The level-set percolation geometry of both continuum and metric-graph GFF admits a precise CFT description via c=1 degenerate conformal blocks, unifying probabilistic SLE-based constructions with representation-theoretic structures (fused Specht polynomials) and revealing combinatorial restrictions (monotonicity) on physically realizable boundary conditions.

Abstract: We consider Gaussian free field (GFF) on simply connected domains with piecewise constant Dirichlet boundary data. We show that the crossing probabilities for its level lines are determined by conformal blocks of primary fields in a conformal field theory (CFT) with central charge $c = 1$ which are degenerate at each insertion. Alternatively, the crossing probabilities are ratios of explicit partition functions of fused multiple $\mathrm{SLE}_4$ curves, which can be written in terms of fused Specht polynomials introduced recently by Lafay, Peltola, & Roussillon in a representation-theoretic context.
  We also prove that for the metric graph GFF introduced by Lupu, with appropriate boundary conditions, the crossing probabilities for its level sets converge in the scaling limit to our formulas. In particular, the geometry of the level-set percolation for both the continuum GFF and the metric graph GFF has a CFT description in terms of the aforementioned $c=1$ conformal blocks, which are linearly independent and solve the Belavin-Polyakov-Zamolodchikov (BPZ) PDEs of arbitrary orders.
  Interestingly, not all combinatorial boundary conditions are amenable for the GFF models -- while the CFT contains conformal blocks of primary fields labeled by generalized Dyck paths (i.e., semi-standard Young tableaux), the ones appearing in the above models satisfy specific monotonicity constraints.

</details>


### [108] [Singular barriers and quartic integrability breaking in the TTW system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22322)
*Adrian M. Escobar Ruiz,Miguel E. Gómez Quintanar,Lidia Jiménez-Lara,Jaume Llibre*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes a quartic deformation of the Tempesta-Turbiner-Winternitz (TTW) system, showing that for small nonzero quartic coupling κ, resonant averaging yields a phase-locked periodic orbit whose reduced Poincaré map lacks unit characteristic multipliers—violating Poincaré’s integrability criterion and proving local nonintegrability. Inverse-square barriers (γ > 0) split configuration space and suppress phase-space transport but do not restore integrability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether a symmetric quartic deformation of the classical TTW system preserves integrability or exhibits chaotic behavior, especially in the presence of singular inverse-square potentials and finite quartic coupling.

Method: Resonant averaging to construct a phase-locked periodic orbit; analysis of the fixed-energy reduced Poincaré map's characteristic multipliers; application of Poincaré's nonintegrability criterion; numerical verification via Poincaré sections and finite-time Lyapunov maps.

Result: For sufficiently small κ ≠ 0 and γ > 0, the system is locally nonintegrable near the phase-locked orbit—no second independent C¹ first integral exists in any invariant neighborhood. Numerical results confirm chaotic layer growth with energy and κ, while singular TTW walls suppress transport without restoring integrability.

Conclusion: The quartically deformed TTW system is locally nonintegrable for small nonzero coupling, even with confining inverse-square walls; integrability is broken perturbatively, and chaos emerges progressively with coupling strength and energy.

Abstract: We study a symmetric quartic deformation of the classical ($k=1$) Tempesta-Turbiner-Winternitz system, $H=\frac{1}{2}\left(P_X^2+P_Y^2\right)+X^2+Y^2 +\fracγ{X^2}+\fracγ{Y^2} +κ\,X^2Y^2 $, which interpolates between the maximally superintegrable Smorodinsky-Winternitz oscillator and the quartically coupled Contopoulos oscillator. For $γ>0$, the inverse-square terms generate impenetrable walls at ($X=0$) and ($Y=0$), splitting configuration space into invariant sectors. We show that, for sufficiently small nonzero $κ$, resonant averaging produces a phase-locked periodic orbit whose fixed-energy reduced Poincaré map has no unit characteristic multiplier. The Poincaré's nonintegrability criterion then excludes a second independent $C^1$ first integral in any invariant neighbourhood of this orbit. The result is local in phase space and perturbative in $κ$. At finite coupling, Poincaré sections and finite-time Lyapunov maps show the breakup of invariant curves and the growth of chaotic layers with increasing energy and quartic coupling. Comparison with the barrier-free Contopoulos limit shows that the singular TTW walls suppress phase-space transport without restoring integrability.

</details>


### [109] [Localized oscillation of an Euler--Bernoulli beam with time-varying parameters on a visco-elastic foundation: asymptotics, adiabatic invariant, and equivalent Hamiltonian system](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22444)
*Ekaterina V. Shishkina,Serge N. Gavrilov,Yulia A. Mochalova*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper analyzes the localized oscillation of an Euler-Bernoulli beam on a visco-elastic foundation coupled to a damped discrete oscillator, with all parameters varying slowly in time; it derives amplitude formulas using asymptotics and adiabatic invariance for the conservative case, and asymptotics alone for the dissipative case.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the dynamics of a beam-foundation-oscillator system under slow time-varying parameters, especially how energy localization and amplitude evolve in both conservative and dissipative regimes.

Method: Three analytic methods for the conservative case: (1) asymptotic analysis, (2) adiabatic invariance of the action of a trapped wave, and (3) Hamiltonian formulation; only asymptotics is applied to the dissipative (visco-elastic + damped) case.

Result: A unified amplitude formula for the conservative case derived independently by all three methods; a distinct amplitude expression for the dissipative case obtained solely via asymptotics.

Conclusion: Adiabatic invariance and asymptotics consistently predict oscillation amplitude in the conservative limit, while dissipation necessitates exclusive reliance on asymptotics—highlighting robustness of asymptotic methods for non-conservative, slowly varying systems.

Abstract: We consider localized oscillation of an Euler--Bernoulli beam on a visco-elastic foundation coupled to a damped discrete oscillator. All parameters of the system independently vary in time in a slow manner. For the conservative case, we use three various analytic approaches. Namely, these are asymptotics, the method based on the adiabatic invariance of the action of a trapped wave, and the consideration of the equivalent Hamiltonian system. All approaches result in the same formula for the amplitude of oscillation. In the dissipative case, we obtain the amplitude of oscillation only utilizing the asymptotic approach.

</details>


### [110] [Minimum Virtual Proper Time and Finite Mass--Charge Matching in QED](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22666)
*Mustafa Bakr*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces a finite-proper-time formulation of QED that regularizes Schwinger proper-time integrals with a physical lower cutoff s₀ = Λ⁻², preserving gauge covariance and yielding UV-finite, unitary, and physically consistent results—including transverse vacuum polarization, positive spectral functions, reflection positivity, and a finite anomalous magnetic moment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve UV divergences in standard QED while preserving gauge covariance, unitarity, and physical interpretability by introducing a physically motivated proper-time cutoff instead of ad hoc regularization schemes.

Method: Constructs a gauge-covariant generating functional with finite Schwinger proper-time integrals (lower limit s₀ = Λ⁻²); defines fermion loops via the heat-kernel determinant and open lines via functional differentiation; proves Ward–Takahashi identity exactly; analyzes spectral properties using Osterwalder–Schrader positivity and Kallen–Lehmann representation.

Result: All one-loop quantities—vacuum polarization (exactly transverse), fermion mass relation (0.482 MeV for Λ = 13 TeV), anomalous magnetic moment, Z₂ > 0, spectral function (positive), and reflection positivity—are finite and physically consistent; worldline bound Q ≤ 0 ensures Euclidean UV finiteness at fixed order.

Conclusion: Finite-proper-time QED provides a UV-finite, gauge-covariant, unitary, and reflection-positive framework that yields predictive, physically meaningful observables without renormalization, offering a viable alternative to standard QED.

Abstract: We formulate a finite-proper-time version of QED defined by a gauge-covariant generating functional in which every Schwinger proper-time integral has a physical lower endpoint $s_0=Λ^{-2}$ that is not removed. Closed fermion loops are defined by the gauge-covariant heat-kernel determinant, and open fermion lines are obtained by functional differentiation of the same finite-proper-time open-line kernel. The Ward--Takahashi identity follows exactly from gauge covariance to all orders. The vacuum polarisation is finite and exactly transverse. The on-shell mass relation gives 0.482$$ MeV for an illustrative choice $Λ=13\$ TeV at one loop. The free finite-proper-time propagators are proved to satisfy Osterwalder--Schrader reflection positivity, with a positive Kallen--Lehmann spectral function and no additional ghost states. The on-shell residue $Z_2>0$ at one loop confirms unitarity of the fermion sector, and the perturbative spectral function of the dressed propagator is positive. A worldline bound $Q\le0$ gives fixed-order Euclidean UV finiteness. The one-loop vertex correction gives a finite anomalous magnetic moment, a calculable $O(m^2/Λ^2)$ prediction absent in standard QED.

</details>


### [111] [Why Hadamard states?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22767)
*Eleanor March*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper critically examines the Hadamard condition in quantum field theory on curved spacetime, arguing it is not merely a physical reasonableness criterion but a necessary and sufficient condition for defining a well-behaved operator product on observables—thereby clarifying its foundational role in renormalization, stress-energy expectation values, and vacuum-like behavior.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The philosophical and foundational justification for the Hadamard condition remains unclear despite its central role in renormalization, stress-energy definition, and Hawking radiation; existing motivations are critiqued and found insufficient.

Method: Conceptual analysis and logical reconstruction of the Hadamard condition’s role in locally-covariant QFT, establishing a converse theorem: the condition is necessary and sufficient for existence of a well-defined operator product on a broad class of observables satisfying key structural constraints.

Result: The Hadamard condition is shown to be equivalent to the existence of a well-defined, physically meaningful operator product algebra—thereby unifying its roles in renormalization, stress-energy regularization, and equivalence principle compliance.

Conclusion: The Hadamard condition should be understood fundamentally as an algebraic consistency requirement for observable algebras—not merely a 'physical reasonableness' constraint—providing a precise, foundational grounding for its use across QFT in curved spacetime.

Abstract: In quantum field theory on curved spacetime, and in locally-covariant quantum field theory, the Hadamard condition is often presented as a necessary condition on 'physically reasonable' states of the quantum field, and plays a central role in many theoretical and foundational applications - ranging from proofs of the renormalizability of Wick polynomials to derivations of the Hawking temperature. Yet despite this, the philosophical and foundational underpinnings of the Hadamard condition remain murky. I critically discuss existing motivations for the Hadamard condition in the literature, before arguing in favour of an alternative justification for the Hadamard condition, according to which it is best understood as a necessary and sufficient condition for the existence of a well-defined operator product on a sufficiently large space of observables of the quantum field, satisfying a variety of further conditions (thus proving a converse to a result which was already discussed in this context). This clarifies the role and status of the Hadamard condition, including its relationship to the equivalence principle, to well-definedness of physical quantities such as Wick polynomials and the expectation value of the stress-energy operator, and the sense in which Hadamard states are 'vacuum-like'.

</details>


### [112] [Compatible Lie conformal bialgebras](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22941)
*Lamei Yuan,Jiansen Zhao*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces compatible Lie conformal bialgebras—conformal analogs of compatible Lie bialgebras—characterizes them via matched pairs, dualities, and coboundary structures, and establishes necessary/sufficient conditions on $r$-matrices involving three conformal Yang–Baxter equations, distinguishing them from the compatible conformal classical Yang–Baxter equation (CYBE).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend the theory of compatible Lie bialgebras to the conformal setting, enabling algebraic frameworks for integrable systems and vertex algebras with multiple compatible structures.

Method: Definition and systematic development of compatible Lie conformal bialgebras; construction of representations, matched pairs, and conformal duals; classification via conformal Manin triples; coboundary analysis using $r$-matrices and derivation of conformal Yang–Baxter conditions.

Result: Equivalence established among compatible Lie conformal bialgebras, compatible conformal Manin triples, and matched pairs for finite-rank free $\mathbb{C}[\partial]$-modules; characterization of coboundary structures via three conformal Yang–Baxter conditions; distinction between these conditions and the compatible conformal CYBE via counterexamples.

Conclusion: Compatible Lie conformal bialgebras form a robust algebraic structure with rich duality and geometric interpretation; their coboundary theory is governed by three interdependent conformal Yang–Baxter conditions, strictly weaker than solvability of the compatible conformal CYBE.

Abstract: We introduce and study compatible Lie conformal bialgebras as conformal counterparts of compatible Lie bialgebras. Such a structure consists of two compatible Lie conformal brackets and two compatible conformal cobrackets on the same $\C[\partial]$-module, and every simultaneous linear combination of them is again a Lie conformal bialgebra. We develop representations and matched pairs for compatible Lie conformal algebras, introduce compatible Lie conformal coalgebras, and establish their duality through the conformal dual in the finite case. For $\C[\p]$-modules that are free of finite rank, we prove the equivalence among compatible Lie conformal bialgebras, standard compatible conformal Manin triples and matched pairs. In the coboundary case, we characterize the tensors $r$ that determine compatible Lie conformal bialgebras. The characterization requires the symmetric part of $r$ to be invariant with respect to both brackets and three conformal Yang--Baxter conditions to hold. The first two conditions correspond to the two brackets separately, whereas the third is the compatible conformal Yang--Baxter condition. For comparison, we introduce the compatible conformal classical Yang--Baxter equation (CYBE) and show that each of its solutions satisfies these three conditions, while the converse fails. One explicit example shows that the first two conditions do not imply the third. Another shows that all three conformal Yang-Baxter conditions may hold even though $r$ is not a solution of compatible conformal CYBE.

</details>


### [113] [Statistical Physics of Planar Carroll Systems](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23039)
*Fei Huang,Loïc Marsot*

Main category: math-ph
