TL;DR: This paper establishes a well-defined statistical physics framework for planar Carrollian systems—enabled by central extensions of the 2D Carroll algebra and angular momentum—using geometric thermodynamics; it shows rotation is necessary for thermal equilibrium, derives logarithmic entropy scaling and 2D ideal-gas pressure, and constructs an effective Hamiltonian from symmetry alone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the non-convergence of Carrollian statistical physics in general dimensions by exploiting unique features of the planar (2D) Carroll algebra—specifically its central extensions and compatibility with angular momentum—to define a consistent thermodynamic framework.

Method: Applies Souriau's geometric thermodynamics to construct the partition function for particles on a uniformly rotating disc; leverages symmetry properties of the centrally extended planar Carroll algebra; derives thermodynamic observables (entropy, pressure) and an effective Hamiltonian from first principles.

Result: Demonstrates that thermal equilibrium requires uniform rotation, with one central charge fixing the rotation direction; obtains entropy scaling as log(area) and pressure obeying the 2D ideal gas law; constructs an explicit effective Hamiltonian consistent with Carrollian symmetries.

Conclusion: A consistent, symmetry-based statistical physics for planar Carrollian systems exists—unlike higher-dimensional cases—and exhibits distinctive thermodynamic behavior rooted in the algebra’s central extensions and rotational structure.

Abstract: In this article, we define and study the statistical physics of planar Carrollian systems. While it has been shown recently that, for general dimensions, the Carroll limit of Poincaré statistical physics typically does not converge, we show that thanks to the central extensions of the Carroll algebra in the plane, and by considering systems with angular momentum, there exists a well defined notion of planar Carrollian statistical physics.
  Using Souriau's geometric thermodynamics, we compute the partition function for particles on a uniformly rotating disc, and show that rotation is inevitable for thermal equilibrium of planar Carroll systems, with one of the central charges determining the direction of rotation. We derive thermodynamic quantities in particular entropy, which scales logarithmically with the disc area, and pressure, which follows the two-dimensional ideal gas law. Though all results are obtained from symmetry considerations, we also derive the corresponding effective Hamiltonian.

</details>


### [114] [Jacobi exceptional orthogonal polynomials for extended Scarf I potentials with position-dependent mass](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23264)
*Christiane Quesne*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper solves the Scarf I potential problem with position-dependent mass via point canonical transformation, yielding exactly solvable rational extensions linked to X_m-Jacobi exceptional orthogonal polynomials and revealing deformed shape invariance in a supersymmetric framework.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend exact solvability of the Scarf I potential to position-dependent mass systems and uncover connections to exceptional orthogonal polynomials and deformed supersymmetry.

Method: Point canonical transformation (PCT) mapping the position-dependent mass Schrödinger equation onto the constant-mass Scarf I problem; inverse PCT to construct rational extensions; analysis of deformed shape invariance within deformed supersymmetric quantum mechanics.

Result: Exact solutions for Scarf I potential with m(α;x) = (1 + α sin x)^{-2}; new exactly solvable rational extensions associated with X_m-Jacobi EOPs of types I, II, III; demonstration of deformed shape invariance for both base and extended potentials.

Conclusion: The position-dependent mass Scarf I system is exactly solvable and admits rational extensions tied to exceptional orthogonal polynomials; its symmetry structure generalizes conventional shape invariance to a deformed supersymmetric setting.

Abstract: We show that the Scarf I potential problem in a position-dependent mass background of the type $m(α;x) = (1 + α\sin x)^{-2}$, $0<α<1$, can be solved by using a point canonical transformation mapping the corresponding Schr\" odinger equation onto that of the Scarf I potential with constant mass. The inverse point canonical transformation then provides some exactly-solvable rational extensions of the Scarf I potential with positive-dependent mass associated with $X_m$-Jacobi exceptional orthogonal polynomials of type I, II, or III. The Scarf I potential problem with position-dependent mass is shown to exhibit a deformed shape invariance property in a deformed supersymmetric framework. Such a property is also valid for extended potentials of type I and II. The results are illustrated with a simple example.

</details>


### [115] [Cantor Spectrum via a Reducibility-Duality Bridge for the Mosaic Almost Mathieu Operator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23422)
*Jiawei He,Yuan Shan,Yongjian Wang*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper proves the Cantor nature of the spectrum for the mosaic Almost Mathieu operator at all noncritical parameters, using duality, reducibility, and Moser–Pöschel techniques.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the spectral structure—specifically, whether the spectrum is a Cantor set—for the mosaic Almost Mathieu operator, a quasiperiodic model admitting a singular strip-Jacobi representation.

Method: Establishes a duality framework; extends the IDS–fibered rotation number correspondence to singular strip-Jacobi operators; reduces the problem to SL(2,ℝ) cocycles; applies Aubry duality, KAM-type reducibility theory, and the Moser–Pöschel argument.

Result: The spectrum of the mosaic Almost Mathieu operator is a Cantor set for all noncritical coupling parameters.

Conclusion: The Cantor spectrum result confirms a fundamental spectral property for this extended quasiperiodic model, generalizing known results for the standard Almost Mathieu operator and highlighting robustness under structural singularities.

Abstract: We study the mosaic Almost Mathieu operator, a quasiperiodic model that naturally admits a singular strip-Jacobi representation. By establishing a duality framework and extending the correspondence between the integrated density of states and the fibered rotation number to this setting, we obtain an effective reduction to $SL(2,\mathbb{R})$ cocycles. As a consequence, combining Aubry duality, reducibility theory, and the Moser--Pöschel argument, we prove that the spectrum is a Cantor set for all noncritical parameters.

</details>


### [116] [Which Waveguide Network Realizes a Prescribed Transmission Profile? An Exact Forward Construction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23645)
*Tristan M. Lawrie*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper introduces an analytically invertible framework for designing periodic waveguide networks to achieve prescribed wave transmission profiles—enabling direct, closed-form mapping from target transmission coefficients to physical parameters (bond connections, refractive indices, lengths, gauge phases)—with applications spanning 1D angular filtering to 2D image synthesis via spectral control.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome limitations of conventional inverse-design methods by providing a closed-form, analytically invertible route from desired wave transmission profiles to physically realizable waveguide network structures.

Method: Leverages scattering properties of periodic waveguide networks governed by a gauge-shifted Helmholtz operator; derives exact transmission coefficients and expresses lattice reactance as a Fourier expansion whose coefficients are analytically mapped onto graph architecture parameters.

Result: Enables direct determination of bond connectivity, refractive indices, segment lengths, and gauge phases from a prescribed target transmission coefficient; extends from 1D angular filtering to 2D image synthesis via exact spectral control of transmitted intensity patterns.

Conclusion: The framework establishes a rigorous, invertible design paradigm for wavefront engineering in periodic photonic networks, bridging abstract spectral specifications with concrete, fabricable geometries.

Abstract: We introduce an analytically invertible framework for wavefront construction based on the scattering properties of periodic waveguide networks governed by a gauge-shifted Helmholtz operator. By determining the exact transmission coefficients of the network, we express the lattice reactance as a Fourier expansion whose coefficients are analytically mapped onto the underlying graph architecture, allowing the required bond connections, refractive indices, lengths, and gauge phases to be determined directly from a prescribed target transmission coefficient. In contrast to conventional inverse-design approaches, the present formulation provides a closed-form route from desired wave transmission profiles to physically realisable structures. The framework extends naturally from one-dimensional angular filtering to two-dimensional image synthesis, where arbitrary transmitted intensity patterns are reconstructed through exact spectral control of the network scattering response.

</details>


### [117] [Position-Space Renormalization and Half-Space Truncations in $φ^4_4$](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23650)
*Majdouline Borji*

Main category: math-ph

TL;DR: This paper establishes that half-space truncations can be applied to renormalized correlators in massive Euclidean φ⁴₄ theory without extra renormalization, using a position-space Wilson–Polchinski flow framework with tailored power-counting and Besov–Hölder estimates.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To rigorously justify the use of half-space observables in massive Euclidean φ⁴₄ theory without introducing additional renormalization counterterms, addressing analytic challenges posed by boundary singularities and UV regularization.

Method: Develops a position-space renormalization framework based on the Wilson–Polchinski flow equation, employing a hierarchy of power-counting spaces adapted to the singularity structure of correlators; derives uniform power-counting bounds and Besov–Hölder regularity estimates.

Result: Proves that products of renormalized correlators with smooth approximations of the half-space indicator converge distributionally and uniformly in the ultraviolet cutoff; establishes convergence of correlators in explicitly identified Besov spaces as the UV cutoff → ∞.

Conclusion: Half-space observables are well-defined distributional limits in massive φ⁴₄ theory without further renormalization; the result is grounded in refined regularity and UV-uniform control via a geometrically adapted renormalization scheme.

Abstract: In this paper, we study half-space observables in the massive Euclidean $φ^4_4$ theory. We prove that the renormalized correlators can be multiplied by half-space truncations without requiring any additional renormalization. More precisely, products with smooth approximations of the half-space indicator converge to well-defined distributional limits, uniformly in the ultraviolet cutoff. The proof relies on a position-space renormalization framework for the Wilson--Polchinski flow equation based on a hierarchy of power-counting spaces adapted to the singularity structure of the correlators. This yields uniform power-counting bounds and Besov--Hölder regularity estimates for the renormalized correlators. As a consequence, the correlators converge as the ultraviolet cutoff tends to infinity, and the convergence takes place in explicitly identified Besov spaces.

</details>


<div id='gr-qc'></div>

# gr-qc [[Back]](#toc)

### [118] [Scalar-scalar-tensor interaction in DHOST theories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20730)
*S. Mironov,V. Volkova*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives the cubic action for cosmological perturbations in quadratic DHOST theories, focusing on scalar-scalar-tensor interactions in unitary gauge, to enable computation of gravitational wave decay into scalars in late-time dark energy models.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To estimate the decay rate of gravitational waves into two scalar excitations in quadratic DHOST dark energy models during the late-time Universe.

Method: Derives the general cubic action for perturbations about a cosmological background in the quadratic subclass of DHOST theories using a fully covariant formulation and imposing unitary gauge at the perturbation level.

Result: Obtains the complete cubic interaction Lagrangian for scalar-scalar-tensor couplings in quadratic DHOST theories, enabling quantitative analysis of graviton-to-scalars decay.

Conclusion: The derived cubic action constitutes the essential theoretical foundation for probing non-linear gravitational-wave phenomenology in viable DHOST dark energy scenarios.

Abstract: We derive the general cubic action for perturbations about a cosmological background in the quadratic subclass of degenerate higher-order scalar-tensor (DHOST) theories, focusing on scalar-scalar-tensor interactions. We adopt a fully covariant formulation and implement unitary gauge at the level of perturbations. This mixed sector provides the key ingredient for estimating the decay rate of a gravitational wave into two scalar excitations in the quadratic DHOST models of dark energy in the late-time Universe.

</details>


### [119] [Study of Cosmic Acceleration of the Universe in the Presence of Bulk Viscous Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20759)
*Dheeraj Singh Rana*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This thesis explores viscous f(Q) gravity—a non-Riemannian extension of GR—as a viable explanation for cosmic acceleration, deriving and constraining exact cosmological solutions with bulk viscosity, and assessing their viability across cosmic epochs via dynamical systems and observational data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The observed late-time accelerated expansion of the Universe, inconsistent with standard GR + ΛCDM without dark energy, motivates exploring modified gravity theories; specifically, non-Riemannian f(Q) gravity incorporating bulk viscosity to address cosmological tensions and provide geometric alternatives to dark energy.

Method: Analytical derivation of exact cosmological solutions in f(Q) gravity coupled to viscous fluid (bulk viscosity); parameter constraint using observational datasets (e.g., SNe Ia, H(z), BAO); dynamical systems analysis to study fixed points and stability; viability assessment across radiation-, matter-, and dark-energy-dominated epochs.

Result: Viscous f(Q) models yield exact solutions consistent with late-time acceleration; constrained parameters align with observational data; dynamical analysis confirms existence of stable attractor solutions spanning standard cosmological eras—including transition from deceleration to acceleration—without invoking dark energy.

Conclusion: Bulk-viscous f(Q) gravity constitutes a theoretically consistent and observationally viable alternative to ΛCDM, offering a geometric origin for cosmic acceleration while maintaining compatibility with known cosmological history.

Abstract: Over the past century, both theoretical advancements and experimental observations have established General Relativity (GR) as the most successful framework for describing gravitational phenomena. However, observations from multiple cosmological probes over the last two decades have provided compelling evidence for the accelerated expansion of the Universe. The rapid progress in observational astronomy and precision cosmology has highlighted several challenges that motivate the search for extensions or alternatives to General Relativity. In this thesis, we investigate alternative formulations of gravity based on non-Riemannian geometry, with particular emphasis on (f(Q)) gravity in the presence of bulk viscosity. We first explore the existence of exact cosmological solutions for viscous fluid models within the framework of (f(Q)) gravity and constrain the free parameters of these solutions using observational datasets. The resulting constrained models are then employed to study the evolutionary history of cosmic expansion. Our analysis demonstrates that the proposed viscous (f(Q)) gravity models can successfully account for the observed late-time acceleration of the Universe. Furthermore, we examine several classes of nonlinear viscous (f(Q)) gravity models through both dynamical systems and observational analyses. Particular attention is given to the cosmological viability of these models and their ability to reproduce the different evolutionary epochs of the Universe, ranging from matter-dominated eras to accelerated expansion phases.

</details>


### [120] [Selective enhancement of quantum decay channels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20766)
*Pritam Nanda,Kinjalk Lochan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper shows that cavity boundary conditions enhance low-energy quantum decay rates by boosting infrared mode density and transition probabilities—offering a geometric alternative to high-energy experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To overcome the suppression of low-energy quantum decays in free space caused by vanishing mode density in the infrared, and to explore alternatives to high-energy experiments for probing QED and new physics.

Method: Theoretical analysis of quantum field decay rates under controlled boundary conditions (e.g., cavities), comparing mode density and transition probability distributions in free space versus confined geometries; identifies resonant cavity configurations that amplify infrared contributions.

Result: Cavity confinement significantly enhances low-energy decay rates due to increased infrared mode support and resonant amplification; specific geometries act as 'sweet spots' for dramatic rate enhancement.

Conclusion: Cavity engineering provides a viable, low-energy pathway to access otherwise suppressed quantum decay processes, with direct implications for precision QED tests and searches for exotic physics.

Abstract: In the decay of quantum particles under field theoretic consideration, the decay rate is typically a convolution of the density of modes the primary field is allowed to decay into and the allowed probability density for the field to decay into such modes. In free space, though many such processes show high amplitude of such transitions towards the infrared sector, the depletion of allowed mode density in that regime arrests the efficacy of such decays at low energies. Therefore in free space, in order to enhance the decay rate, one needs the transition probability density to be rich enough towards the high energy sector where mode density support is also high enough to make the rate sufficiently large. In this work we argue that in the controlled boundary condition environment e.g. in a cavity, the mode functions of product field receive significant support towards their infrared sector, boosting the probability (and hence rates) of low energy processes. The cavity geometry offers sweet spots in terms of resonant geometry around which the interaction of a primary field with product fields receives dramatic enhancement, significantly enlarging its decay rates. Therefore, a judicious selection of cavity geometry serves as a potential substitute to studying interesting processes at high energy. The results have direct relevance for the study of QED processes and implications for the study of exotic new physics are also discussed.

</details>


### [121] [The Cosmological Constant Problem: An Accessible Introduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20778)
*Ali Kaya,Adam Lahey*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper provides a pedagogical exposition of the cosmological constant problem using a massive real scalar field, computing quantum vacuum energy density and pressure in both flat and expanding spacetimes with multiple regularization schemes; it clarifies that the 120-order-of-magnitude discrepancy is oversimplified but reaffirms a deep theoretical mismatch, while highlighting conceptual issues like vacuum non-uniqueness and scale choices in cosmology.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To offer an accessible, pedagogical treatment of the cosmological constant problem—clarifying misconceptions (e.g., the naive 120-orders-of-magnitude estimate) and exposing subtleties (vacuum ambiguity, regularization dependence, scale choices) often omitted in introductory accounts.

Method: Computes vacuum energy density and pressure for a massive real scalar field using dimensional regularization, momentum-cutoff regularization, and adiabatic regularization, both in Minkowski spacetime and in a classical Friedmann–Lemaître–Robertson–Walker (FLRW) expanding background; treats gravity classically (no quantum gravity), employs standard QFT in curved spacetime formalism.

Result: Demonstrates that regularization and renormalization significantly affect the magnitude and interpretation of vacuum energy, invalidating the simplistic 120-orders estimate; confirms a large residual discrepancy remains after proper QFT treatment; identifies conceptual tensions—including non-uniqueness of the vacuum state and ambiguity between comoving vs. physical scales—in relating QFT vacuum to cosmic acceleration.

Conclusion: The cosmological constant problem persists not merely due to crude estimates but because fundamental QFT principles—when rigorously applied in an expanding universe—yield results incompatible with observed dark energy density; resolving it requires confronting deep interface issues between quantum field theory and classical cosmology.

Abstract: We present a pedagogical introduction to the cosmological constant problem that requires only basic knowledge of quantum field theory and general relativity. A massive real scalar field is used to illustrate how the quantum vacuum energy density and pressure can be calculated both in flat spacetime and in an expanding universe. Detailed computations are provided for dimensional, cutoff, and adiabatic regularizations. No attempt is made to address quantum gravitational effects, and the expanding-universe background is treated classically. We point out that although the commonly cited discrepancy of 120 orders of magnitude between theory and observation is based on an estimate that does not account for regularization and renormalization, fundamental principles of quantum field theory nevertheless lead to a huge mismatch. In addition to this large discrepancy, we emphasize that there are also conceptual challenges related to cosmic expansion, such as the choice between comoving and physical scales in certain contexts and the non-uniqueness of vacuum.

</details>


### [122] [$\mathrm{ISIM}(2)$ gravitational gauge model via Soft Algebra formalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20806)
*J. M. B. Matzenbacher,J. M. Hoff da Silva*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a torsionless gravitational model by gauging the ISIM(2) Lorentz subgroup using the soft algebra formalism, deriving a closed-form algebra and an invariant action.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To formulate a consistent gravitational theory based on the ISIM(2) symmetry—a non-semisimple subgroup of the Lorentz group—via the soft algebra formalism, enabling gauge-theoretic gravity beyond standard Poincaré or (A)dS frameworks.

Method: Gauging of the ISIM(2) Lie algebra using soft algebra techniques; construction of a torsionless Cartan connection; derivation of curvature constraints and invariant action via geometric Lagrangian methods.

Result: A complete, diffeomorphism- and local ISIM(2)-invariant torsionless gravitational action is obtained, with explicit expressions for the algebra structure and field equations implied by the variational principle.

Conclusion: The ISIM(2)-based gauge gravity framework provides a viable, lower-symmetry alternative to standard gauge gravities, potentially relevant for null-surface or Carrollian geometries and asymptotically flat or ultra-relativistic limits.

Abstract: This work presents a comprehensive account of a gravitational model based on the gauging of the $\mathrm{ISIM}(2)$ Lorentz subgroup parameters through the so-called soft algebra formalism. After presenting a closed form for $\mathfrak{sim}(2)$ and its inhomogeneous extension, we proceed to develop a complete torsionless gravitational model, culminating in an invariant action.

</details>


### [123] [Exact solutions using power law scalar potential in the Saez-Ballester-K-essence like theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20894)
*J. Socorro,A. Gil-Ocaranza,Ximena López-Mujica,Cesar Aarón Pacheco-Vázquez*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper constructs a K-essence cosmological model using a negative power-law Sáez–Ballester potential, maps it via field redefinition to a solvable FLRW system, obtains exact classical solutions showing late-time de Sitter-like acceleration (q → −1), derives and solves the Wheeler–DeWitt equation exactly, yielding a consistent classical–quantum description where the scalar field acts as a fixed cosmic background.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To construct a tractable K-essence model admitting both exact classical and quantum cosmological solutions, bridging phenomenologically viable late-time acceleration with a well-defined quantum gravitational framework.

Method: Field redefinition (φ → φ) to map the K-essence action with negative power-law Sáez–Ballester potential onto a known integrable FLRW model; Hamiltonian formulation for exact classical integration; canonical quantization leading to the Wheeler–DeWitt equation with exact analytical solutions.

Result: Exact classical solutions for scale factor and scalar field exhibiting asymptotic de Sitter expansion (q → −1); exact analytical solutions of the Wheeler–DeWitt equation; quantum solutions indicate the scalar field behaves as a fixed background rather than a dynamical degree of freedom.

Conclusion: The model provides a rare fully solvable K-essence framework with consistent classical dynamics and quantum description, supporting late-time cosmic acceleration while suggesting a background role for the scalar field in quantum cosmology.

Abstract: We investigate a K-essence like cosmological model whose scalar-field potential is constructed from a negative power-law Sáez--Ballester potential. By means of a suitable field redefinition from $φ$ to $\varphi$, we show that the resulting field equations acquire a mathematical structure analogous to that of a previously solved Friedmann-Lemaître-Robertson-Walker (FLRW) cosmological model. This correspondence allows us to obtain exact classical solutions for both the scale factor and the scalar field within the Hamiltonian formalism. The resulting cosmological dynamics exhibits a late-time accelerated expansion, with the deceleration parameter approaching the asymptotic value $q\rightarrow -1$, characteristic of a de Sitter phase. At the quantum level, the corresponding Wheeler-DeWitt (WDW) equation is derived and exact quantum solutions are obtained. These results provide a consistent classical and quantum description of the cosmological evolution generated by this class of K-essence models. In this formalism, the scalar field remains as a cosmic background where the universe unfolds, which is glimpsed from the quantum solution perspective.

</details>


### [124] [Scattering, Hawking Radiation and Neutrino Energy Deposition in Euler-Heisenberg Black Holes Surrounded by Perfect Fluid Dark Matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20931)
*Ramon Becar,P. A. Gonzalez,Ali Ovgun,Joel Saavedra,Yerko Vasquez*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates scattering and dynamical properties of an Euler-Heisenberg black hole embedded in perfect fluid dark matter (PFDM), analyzing quasinormal modes, greybody factors, absorption cross sections, Hawking radiation, and neutrino-antineutrino annihilation—finding PFDM contracts optical structure and enhances damping, while Euler-Heisenberg effects are charge-dependent and weaker near the horizon.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically compare how nonlinear electrodynamics (via Euler-Heisenberg correction) and dark-matter environments (via perfect fluid dark matter halo) jointly influence black hole perturbations, scattering, and emission properties—beyond standard GR or vacuum BH models.

Method: Analyzes scalar, electromagnetic, and effective axial spin-2 perturbations on a fixed Euler-Heisenberg + PFDM black hole background; computes quasinormal modes using 13th-order WKB + Padé resummation; derives near-extremal purely imaginary QNMs analytically; calculates exact greybody factors via numerical integration of radial wave equation; computes absorption cross sections, Hawking spectra, and relativistic enhancement of neutrino-antineutrino annihilation.

Result: PFDM parameter contracts photon sphere, increases QNM oscillation frequency, enhances damping rate, suppresses transmission, and yields a spin-nearly-independent purely imaginary QNM branch in near-extremal limit; Euler-Heisenberg correction induces milder near-horizon deformation, significant only at large charge; analytical greybody lower bounds are validated against numerical results; neutrino-antineutrino annihilation is relativistically enhanced outside the BH.

Conclusion: The combined Euler-Heisenberg and PFDM framework provides a unified scattering formalism to disentangle environmental (dark matter) and intrinsic (nonlinear electrodynamics) imprints on black hole observables—enabling potential discrimination between competing beyond-GR scenarios using gravitational-wave and multi-messenger signatures.

Abstract: We study the dynamical and scattering properties for the Euler-Heisenberg BH surrounded by perfect fluid dark matter. The geometry contains a compact non-linear electrodynamic correction governed by the EH coupling and a logarithmic dark-matter contribution governed by the surrounding PFDM halo. We study the scalar, electromagnetic and a particular effective axial spin-2 channel constructed on the fixed EH plus PFDM background, acting as a proxy for the gravitational-like perturbation problem and not for the fully coupled gravitational perturbation problem. We compute the quasinormal-mode spectrum employing a thirteenth-order WKB method supplemented with Padé resummation and compare it with the eikonal prediction calculated in terms of the angular frequency of the photon sphere and the Lyapunov exponent. Moreover, we study the near-extremal configurations and derive a purely imaginary branch of quasinormal frequencies in the near-horizon region, whose damping rate increases with the PFDM parameter and is nearly spin-independent. We then compute exact greybody factors by direct numerical integration of the radial wave equation and compare them to analytical lower bounds. We also analyze the absorption cross sections and the Hawking emission spectra. We also calculate the relativistic enhancement of the neutrino-antineutrino annihilation channel outside the EHPFDM black hole. We find that the PFDM parameter contracts the optical structure, increases the oscillation frequency, enhances the damping rate and suppresses transmission. On the other hand, the Euler-Heisenberg correction leads to a weaker near-horizon deformation whose effect becomes relevant for sufficiently large charge. These results provide a common scattering framework for comparing the impact of dark-matter environments and nonlinear electrodynamics

</details>


### [125] [Quantum Dust from the Curse of Dimensionality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20943)
*Kenan Oggad*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper identifies a kinematic, geometry-of-states explanation—via concentration of measure on Fubini-Study space—for why diverse quantum gravity approaches consistently yield a spectral dimension of two at short scales; it argues this value is often an artifact of measurement (diffusion probe on equidistant state dust), not intrinsic spacetime structure, and introduces 'spectral faithfulness'—a criterion based on Laplacian eigenvalue density near zero—to distinguish genuine dimensionality from probe-induced artifacts; all core claims are machine-verified in Lean 4.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the ubiquitous emergence of spectral dimension two across disparate quantum gravity theories—not as dynamical coincidence but as a universal kinematic consequence of high-dimensional quantum state geometry, independent of field equations.

Method: Applies concentration of measure on the Fubini-Study manifold of pure quantum states to show random state samples collapse to equidistant 'dust'; analyzes diffusion-based spectral dimension estimation on such dust; derives the resulting spectral dimension analytically and verifies key results (collapse, probe behavior, eigenvalue-density criteria) in Lean 4 using Mathlib and the Beta distribution of state overlaps.

Result: Demonstrates that a finite sample of random quantum states in high dimension becomes metrically indistinguishable (equidistant dust) due to concentration; diffusion probes on such dust asymptotically yield spectral dimension two—not because spacetime is two-dimensional, but because the probe operates at a single relaxation scale; establishes that only the low-lying Laplacian spectrum (specifically eigenvalue density near zero) determines whether dimension two reflects physical structure ('spectral faithfulness') or measurement artifact.

Conclusion: The recurrent value of two for the spectral dimension at Planckian scales is not necessarily evidence of fundamental two-dimensionality, but may arise generically from geometric properties of quantum state space and limitations of diffusion-based dimensional probes; distinguishing physical dimension from artifact requires verifying spectral faithfulness—i.e., a power-law eigenvalue density near zero—rather than relying on the numerical value alone.

Abstract: Why do unrelated approaches to quantum gravity nearly all find spacetime two-dimensional at the shortest scales? Each theory answers only within its own dynamics; we highlight a single kinematic route to the same value, one assuming no field equation and living in the geometry of the space of states alone. That route is concentration of measure on the Fubini-Study geometry of pure states, which forces the pairwise distances of a random sample to equalize as the dimension grows, so any finite sample collapses to an equidistant dust whose thresholded metric graph is the complete graph. Handed this dust, a diffusion probe reads it as two-dimensional in the large-sample limit, the value the running spectral dimension takes at the dust's single relaxation scale, a property of the measurement rather than the structure; this convergence on two is not, by itself, evidence that spacetime is two-dimensional. Whether a given two is such an artifact is governed by the Laplacian spectrum near zero, and whether that reading carries across an emergence map is the condition we call spectral faithfulness; a single relaxation scale encodes no spectral dimension that tells one structure from another. The collapse, the probe value, and the eigenvalue-density criterion are machine-checked in Lean 4 against Mathlib, resting on the standard Beta law of overlaps; a power-law tail of small eigenvalues reads a genuine dimension, a single scale above a gap reads two at its own clock, and a gapped two-scale band reads off the universal line. These classes are run on graph-Laplacian proxies, and whether a link-graph reading carries to the physical nonlocal operator is left open. The spectral test reads the eigenvalue density near zero and separates, on a given structure, a measurement artifact from a dimension the structure genuinely expresses.

</details>


### [126] [Out-of-Equilibrium Effects in Non-Radial Relativistic Stellar Perturbations: A Model-Agnostic Formulation and Mode Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20957)
*Takuya Katagiri,Victor Guedes,Kent Yagi*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops a general, model-agnostic framework for analyzing non-radial oscillations of relativistic stars with out-of-equilibrium effects (e.g., viscosity, thermal conductivity), extending the Lindblom-Detweiler formalism to both even- and odd-parity perturbations without assuming specific constitutive relations; it applies the framework to the Bemfica-Disconzi-Noronha-Kovtun fluid, computes mode frequency/damping shifts, and identifies new mode families arising from structural features of the eigenvalue problem.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To systematically understand how generic dissipative (non-equilibrium) effects—beyond perfect-fluid idealizations—affect non-radial oscillations of relativistic stars, enabling robust comparisons across different relativistic fluid theories.

Method: Extends the Lindblom-Detweiler formalism to incorporate generic, linearized non-equilibrium corrections in both even- and odd-parity sectors; uses tensorial and thermodynamic decomposition of corrections without assuming constitutive relations; formulates a closed eigenvalue problem for stellar perturbations; applies perturbatively to the Bemfica-Disconzi-Noronha-Kovtun causal fluid model.

Result: Derives the first general framework for linear non-radial relativistic stellar perturbations including arbitrary dissipative corrections; quantifies frequency and damping time shifts relative to perfect-fluid modes; identifies structural conditions in the eigenvalue problem that admit novel mode families beyond standard QNMs.

Conclusion: The framework provides a unified, structural basis for assessing how distinct relativistic fluid theories modify stellar oscillation spectra, enabling principled modeling of dissipation in neutron star asteroseismology and gravitational-wave asteroseismology.

Abstract: We present a systematic, model-agnostic analysis of out-of-equilibrium effects, including viscosity and thermal conductivity, in non-radial oscillations of relativistic stars. Extending the Lindblom-Detweiler formalism, we construct, to our knowledge, the first general framework for linear, non-radial relativistic stellar perturbations that incorporates generic nonequilibrium corrections to the perfect-fluid sector in both the even- and odd-parity channels. Our framework is formulated in terms of the tensorial structure and thermodynamic decomposition of generic corrections without relying on any specific constitutive relations, thereby allowing us to elucidate, at a structural level, how these effects enter the perturbation equations and contribute to geometric deformations and fluid fluctuations. As an application, we consider the Bemfica-Disconzi-Noronha-Kovtun fluid and perturbatively investigate shifts in the frequencies and damping times of modes connected to their perfect-fluid counterparts in the limit of vanishing transport coefficients. We also identify structural features of the closed eigenvalue problem that can give rise to additional mode families. Our formalism provides a unified framework for analyzing how different relativistic fluid theories modify the structure of non-radial stellar perturbations.

</details>


### [127] [Solving Einstein Field Equations on a Digital Quantum Computer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.20975)
*Clelia Altomonte,Malcolm Fairbairn*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper presents a proof-of-principle quantum algorithm for solving Einstein Field Equations within the WEBB tetrad formalism of Numerical Relativity, implemented in Qiskit and tested on classical simulators and IBM quantum hardware to compute Schwarzschild black hole evolution and quasinormal modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore quantum computing’s potential for accelerating computationally intensive simulations in Numerical Relativity—particularly solving partial differential equations like the Einstein Field Equations—which are currently limited by classical computational cost and scalability.

Method: Developed a quantum algorithm tailored to the Wahlquist-Estabrook-Buchman-Bardeen (WEBB) tetrad formalism; discretized and encoded the EFE into a quantum circuit using Qiskit; executed on classical simulators and real IBM quantum processors via the UKRI NQCC Quantum Access program; performed spacetime evolution and quasinormal mode extraction via perturbation.

Result: Demonstrated feasibility of encoding and evolving Schwarzschild spacetime in the WEBB formalism on quantum hardware; quantified qubit count, circuit depth, and runtime; observed fidelity degradation due to noise on physical devices; validated algorithmic correctness on simulators.

Conclusion: Quantum algorithms hold promise for Numerical Relativity but face significant near-term hardware limitations; this work establishes foundational methodology and resource benchmarks for future fault-tolerant implementations.

Abstract: In this work, we show how simulations performed on classical computers such as those of Numerical Relativity can be tackled by quantum algorithms for solving systems of partial differential equations. We develop a proof-of-principle quantum algorithm for solving Einstein Field Equations in the Wahlquist-Estabrook-Buchman-Bardeen(WEBB) tetrad Numerical Relativity formalism [1], and test it by evolving the Schwarzschild Black Hole spacetime in the WEBB Numerical Relativity formalism [2], perturbing it to obtain gravitational Quasinormal Modes [3]. We program the algorithm components for a gate-based, digital quantum computer using the Qiskit software [4] and run it on classical simulators and physical IBM quantum computers through the UKRI National Quantum Computing Centre (NQCC) Quantum Access program and quantify the computational resources and runtime.

</details>


### [128] [Probing the Circular Unruh Effect with Cavity-Controlled Lamb Shifts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21019)
*Yan Peng,Jiawei Hu,Hongwei Yu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes using cavity-modified Lamb shift spectroscopy of a centripetally accelerated atom to detect the Unruh effect at accelerations as low as 0.5 m/s²—20+ orders of magnitude below conventional thresholds—by leveraging cavity QED to amplify and tune noninertial vacuum corrections.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Direct experimental observation of the Unruh effect is hindered by the enormous accelerations (~10²⁰ m/s²) required to reach measurable Unruh temperatures (~1 K); thus, a feasible, low-acceleration detection scheme is needed.

Method: Theoretical analysis of the Lamb shift of an atom undergoing uniform circular motion inside a high-Q optical cavity; calculation of acceleration-dependent vacuum fluctuations modified by cavity boundary conditions and density-of-states engineering.

Result: Cavity-induced modification of the electromagnetic environment enables tunable, observable Lamb shifts (e.g., ~10 Hz) at ultralow accelerations (~0.5 m/s²), with shift magnitude controllable via atomic angular velocity and cavity detuning (enhancement, quenching, or screening).

Conclusion: Cavity-controlled Lamb-shift spectroscopy provides a realistic, tabletop experimental pathway to probe the circular Unruh effect in the ultralow-acceleration regime, circumventing traditional sensitivity barriers.

Abstract: The Unruh effect predicts that accelerated observers perceive the inertial vacuum as populated by particles, providing a flat-spacetime analogue of Hawking radiation. Its direct observation, however, remains experimentally challenging, since an Unruh temperature of $1\,\mathrm{K}$ requires accelerations of order $10^{20}\,\mathrm{m/s^2}$. Here, we show that the Lamb shift of a centripetally accelerated atom inside a high-$Q$ cavity provides a sensitive spectroscopic probe of the Unruh effect at dramatically lower accelerations. The cavity reshapes the electromagnetic density of states and converts otherwise tiny noninertial corrections into tunable level shifts. Depending on the atomic angular velocity and cavity detuning, the Lamb shift can be enhanced, strongly quenched, or completely screened. Remarkably, for experimentally realistic parameters, a rotation-induced shift of order $10\;\mathrm{Hz}$ can arise already at accelerations as low as $0.5\,\mathrm{m/s^2}$, more than twenty orders of magnitude below the acceleration scale conventionally associated with direct Unruh detection. These results identify cavity-controlled Lamb-shift spectroscopy as a viable route toward laboratory tests of the circular Unruh effect in the ultralow-acceleration regime.

</details>


### [129] [An open-source numerical tool for rational orbits and gravitational radiation in static spherically symmetric spacetimes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21029)
*Dan Li,Shiyang Hu,Chen Deng,Shijie Tan,Guansheng He*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a Mathematica-based, OpenMP-parallelized computational framework for simulating rational timelike orbits and associated gravitational radiation in static spherically symmetric spacetimes—requiring only a user-defined metric—and validates it on Schwarzschild geometry; it further estimates detectability of EMRI gravitational waves from an intermediate-mass black hole orbiting Sgr A* with future space-based detectors.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a flexible, efficient, and self-contained numerical tool for studying timelike geodesics and gravitational wave emission in arbitrary static spherically symmetric spacetimes—enabling exploration of strong-field gravity and observational signatures such as EMRI signals.

Method: Developed a parallelized Mathematica package leveraging OpenMP to compute rational (resonant) timelike geodesic orbits, effective potentials, orbital stability, and gravitational wave polarizations (h+, h×) and characteristic strain h_c via the quadrupole–octupole formalism; input is solely the covariant metric tensor.

Result: The code successfully reproduces known Schwarzschild orbital features (e.g., ISCO, stable/unstable rational orbits, frequency spectra) and computes realistic h_c for IMBH–Sgr A* EMRIs; predicts SNR > 5 for LISA-like missions under conservative assumptions.

Conclusion: The framework is robust, generalizable beyond Schwarzschild (e.g., to Reissner–Nordström or exotic metrics), and bridges theoretical geodesic analysis with observational GW forecasting—establishing a reusable pipeline for strong-field tests of gravity.

Abstract: Timelike orbits constitute a crucial probe for exploring the intrinsic properties of curved spacetimes, and the carried gravitational radiation signals provide a direct window into strong field gravity. In this paper, we develop a versatile computational framework based on Mathematica and the OpenMP parallel architecture to simulate the rational orbits of timelike particles and their gravitational radiation in static spherically symmetric spacetimes. Specifically, requiring only the user defined covariant metric, this numerical tool can efficiently calculate rational orbits across various configurations, as well as the corresponding gravitational wave polarization states and characteristic strains. The package presented here offers a highly efficient and comprehensive one-stop solution for investigating the properties of curved spacetimes and their potential observational signatures. To demonstrate the reliability and capability of our code, we apply it to the Schwarzschild spacetime as a test case, illustrating the functionality of the code across several key aspects, including the effective potential, stable orbital regions, rational and irrational orbits, and gravitational wave signals. Furthermore, we show that the gravitational waves emitted by an extreme mass ratio inspiral system composed of an intermediate mass black hole and the Galactic Center supermassive black hole have the potential to be identified by future space detectors.

</details>


### [130] [Population-level correlations in Bayesian statistics: an illustrative model for gravitational-wave astronomy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21076)
*Caroline B. Owen,Alexandre Toubiana,Davide Gerosa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops an analytical Gaussian model to study how single-event parameter correlations and waveform systematics can bias or obscure true population-level correlations in gravitational-wave population inference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how single-event correlations and systematic biases (e.g., from waveform modeling) affect the reliability of inferred population-level correlations—crucial for astrophysics and tests of general relativity.

Method: Constructs an idealized analytical Gaussian model linking single-event likelihoods, catalog-wide systematic biases, and hierarchical population inference; analytically quantifies uncertainty inflation and bias absorption in correlation measurements.

Result: Single-event correlations increase uncertainty in population-level correlation estimates; correlated waveform systematics across events can be degenerate with true population correlations and misinterpreted as such.

Conclusion: Population-level correlation measurements are vulnerable to both statistical degradation from single-event structure and misleading absorption of systematic biases—highlighting the need for careful validation and modeling-aware inference.

Abstract: With increasingly large numbers of gravitational-wave events, population inference is now beginning to move beyond predictions of marginal distributions and to probe correlations between compact-binary parameters such as masses, spins, and redshifts. These correlations have strong constraining power for both astrophysics and tests of general relativity. In this paper, we present an idealized analytical model to study the interplay between single-event correlations, systematic biases, and population-level correlations. With this, we investigate the potential emergence of false-positive measurements of population-level correlations. We quantify how the presence of correlations at the single-event level between a pair of parameters increases the uncertainty of population-level correlations for those parameters, potentially obscuring the true underlying population correlation if present. We also find that if waveform systematics lead to biases that are correlated across the catalog (which is likely, because certain regions of the parameter space are more difficult to model), this can be effectively absorbed by a population analysis that targets correlations and can be misinterpreted as such. This simple Gaussian-based model may serve as a broad compass for future, more detailed explorations.

</details>


### [131] [Reconstructing the slope of a nearly flat quintessence potential from cosmography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21127)
*Saikat Chakraborty,Peter K. S. Dunsby,Robert J. Scherrer*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper revisits thawing quintessence models with nearly flat potentials using cosmography, showing that slow-roll conditions enable reconstructing the potential slope λ from only q and Ω_φ (not requiring jerk j), but DESI DR2 data suggest tension with near-flatness; it also identifies attractor behavior in w–Ω_φ and w–w′ planes, derives the corresponding q–j relation, and notes viable trajectories cluster near the ΛCDM limit j = 1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the viability and observational consistency of thawing quintessence with nearly flat potentials under modern cosmographic constraints (especially post-DESI DR2), and to clarify whether high-order cosmographic parameters (e.g., jerk j) are strictly necessary for reconstructing the potential slope under slow-roll assumptions.

Method: Cosmographic analysis applied to thawing quintessence: derivation of λ = −(dV/dφ)/V under slow-roll conditions [(dV/dφ)/V]² ≪ 1 and |(d²V/dφ²)/V| ≪ 1; phase-space analysis in w–Ω_φ, w–w′, and q–j planes; comparison with DESI DR2 cosmographic data; identification of attractor solutions and ΛCDM limits.

Result: Under slow-roll, λ can be reconstructed from q and Ω_φ alone—no jerk j needed; DESI DR2 data indicate possible tension with the near-flat-potential assumption; clear attractor behavior is found in w–Ω_φ and w–w′ planes, implying universal thawing evolution (w ≈ −1 early); a degenerate q–j relation shows distinct expansion histories can yield identical thawing dynamics; all consistent trajectories lie close to j = 1 (ΛCDM limit).

Conclusion: Slow-roll thawing quintessence with nearly flat potentials is theoretically simplified (reducing required cosmographic order) but faces emerging observational tension with DESI DR2; its dynamical predictability is reinforced by attractor behavior, yet empirical viability remains constrained tightly near ΛCDM in the q–j plane.

Abstract: We revisit thawing quintessence models with nearly flat scalar-field potentials using a cosmographic framework. Earlier work indicates that the cosmographic reconstruction of the slope $λ=-(dV/dφ)/V$ of the quintessence potential in the general case requires the knowledge of the cosmographic paremeters up to the jerk parameter $j$. In this work we show that the slow-roll conditions $[(dV/dφ)/V]^2 \ll 1$ and $|(d^2V/dφ^2)/V| \ll 1$ allow the reconstruction of the slope of a nearly flat potential with knowledge of only the deceleration parameter $q$ (and the density parameter $Ω_φ$). Confronting the assumption of near-flatness with the cosmographic data after DESI DR2, however, reveals possible tension between the two. We further show that these models exhibit attractor behaviour in the $w$--$Ω_φ$ and $w$--$w'$ phase planes, corresponding to a universal thawing evolution with $w \approx -1$ at early times. We also derive the corresponding relation in the cosmographic $q$--$j$ plane and show that different cosmological expansion histories can produce the same thawing evolution. Nevertheless, all viable trajectories remain close to the $Λ$CDM limit $j=1$.

</details>


### [132] [Entanglement and firewalls in quantum circuit model of black hole evaporation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21181)
*Yi-Chen Lu,Tien Hsieh,Da-Shin Lee*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper revisits a quantum circuit model of black hole evaporation with tripartite systems (BH, just radiation, early radiation), introducing a tunable scrambling unitary parameter θ to generalize initial BH qubit states; it analyzes how quantum monogamy governs firewall emergence—showing earlier firewall formation at maximal scrambling (θ = π/2) and demonstrating that firewalls between BH and just radiation arise while preserving information recovery from radiation for Mω across a determined θ range.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To investigate the role of quantum monogamy in firewall formation during black hole evaporation by generalizing the initial black hole state via a tunable scrambling parameter θ in a tripartite quantum circuit model.

Method: Extends the prior quantum circuit model by applying a single-parameter scrambling unitary matrix U(θ) to infalling matter qubits; analyzes entanglement structure, firewall conditions, and information recovery as functions of θ, black hole mass M, and Hawking frequency ω using unitary gate dynamics and monogamy constraints.

Result: Firewall emergence between black hole and just radiation depends on θ, M, and ω; maximal scrambling (θ = π/2) induces earlier firewall formation than no scrambling (θ = 0); a firewall forms for all Mω provided θ lies in an analytically determined range; initial BH qubit state is fully recoverable from final radiation state via unitary evolution.

Conclusion: Quantum monogamy critically governs firewall timing and structure in this evaporation model; tunable scrambling reveals a θ-dependent regime where unitarity, information recovery, and firewall formation coexist consistently—offering new insight into multipartite entanglement dynamics in quantum black holes.

Abstract: We reexamine the quantum circuit model of black hole evaporation proposed in Class. Quantum Grav. 35, 235013 (2018). This model incorporates the tripartite systems: black hole, just radiation and early radiation. We apply the scrambling unitary matrix with a single parameter $θ$ to the ground state of the qubits in infalling matter towards a black hole in order to generate initial qubit states of the black hole that are more general than those in Class. Quantum Grav. 35, 235013 (2018). Specifically, the scrambling unitary matrix reduces to no scrambling and maximum scrambling when $θ=0$ and $θ=π/2$, respectively. Our aim is to explore the role of quantum monogamy in the firewall formation between the black hole and radiation. In this model, entanglement and firewall formation depend on the black hole mass $M$ and the frequency of Hawking radiation $ω$. For the initial state with $θ=π/2$, a firewall emerges at an earlier stage of the evolution than with $θ=0$. We also find that a firewall structure emerges between BH and JR, and that the information is carried away by radiation for all values of $Mω$, provided that $θ$ lies within a certain analytically determined range. Following the unitary gate dynamics, the initial black hole qubit state can be retrieved from its imprint on the final radiation state, which was originally hidden behind the black hole's horizon. These results may provide insight into the properties of multipartite entanglement due to the different initial states in the evolution of a quantum circuit model for black hole evaporation.

</details>


### [133] [Temperature of free gravitational field: A geometrical perspective](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21183)
*Samarjit Chakraborty,Gareth Amery,Sunil D Maharaj,Rituparno Goswami*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a novel geometric framework linking thermodynamic temperature of the free gravitational field to the non-affinity of null geodesics in a Newman–Penrose tetrad, unifying various temperature concepts (Clifton–Ellis–Tavakol, Hawking, Unruh) in appropriate limits; extends results beyond local rotational symmetry to Petrov type D spacetimes (e.g., Kerr); derives hyperbolic wave equations governing temperature transport, sourced by Weyl curvature and matter; and interprets non-affinity as encoding gravitational redshift/blueshift.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To unify disparate notions of gravitational temperature—such as Clifton–Ellis–Tavakol, Hawking, and Unruh temperatures—within a single geometric framework grounded in null geodesic structure and curvature.

Method: Uses a novel geometrical approach based on non-affinity of real null geodesics in a Newman–Penrose tetrad; performs analysis within local rotational symmetry (LRS) class II spacetimes and extends to Petrov type D geometries; derives causal transport equations using geometric optics and curvature decomposition.

Result: Establishes a geometrically defined thermodynamic temperature for the free gravitational field; shows equivalence of known temperature functions in proper limits; obtains hyperbolic wave equation with Weyl- and matter-sourced forcing term; identifies non-affinity parameter as encoding gravitational redshift/blueshift along null rays.

Conclusion: Thermodynamic temperature of the free gravitational field is fundamentally encoded in the non-affine parametrization of null geodesics; this provides a unified, covariant, and physically interpretable geometric origin for gravitational temperature across diverse spacetime classes.

Abstract: In this paper, using a novel geometrical approach, we relate the concept of the thermodynamic temperature of the free gravitational field, to the non-affinity of real null geodesics in a Newman Penrose tetrad. This naturally links various temperature functions like Clifton, Ellis and Tavakol temperature, Hawking temperature, Unruh temperature etc., in their respective proper limits. Although our analysis is done within the realm of local rotational symmetry, we show that the result can be extended to other Petrov type D geometries, like the Kerr spacetime. We also obtain the geometrical and causal transport equations for this temperature function, in the form of a hyperbolic wave equation with a forcing term, sourced by Weyl curvature and matter. Finally, as a possible physical interpretation of the non-affinity, we relate the geometrical temperature with the gravitational red/blue shift of light rays travelling along null geodesics.

</details>


### [134] [Thermal Stability and QNMs of a Hairy Black Hole in the Presence of a Monopole Field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21332)
*George Koutsoumbas,Andri Machattou,Eleftherios Papantonopoulos*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates the thermal and dynamical stability of a generalized GHS-GM black hole coupled to a dilaton and a magnetic monopole field; it finds that decreasing dilaton scalar charge enhances thermal stability, while increasing black hole charge suppresses quasi-normal mode frequencies—both real and imaginary parts—ensuring no instability (no positive imaginary parts), with higher overtones decaying faster than fundamental modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To examine the stability—both thermal and dynamical—of a generalized Garfinkle–Horowitz–Strominger–Gibbons–Maeda (GHS-GM) black hole in the presence of a dilaton and a magnetic monopole field, extending previous analyses to include scalar-charge dependence and quasi-normal mode (QNM) behavior.

Method: Thermodynamic analysis (e.g., heat capacity, temperature–entropy relations) combined with perturbative computation of quasi-normal modes (QNMs) for scalar perturbations, solving the radial wave equation in the black hole background and analyzing the complex frequency spectrum as functions of dilaton scalar charge and black hole charge.

Result: 1) Thermal stability increases monotonically as the dilaton scalar charge decreases; 2) Both real and imaginary parts of QNMs decrease in magnitude with increasing black hole charge; 3) Overtone modes decay faster than fundamental ones; 4) All imaginary parts remain negative—no unstable (exponentially growing) modes observed.

Conclusion: The generalized GHS-GM black hole with dilaton and monopole fields is thermally and dynamically stable across the explored parameter space, with stability tunable via scalar charge and black hole charge.

Abstract: We study the stability of a generalization of the GHS-GM black hole in the presence of a dilaton and a monopole field. We find that the thermal behaviour of system depends on the scalar charge of the dilaton field and as this parameter is decreasing the system becomes more thermally stable. We also find that, as the charge of the black hole is increasing, both the real and the imaginary parts of the quasi-normal frequencies decrease in absolute value. The overtone modes die out faster than the fundamental modes and no positive imaginary parts appear, indicating the stabilty of the system

</details>


### [135] [Relativistic effects in extreme-mass-ratio inspirals within scalar clouds: Eccentric and inclined orbits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21439)
*Qi-Xuan Xu,Richard Brito,Riccardo Della Monica,Rodrigo Vicente,Chen Yuan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends relativistic modeling of extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) in scalar clouds—formed via superradiance—to generic (eccentric, inclined) orbits around a Schwarzschild black hole, revealing resonance structures driven by relativistic apsidal precession and inclination-dependent energy transfer, including a critical inclination where the scalar cloud energizes the orbit.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To generalize previous relativistic EMRI studies in scalar clouds—which were limited to circular, equatorial orbits—to fully generic (eccentric and inclined) trajectories, enabling more realistic gravitational-wave waveform modeling for LISA and probing scalar-field–black-hole interactions.

Method: Uses a fully relativistic perturbative framework on a Schwarzschild background to compute scalar energy and angular momentum fluxes scattered to infinity and absorbed at the horizon, incorporating orbital eccentricity and inclination; analyzes resonant features via relativistic apsidal precession and identifies critical inclination effects.

Result: Eccentricity induces dense resonance spectra near the last stable orbit due to relativistic apsidal precession; inclination strongly modifies energy/angular momentum losses, with a critical inclination below which energy is transferred *from* the scalar cloud *to* the orbit at small radii; resonances persist across all inclinations for high eccentricity but differ markedly between prograde and retrograde cases.

Conclusion: Generic orbital configurations introduce rich dynamical features in EMRIs embedded in scalar clouds, necessitating inclusion of eccentricity and inclination for accurate waveform modeling and scalar-field parameter estimation; this work lays the groundwork for extension to Kerr black holes.

Abstract: We study extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs) evolving in a scalar cloud environment that may form through superradiant instabilities, using a fully relativistic perturbative framework that allows for eccentric and inclined orbits. EMRIs, consisting of a stellar-mass compact object inspiraling into a supermassive black hole, are key sources for space-based gravitational-wave detectors such as LISA. Previous relativistic studies of EMRIs in scalar clouds have been restricted to circular, equatorial motion. Here, instead, we focus on a Schwarzschild black hole background to incorporate eccentricity and orbital inclination. By computing the scalar energy and angular momentum scattered off to spatial infinity and absorbed at the event horizon, we show that orbital eccentricity can induce a dense spectrum of resonances near the last stable orbit, associated with strong relativistic apsidal precession. We further find that orbital inclination can significantly modify the orbital energy and angular momentum losses. In particular, we identify a critical inclination angle below which, at sufficiently small orbital radii, there is a net transfer of energy from the scalar cloud to the orbit. Moreover, for sufficiently large eccentricities, resonances associated with relativistic apsidal precession persist across the full range of inclinations, although their structure changes significantly between prograde and retrograde orbits. These results provide a foundation for future studies of EMRIs in scalar cloud environments on fully generic orbits around spinning black holes.

</details>


### [136] [The WDM Time-Frequency Transform in Gravitational-Wave Data Analysis I: Formalism](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21473)
*Aaron Johnson,Katerina Chatziioannou,Jake Summers*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper provides a pedagogical, self-contained introduction to time-frequency methods—specifically the Wilson-Daubechies-Meyer (WDM) basis—for gravitational-wave data analysis, emphasizing practical implementation, basis properties, noise modeling, and likelihood formulation in the time-frequency domain.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To bridge the knowledge gap in gravitational-wave literature by systematically documenting time-frequency methods—particularly the WDM basis—which offer a computationally efficient and physically intuitive alternative to pure frequency- or time-domain approaches for slowly-varying non-stationary noise.

Method: Derives the Wilson-Daubechies-Meyer (WDM) time-frequency basis from first principles; details orthogonality, time-frequency localization, forward/inverse transforms, edge handling at DC/Nyquist, and constructs the noise covariance matrix and likelihood function in the WDM domain.

Result: A complete, implementable framework for time-frequency analysis using the WDM basis, including practical insights on numerical implementation, mitigation of spectral edge effects, and formulation of the likelihood for gravitational-wave inference under non-stationary noise.

Conclusion: Time-frequency methods—especially the WDM basis—are viable, well-founded tools for gravitational-wave analysis of slowly-varying noise; this work serves as both a theoretical foundation and a practical guide for adoption in real analyses.

Abstract: For slowly-varying noise, time-frequency methods offer a natural middle ground between the efficiency of the frequency domain and the generality of the more expensive time domain. Despite growing adoption, such methods remain less well documented and less familiar in the gravitational-wave literature, compared to the ubiquitous frequency domain. Aimed at gravitational-wave analysts, in this paper we present a self-contained account of time-frequency methods, detail derivations for a specific basis, namely the Wilson-Daubechies-Meyer (WDM) basis, and share intuition and lessons learned. We document key concepts: the orthogonality and good time-frequency localization of the basis, the edge effects at the DC and Nyquist frequencies, the forward and inverse transforms and their practical implementation, and the noise covariance matrix and likelihood in the time-frequency domain.

</details>


### [137] [Binary Black Hole Coalescence and the Dynamics of Scalar Hair in Einstein-Maxwell-Scalar Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21549)
*Jean Pierre Díaz,P. A. González,Eleftherios Papantonopoulos,Yerko Vásquez*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper numerically studies head-on collisions of charged black holes in Einstein-Maxwell-Scalar theory, showing that scalar hair can dynamically emerge post-merger due to nonminimal electromagnetic-scalar coupling — with final scalarization or descalarization determined by coupling strength and remnant charge.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how scalar hair forms dynamically during binary black hole coalescence in Einstein-Maxwell-Scalar (EMS) theory, especially when initial black holes are scalar-free but embedded in a scalar perturbation.

Method: Numerical relativity simulations of head-on collisions using charged puncture initial data (Reissner-Nordström black holes) with a purely kinetic scalar field seed; full evolution of coupled gravitational, electromagnetic, and scalar fields; monitoring of apparent horizons, radiation, and horizon scalar profiles.

Result: Nonminimal coupling triggers scalar hair growth post-merger even from scalar-free initial black holes; weak coupling or charge cancellation leads to descalarization (radiation/absorption), while strong coupling + nonzero remnant charge yields persistent scalarized final black holes; scalar radiation waveform correlates qualitatively with dominant gravitational-wave mode in nonlinear merger phase.

Conclusion: Scalarization/descalarization transitions in EMS theory are realized dynamically in binary coalescence, and the fate of scalar hair is governed by the interplay between scalar coupling strength and the electric charge retained by the merged black hole.

Abstract: We investigate the head-on coalescence of charged binary black holes in Einstein-Maxwell-Scalar (EMS) theory using numerical relativity. The binaries are built from charged puncture initial data representing two Reissner-Nordström black holes immersed in a purely kinetic scalar perturbation: the scalar field initially vanishes, while its conjugate momentum provides a small seed for the instability. We evolve the coupled gravitational, electromagnetic, and scalar sectors and monitor the apparent horizons, the emitted radiation, and the scalar field on the horizons. Our simulations show that the nonminimal electromagnetic-scalar coupling can dynamically trigger the growth of scalar hair even when the individual black holes are initially scalar-free. The subsequent evolution depends on the coupling strength and on the charge retained by the remnant. For weak coupling, or when charge cancellation suppresses the electromagnetic source after merger, the scalar field is radiated away or absorbed by the final horizon and the system dynamically descalarizes. For sufficiently strong coupling and nonzero remnant charge, the scalar field remains finite and the final black hole approaches a scalarized configuration. The coalescence also excites scalar radiation whose time profile is qualitatively correlated with the dominant gravitational-wave mode during the nonlinear stage of the collision. These results provide a binary realization of scalarization/descalarization transitions in EMS theory and show that the fate of scalar hair is controlled by the interplay between the scalar coupling and the charge content of the remnant.

</details>


### [138] [Consistency Conditions and the Derivation of Harmonic Structure in Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21603)
*Bardia H. Fahim,Joshua G. Fenwick*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper derives the harmonic and conformal structure of Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton solutions from first principles, showing that generic solutions require equal dilaton couplings, Ricci-flat spatial base, and harmonic metric functions—unifying features across multi-center, cosmological, and black hole geometries.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the common harmonic and conformal structure observed in exact solutions of Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton theory—especially across diverse solution classes—and determine whether it arises necessarily from field equations rather than being imposed by ansatz.

Method: Analyzes the full Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton field equations in arbitrary dimensions with non-trivial dilaton-Maxwell coupling and Liouville-type potential; imposes no prior assumptions on base geometry or harmonicity; classifies solutions into generic and purely spatial branches via systematic constraint analysis.

Result: For the generic branch: field equations force equality of dilaton-Maxwell and dilaton-potential couplings, enforce Ricci-flatness of the spatial base, and imply harmonicity of the metric function on that base—leading to a conformal potential representation. The purely spatial branch yields an alternative coupling constraint.

Conclusion: The harmonic and conformal structures ubiquitous in exact solutions are not ad hoc but are direct, necessary consequences of the field equations under generic conditions—providing a unified geometric origin for multi-center, cosmological, and dynamical black hole solutions.

Abstract: Many exact solutions of Einstein-Maxwell and Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton theory share a common structural pattern in which the metric functions are built from harmonic functions on a specified spatial base, often taken to be flat. We investigate this pattern by considering the Einstein-Maxwell-dilaton theory in arbitrary dimensions, where the dilaton field is non-trivially coupled to the Maxwell field and to a Liouville-type potential proportional to the cosmological parameter. Without imposing either the base geometry or the harmonic form of the metric function in advance, we show that for the generic branch, the field equations force the dilaton couplings to be equal, restrict the spatial base geometry to be Ricci flat, and make the metric function harmonic on this base. The resulting spacetime can then be written in terms of a conformal potential. We also consider a purely spatial branch, which instead leads to a distinct constraint on the coupling constants. These results provide a unified field-equation derivation of the harmonic behavior and conformal structure that appear in several classes of solutions, including multi-center geometries, cosmological solutions, and dynamical black holes.

</details>


### [139] [Mirror Symmetry and Double Signature Change](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21805)
*Tevian Dray,Charles Hellaby*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper reexamines the black mirror spacetime—a model connecting two Schwarzschild exteriors without an interior—using double signature change techniques, confirming absence of distributional curvature singularities (i.e., no impulsive gravitational wave layer) at the horizon, and clarifying its global structure and causal behavior of worldlines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve inconsistencies in prior claims about curvature analyticity and to determine whether the black mirror spacetime contains a surface layer (distributional curvature singularity) at the horizon, interpreted as an impulsive gravitational wave.

Method: Adaptation of signature-change formalism to model the black mirror spacetime as a double signature-change geometry; rigorous distributional analysis of curvature components and examination of junction conditions across the horizon.

Result: No distributional curvature singularity exists at the horizon; curvature components are regular (non-distributional), contradicting earlier suggestions of impulsive wave layers; global structure is shown to be causally well-behaved with smooth passage of timelike and null geodesics across the horizon.

Conclusion: The black mirror spacetime is geometrically regular at the horizon—free of impulsive gravitational waves or delta-function curvature contributions—and constitutes a consistent, albeit exotic, signature-changing extension of Schwarzschild geometry.

Abstract: The black mirror spacetime proposed by Tzanavaris, Boyle, and Turok [1] connects the two exterior regions of the extended Schwarzschild black hole directly to each other, with no intervening interior region. Using techniques adapted from previous work on signature change, we reexamine the black mirror spacetime as a model of double signature change, and investigate whether there is a surface layer at the horizon, that is, a distributional curvature singularity corresponding to an impulsive gravitational wave. We confirm that the black mirror spacetime does not contain any such singularity, and compare our result with previous claims that the curvature components are analytic. We also discuss the global structure of the black mirror spacetime, and examine what happens to worldlines and curves passing through.

</details>


### [140] [Quantum Memory in Scalar-Induced Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21835)
*Waqas Ahmed*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper demonstrates that the standard classical stochastic treatment of scalar-induced gravitational waves overlooks residual quantum information—specifically, anomalous coherence in the scalar field—and derives how this coherence manifests as nonclassical signatures (e.g., tensor discord and connected power covariance) in the induced gravitational-wave background.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To uncover whether residual quantum coherence in decohered scalar perturbations survives and leaves observable imprints in secondary gravitational-wave backgrounds—beyond the standard classical stochastic description.
