Method: Starting from a decohered two-mode Gaussian scalar state, the authors derive exact transfer relations linking scalar anomalous coherence to the covariance matrix of induced tensor (gravitational-wave) modes; they analyze spectral localization, distinguish ordinary power from opposite-mode coherence contributions, and compute tensor discord and connected power covariance.

Result: Scalar anomalous coherence sources opposite-mode tensor coherence—not captured by standard power-spectrum calculations—leading to nonzero tensor quantum discord and connected, phase-sensitive tensor-power covariance even after scalar entanglement is lost.

Conclusion: Primordial quantum coherence in scalar perturbations can imprint detectable nonclassical signatures (discord, connected covariance, phase-sensitive strain correlations) in induced gravitational-wave backgrounds, offering novel probes for space-based interferometers and pulsar timing arrays.

Abstract: Scalar-induced gravitational waves are usually treated as a classical stochastic background sourced by phase-random curvature perturbations. We show that this description can miss residual quantum information. Starting from a decohered two-mode Gaussian scalar state, we derive explicit transfer relations between the scalar anomalous coherence and the covariance matrix of induced tensor modes. For a localized scalar power spectrum, the ordinary tensor power is sourced by scalar power contractions, whereas the opposite-mode tensor coherence is sourced by scalar anomalous-coherence contractions. This coherence can generate nonzero tensor discord and a connected tensor-power covariance even after scalar entanglement has vanished. We identify the connected covariance and phase-sensitive strain correlations as probes of primordial quantum coherence in secondary gravitational-wave backgrounds, and discuss their possible relevance for future space-based interferometers and pulsar timing arrays.

</details>


### [141] [Observational Constraints on $f(Q,T)$ Gravity in the Presence of DBI-Essence Scalar Field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21839)
*Mayur Mune,Praveen Kumar Dhankar,Goutam Manna,Safiqul Islam,Bidisha Samanta,Behnam Pourhassan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores late-time cosmology in f(Q,T) gravity coupled to a DBI scalar field, derives analytic background solutions for linear f(Q,T), and constrains parameters using H(z), BAO, and SNe Ia data—showing statistical viability as an alternative to ΛCDM and potential relevance for H₀ tension.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a viable alternative to ΛCDM that addresses observational tensions—particularly the H₀ discrepancy—by extending symmetric teleparallel gravity with a Dirac-Born-Infeld scalar field and non-minimal Q–T coupling.

Method: Derives modified Friedmann equations in f(Q,T)+DBI gravity on a flat FLRW background; obtains analytic solutions for f(Q,T) = αQ + βT; performs Markov Chain Monte Carlo parameter estimation using Hubble parameter (H(z)), DESI BAO (DR2), and Pantheon+SH0ES supernova data.

Result: Joint constraints on α, β, and DBI parameters are consistent with current late-time observations; model shows statistically comparable fit to ΛCDM but with quantifiable departures due to βT coupling and DBI dynamics; does not fully resolve all tensions but remains viable.

Conclusion: f(Q,T)+DBI gravity constitutes a statistically acceptable extension of GR that offers a flexible framework for probing late-time cosmic acceleration and tension-mitigating mechanisms—though it does not definitively solve the H₀ problem.

Abstract: We investigate late-time cosmology in extended symmetric teleparallel gravity coupled to a Dirac-Born-Infeld (DBI) scalar field within $f(Q,T)$ gravity, where $Q$ is the non-metricity scalar and $T$ is the trace of the matter energy-momentum tensor. Working on a spatially flat Friedmann-Lemaître-Robertson-Walker background and treating the cosmic medium as an effective perfect fluid, we derive the background field equations for $f(Q,T)+\mathrm{DBI}$ gravity and obtain analytic solutions for the linear choice $f(Q,T)=αQ+βT$. We then constrain the model parameters with a Markov Chain Monte Carlo analysis using Hubble-rate data, DESI BAO (DR2) measurements, and the Pantheon+SHOES Type~Ia supernova sample. The joint posteriors (Tables II and III) are broadly consistent with current late-time constraints and allow a direct comparison with $Λ$CDM, quantifying the departures driven by the $βT$ coupling and the DBI sector. Although the model does not reproduce every observational feature exactly, it provides a statistically viable alternative avenue to the standard paradigm and a useful framework for exploring potential remedies to existing tensions, including the $H_0$ discrepancy, without claiming a definitive resolution.

</details>


### [142] [Entanglement, Discord, and Residual Coherence in Scalar-Induced Gravitational Waves](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21901)
*Waqas Ahmed*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores whether quantum coherence in primordial scalar perturbations—beyond entanglement—can survive decoherence and imprint detectable non-Gaussian, phase-sensitive correlations in scalar-induced gravitational waves (GWs), using covariance-matrix formalism for decohered two-mode squeezed states.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether residual quantum-informational features (e.g., anomalous scalar coherence) in the primordial curvature perturbations persist after decoherence and manifest in induced gravitational-wave observables beyond classical stochasticity.

Method: Models primordial curvature perturbations as decohered two-mode squeezed Gaussian states via covariance-matrix formalism; derives scalar-to-tensor transfer relations for opposite-momentum tensor modes; distinguishes ordinary tensor power (from scalar power contractions) from tensor coherence (from anomalous scalar-coherence contractions); computes induced Gaussian discord and connected, phase-sensitive observables like connected power covariance κ(k).

Result: Anomalous scalar coherence survives decoherence and sources opposite-momentum tensor coherence, generating Gaussian discord and connected, phase-sensitive observables—most notably a nontrivial covariance structure κ(k) ∝ |γₖ|²/αₖ²—not captured by standard stochastic GW spectra.

Conclusion: Scalar-induced gravitational waves can encode robust signatures of primordial quantum correlations beyond entanglement, offering a novel probe of early-universe quantum information via correlated, phase-structured tensor backgrounds rather than spectral shifts.

Abstract: Scalar-induced gravitational waves are usually modeled as a classical stochastic background sourced by primordial curvature perturbations. We investigate whether residual quantum-information properties of the scalar sector can survive decoherence and leave imprints in the induced tensor background. Using the covariance-matrix formalism, we describe primordial curvature perturbations as decohered two-mode squeezed Gaussian states and identify the anomalous scalar coherence that may remain after scalar entanglement has vanished. We then derive the leading scalar-to-tensor transfer relations for opposite-momentum induced tensor modes. The ordinary tensor power is sourced by scalar power contractions, whereas the opposite-mode tensor coherence is sourced by anomalous scalar-coherence contractions. This tensor coherence controls the induced Gaussian discord and generates connected and phase-sensitive observables, including a connected power covariance $κ(k)\propto |γ_k|^2/α_k^2$. Thus the robust signature is not a universal shift of the gravitational-wave spectrum, but a correlated tensor background with nontrivial covariance and phase structure. We discuss phenomenological templates and provide an illustrative Fisher estimate for future gravitational-wave observations. Our results suggest that scalar-induced gravitational waves may offer a new probe of primordial quantum correlations beyond entanglement.

</details>


### [143] [Determining Kerr black hole spin and inclination from a segment of the critical curve in black hole images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.21921)
*Kenta Hioki,Umpei Miyamoto,Tomohiro Harada*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a method to infer Kerr black hole spin (a/M) and inclination (i) from partial, observable segments of the critical curve in black hole images—leveraging geometric features of photon ring accumulation—without requiring full critical curve reconstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The critical curve is not directly observable, but localized brightness enhancements from higher-order photon rings approximate segments of it; thus, extracting physical parameters from such segments would enable spin and inclination estimation in realistic EHT-like observations.

Method: Introduces standardized critical curve segments and defines three geometric observables characterizing their shape; analytically demonstrates injectivity of the mapping from (a/M, i, r_nl) to these observables in the non-extremal Kerr regime.

Result: The three observables uniquely determine the black hole spin a/M, inclination i, and auxiliary radial parameter r_nl ∈ [0,1] specifying segment location along the critical curve.

Conclusion: Even incomplete, localized observations of photon ring structure contain sufficient information to constrain fundamental black hole parameters—enabling robust, model-based inference without full image reconstruction or assumption of axisymmetry beyond Kerr.

Abstract: We present a method for determining the spin parameter $a/M$ and inclination angle $i$ of a non-extremal Kerr black hole from segments of the critical curve identified in black hole images. Although the critical curve itself is not directly observable, higher-order photon rings accumulate near it, and in realistic observations localized portions of the resulting brightness enhancement may be available for identifying segments of the critical curve. We introduce standardized segments of the critical curve and define three observables that characterize their geometry. We show that these observables uniquely determine $(a/M,i)$, together with an auxiliary parameter $r_{nl}\in[0,1]$ specifying the location of the identified segment along the critical curve, within the domain considered. Thus, even a segment of the critical curve contains sufficient geometric information to constrain the black hole spin and inclination without reconstructing the full critical curve. The framework is naturally suited to realistic observations and may be extended to more general rotating black hole spacetimes.

</details>


### [144] [Gravitational Radiation and Charges on de Sitter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22060)
*Emine Şeyma Kutluk*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper summarizes work on defining Bondi mass loss in Λ-BMS spacetimes by analyzing linearized Einstein equations in generalized harmonic gauge, linking multipole moments to memory effects and charge flows via the holographic stress tensor.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to resolve the nontrivial problem of defining an analogue of Bondi mass loss in asymptotically de Sitter (Λ > 0) or anti-de Sitter (Λ < 0) spacetimes — i.e., in the Λ-BMS solution space — where standard null infinity structures break down.

Method: The authors study linearized Einstein equations in generalized harmonic gauge under assumptions of adiabaticity and large-distance approximation; they express metric perturbations in terms of source multipole moments, consistently truncate the multipole expansion, compute net changes in perturbations at future null infinity linked to gravitational memory, and define charge flows using the holographic stress tensor at second order in perturbation theory.

Result: They obtain a well-defined expression for the net change of metric perturbations at future infinity tied to time-varying sources (memory effect), and derive explicit forms of charge flows in terms of multipole moments at second order, enabling a physical interpretation of energy-momentum and angular momentum fluxes in Λ-BMS contexts.

Conclusion: The framework successfully generalizes Bondi-type conservation laws and memory effects to Λ-BMS spacetimes, bridging asymptotic symmetries, holography, and observable gravitational-wave signatures even in cosmologically relevant (Λ ≠ 0) settings.

Abstract: We give a summary of our work [3] and [4] with Geoffrey Compère and Sk.Jahanur Hoque. After quickly reviewing the $Λ$-BMS solution space and why it is not trivial to define an analogue of Bondi mass loss, we discuss how this can be resolved by studying the linearized solutions. Then we describe how to solve the linearized Einstein s equations in the generalized harmonic gauge and the set of assumptions such as adiabaticity and being at a large distance from the source that allows us to write the metric perturbations in terms of the source multipole moments. We point out how to consistently truncate the multipole expansion. We then discuss the net change of the metric perturbation at future infinity due to a source that is time-varying in a finite time interval that will be connected to the memory effect. We explain how to define charge flows using the holographic stress tensor and what form they take in terms of multipole moments at second order in perturbations. We conclude with a some remarks on the results.

</details>


### [145] [Extended spherically symmetric solutions in revised Deser--Woodard nonlocal gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22086)
*Haida Li,Xiangdong Zhang*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends static spherically symmetric black hole solutions in revised Deser–Woodard nonlocal gravity, showing linear first-order deviations from Schwarzschild geometry and analyzing two physically motivated asymptotically flat corrections: logarithmically dressed (slowly decaying, extended) and exponentially suppressed (localized near horizon, shifts horizon inward).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore physically relevant nonlocal corrections to Schwarzschild black holes in revised Deser–Woodard gravity, motivated by quantum corrections (logarithmic terms) and finite-range/screened gravitational effects (exponential terms).

Method: Leverages linearity of field equations to perform first-order perturbative expansion around Schwarzschild solution; constructs and analyzes two asymptotically flat correction ansätze—logarithmically dressed and exponentially suppressed—within the nonlocal gravity framework.

Result: First-order metric deviations are linear in temporal component and reciprocal radial component; logarithmic correction yields slow large-radius decay (extended nonlocality), while exponential correction localizes near horizon and shifts horizon inward for positive amplitude.

Conclusion: Nonlocal gravity admits physically distinct classes of black hole corrections—extended vs. localized—with implications for observational signatures and theoretical consistency of modified gravity models.

Abstract: In this work, we extend the static spherically symmetric black hole solutions of revised Deser--Woodard (D-W) nonlocal gravity. Due to the linearity of the field equations, we show that the first-order expansion around the Schwarzschild solution is linear in both the temporal metric component and the reciprocal of the radial metric component. Therefore, a mode-by-mode superposition can be properly defined for different types of correction terms. We then further study two additional asymptotically flat extensions, a logarithmically dressed correction and an exponentially suppressed correction. The logarithmically dressed correction gives a slowly decaying deviation from Schwarzschild as the radius becomes large, making it a more extended nonlocal effect, whereas the exponentially suppressed correction is localized near the horizon and shifts the horizon inward for positive correction amplitude. These correction terms are physically motivated by common mechanisms in modified gravity: Logarithmic terms are possibly related to effects of quantum corrections, and exponential terms can arise from finite-range or screened gravitational effects.

</details>


### [146] [Horizon Singularities in the Schwarzschild Geometry of the Teleparallel Equivalent of General Relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22121)
*D. F. López,A. A. Coley,R. J. van den Hoogen*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper resolves horizon singularities in Schwarzschild TEGR by classifying tetrad–spin connection (Lorentz sector) configurations: one subclass yields finite torsion scalars and a regular, extendible horizon; the other retains divergences and excludes the horizon from the manifold. Inertial effects are shown not to cause the singularities, and three complementary criteria identify all regular Lorentz sector choices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve apparent torsion scalar divergences at the Schwarzschild horizon in TEGR—which threaten its black hole interpretation—by examining the underdetermined Lorentz sector (tetrad and spin connection) beyond the metric.

Method: Classification of Schwarzschild TEGR geometries via Lorentz transformations of the tetrad and spin connection; application of three independent regularity criteria: inertial frame condition, horizon-penetrating coordinates (e.g., Eddington–Finkelstein), and horizon regularity criterion for torsion scalars; analysis of inertial contributions and their decoupling from horizon singularities.

Result: Two disjoint subclasses identified: (i) regular subclass—torsion scalars finite at r = 2M, analytic extension across horizon possible; (ii) singular subclass—genuine curvature-type divergences, horizon excised. Full characterization of Lorentz sector functions ensuring regularity is provided.

Conclusion: Horizon singularities in Schwarzschild TEGR are gauge artifacts tied to improper Lorentz sector choices, not intrinsic to the spacetime; a physically admissible black hole interpretation requires selecting the regular subclass, affirming TEGR’s consistency with black hole physics when the full teleparallel geometry (not just the metric) is properly specified.

Abstract: Certain torsion scalar invariants are known to diverge at the horizon of the Schwarzschild solution in the Teleparallel Equivalent of General Relativity (TEGR), obstructing its interpretation as a black hole spacetime. We show that Schwarzschild TEGR geometries split into two distinct subclasses determined by the Lorentz sector of the geometry -- the elements of the tetrad and spin connection not encoded in the metric but appearing in the torsion. In the regular subclass, the divergences are absent and the horizon belongs to the manifold, supporting a consistent black hole interpretation. In the singular subclass, the divergences are genuine and the horizon is excluded from the manifold. As part of this analysis, we clarify the role of inertial contributions in teleparallel gravity, showing that the proper frame does not, by itself, eliminate inertial effects and that the horizon singularities are independent of the inertial structure of the frame. Using three independent approaches -- the inertial frame condition, horizon-penetrating coordinates, and the horizon regularity criterion -- we determine the complete class of Lorentz sector functions compatible with a regular horizon in Schwarzschild TEGR geometries. For this class, all torsion scalar invariants remain finite at the horizon and analytic extensions across it are admitted.

</details>


### [147] [Covariant virtual work and the d'Alembert-Lagrange formulation of general relativity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22196)
*A. Cuevas,C. Ortiz*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces a covariant virtual work framework for spacetime geometry, reformulating General Relativity via a d'Alembert-Lagrange principle—where Einstein’s equations follow from vanishing total covariant virtual work on metric variations, not action extremization; ideal constraints (e.g., isoperimetric) yield cosmological constants as global, nonlocal geometric parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To provide a geometrically intrinsic, covariant mechanical foundation for gravity that replaces the standard action-principle paradigm with a virtual-work-based formulation, clarifying the origin of constraints and the cosmological constant.

Method: Development of a covariant virtual work structure and d'Alembert-Lagrange principle for Lorentzian geometry; classification of constraint reactions (ideal vs. non-ideal) under admissible metric variations; extension to one-sided (inequality-type) constraints; derivation of field equations from vanishing total covariant virtual work.

Result: Einstein field equations emerge as equilibrium conditions (zero total covariant virtual work), not Euler–Lagrange equations; cosmological constant arises as an ideal reaction from an isoperimetric constraint, fixed by global spacetime averages—hence determined nonlocally by admissibility conditions.

Conclusion: General Relativity admits a fully covariant mechanical interpretation rooted in virtual work and constraint mechanics, offering a novel, non-action-based foundation where the cosmological constant acquires a geometric, global, and constraint-driven origin.

Abstract: We develop a covariant virtual work structure and a corresponding d'Alembert-Lagrange principle for spacetime geometry. Within this framework, General Relativity arises as a particular realization of the principle, leading to a covariant d'Alembert-Lagrange formulation in which the Einstein field equations arise from the vanishing of total covariant virtual work on admissible metric variations, rather than from action extremality. The covariant virtual work structure provides a covariant classification of constraint-induced contributions, distinguishing ideal reactions, which perform no virtual work, from non-ideal sectors contribute explicitly to it. The structure extends naturally to one-sided admissibility conditions, yielding a covariant inequality structure. Constraints generate reaction terms. In particular, an isoperimetric constraint produces a cosmological term as an ideal reaction fixed by spacetime averages, so that the cosmological constant emerges as a global parameter determined by admissibility, reflecting an intrinsically nonlocal geometric origin.

</details>


### [148] [Non-Metricity Corrections Approach to Alleviate $H _0$ Tension: The Logarithmic and Nonlinear $f(Q)$ Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22262)
*Rahul Bhagat,Kesava Chodavarapu,B. Mishra*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates whether symmetric teleparallel $f(Q)$ gravity—specifically logarithmic and nonlinear saturation models—can alleviate the $H_0$ tension without Λ, finding the logarithmic model yields intermediate $H_0$ values and better information criteria than ΛCDM.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address the persistent $H_0$ tension between early- and late-universe measurements by exploring geometric modifications to gravity within symmetric teleparallel (i.e., non-metricity-based) frameworks, avoiding dark energy parametrization.

Method: Bayesian MCMC analysis of two $f(Q)$ gravity models (logarithmic and nonlinear saturation) constrained jointly by cosmic chronometers, multiple Type Ia supernova datasets (Pantheon, Pantheon+SH0ES, DES SN5YR), and BAO (SDSS, DESI), with model comparison via AIC/BIC.

Result: Both $f(Q)$ models are statistically competitive with ΛCDM; the logarithmic model consistently yields $H_0$ values intermediate between Planck (67.4 km/s/Mpc) and SH0ES (73.2 km/s/Mpc) across all data combinations and shows lower AIC/BIC penalties.

Conclusion: Logarithmic $f(Q)$ gravity provides a viable, Λ-free modified-gravity solution that partially alleviates the $H_0$ tension, making it a promising alternative to ΛCDM within the symmetric teleparallel paradigm.

Abstract: The persistent discrepancy between early-time and late-Universe measurements of the Hubble constant commonly known as the $H_0$ tension remains one of the most pressing open questions in modern cosmology. In this work, we explore whether modifications to the gravitational sector, specifically within the framework of symmetric teleparallel gravity, can offer a viable pathway toward alleviating this tension. We consider two functional forms of $f(Q)$ gravity: a logarithmic model and a nonlinear saturation model, both of which introduce geometric corrections to the standard expansion history without invoking a cosmological constant. Constraining these models through a Bayesian MCMC analysis against a comprehensive suite of observational data, including cosmic chronometers, Type Ia supernova compilations (Pantheon, Pantheon$+$SH0ES, and DES SN5YR), and BAO measurements from SDSS and DESI, we find that both models remain statistically competitive with $Λ$CDM. The logarithmic model, in particular, consistently infers intermediate values of $H_0$ between the \textit{Planck} and SH0ES benchmarks across all dataset combinations, and carries lower AIC and BIC penalties, establishing it as the more promising candidate for partially easing the $H_0$ tension within a modified gravity framework.

</details>


### [149] [Null geodesic defocusing in dynamical black-hole-to-white-hole transitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22404)
*Johanna Borissova,Stefano Liberati,Matt Visser*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proves that dynamical black-hole-to-white-hole transitions require violation of the null convergence condition—regardless of underlying gravity theory—and constructs explicit bouncing spacetimes to illustrate this, showing the violation is localized in the transition phase; instantaneous transitions would demand unbounded violation, implying breakdown of classical geometry.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand necessary geometric conditions for non-singular black-hole-to-white-hole transitions, independent of specific quantum gravity models or field equations.

Method: Kinematic analysis of null geodesic expansions across trapping/anti-trapping horizons; construction of time-dependent bouncing geometries in generalized Painlevé–Gullstrand coordinates using regular black hole mass functions (e.g., Bardeen-type).

Result: Violation of the null convergence condition is necessary and localized during the dynamical transition phase; instantaneous transitions imply unbounded violation, signaling failure of semiclassical spacetime description.

Conclusion: Singularity resolution via black-hole-to-white-hole transitions generically requires energy condition violation in the null sector—a robust, model-independent feature of the spacetime kinematics.

Abstract: We investigate the defocusing of null geodesics in dynamical, non-singular black-hole-to-white-hole transitions. Working at the level of spacetime kinematics, and without assuming any specific gravitational field equations, we show that the contraction and disappearance of a trapped region, as well as the subsequent formation and expansion of an anti-trapped region, necessarily require a violation of the null convergence condition. This conclusion follows directly from the behaviour of the null expansions across the trapping and anti-trapping horizons, and is therefore independent of the microscopic mechanism responsible for singularity resolution. We then illustrate this general argument by constructing a class of explicit bouncing geometries in generalised Painlevé-Gullstrand coordinates, obtained by promoting static regular black holes with de Sitter cores to time-dependent black-hole-to-white-hole transition models. For a Bardeen-type mass function, we show that the required violation of the null convergence condition is localised within the intermediate dynamical phase in which the trapped region evaporates and the anti-trapped region forms. Finally, we argue that the limiting case of an instantaneous black-hole-to-white-hole transition would require an unbounded violation of the null convergence condition, signalling a breakdown of the effective continuum metric description, and the need to appeal to a full quantum-gravitational description.

</details>


### [150] [Starobinsky-inflation in asymptotically safe shift-symmetric scalar-tensor theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22408)
*Benjamin Koch,Cristobal Laporte,Frank Saueressig*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper explores how scalaron-driven inflation constrains asymptotically safe scalar-tensor theories, showing that the non-minimal gravity-matter coupling parameter influences both matter and gravitational higher-derivative terms, and uses cosmological data to rule out two of three non-Gaussian fixed points while identifying viable RG trajectories from the third.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To test the phenomenological viability of asymptotically safe scalar-tensor theories by linking their UV fixed-point structure to observable inflationary predictions via renormalization group improved Horndeski theory.

Method: Starts from a Horndeski-type scalar-tensor theory, applies renormalization group improvement to generate higher-derivative couplings fixed by microscopic parameters at the Gaussian fixed point; employs multi-field inflation formalism to compute observational constraints on the non-minimal coupling.

Result: The non-minimal coupling parameter propagates into gravitational-sector higher-derivative terms; cosmological observations constrain this parameter and thereby exclude UV completions based on two of the three non-Gaussian fixed points, leaving only one yielding viable inflationary trajectories.

Conclusion: Asymptotically safe scalar-tensor theories with viable scalaron-driven inflation must originate from a specific non-Gaussian fixed point, providing a concrete link between UV completion and low-energy cosmology.

Abstract: We investigate the constraining power of scalaron-driven inflation on asymptotically safe scalar-tensor theories. Starting from a Horndeski-type theory and applying a renormalization group improvement procedure generates higher-derivative couplings which are fixed in terms of the microscopic parameters - a structure that is expected to occur also within first principle computations based on the asymptotic safety mechanism. The latter are taken to be the free parameters appearing at the Gaussian fixed point. We find that the free parameter initially associated with the non-minimal gravity-matter coupling is not confined to the gravity-matter sector of the theory and also enters the effective higher-derivative couplings in the gravitational sector. We review the setting of multi-field inflationary models which is appropriate to analyze the inflationary dynamics in this context and illustrate their applicability by working out the explicit bounds on the non-minimal gravity-matter coupling resulting from cosmological observations. Given the fixed point structure of asymptotically safe scalar-tensor theories, the results indicate that UV-completions by two of the three available non-Gaussian fixed points can be ruled out while pinpointing phenomenologically viable RG trajectories emanating from the third fixed point.

</details>


### [151] [An Interplay Between Fractional Calculus and Holographic Dark Energy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22431)
*Ayush Bidlan*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This dissertation introduces Fractional Holographic Dark Energy (FHDE) by incorporating a Riesz fractional spatial derivative into black hole thermodynamics via the Wheeler–DeWitt equation, yielding α-dependent Bekenstein–Hawking entropy and a new dark energy density; it explores cosmological implications—including equation-of-state, deceleration parameter, and future singularities—across multiple modified gravity frameworks (BD, DGP, EPN, Horndeski), aiming to resolve Hubble-cutoff issues in standard HDE and link quantum-gravitational non-locality to cosmic acceleration.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address theoretical challenges of standard Holographic Dark Energy (HDE), particularly those arising from the Hubble cutoff, by incorporating non-local, memory-based effects from fractional calculus—motivated by quantum gravity considerations—into black hole thermodynamics and dark energy construction.

Method: Introduces a Riesz fractional spatial derivative into the Hamiltonian constraint of a Schwarzschild black hole in quantum geometrodynamics, deriving a Fractional Wheeler–DeWitt equation; solves it to obtain α-dependent fractional Bekenstein–Hawking entropy; constructs FHDE density using Cohen’s inequality; analyzes cosmological behavior via observables, effective field descriptions (spin-0/spin-1), and extensions to BD, DGP, EPN, and Horndeski theories; classifies late-time singularities (big/little/pseudo-rip) in the Granda–Oliveros FHDE setting.

Result: Derives fractionally corrected black hole thermodynamics with Lévy index α ∈ (1,2]; constructs FHDE density; obtains redshift- and α-dependent kinetic/potential terms, equation-of-state w(z,α), deceleration q(z,α), and singularity conditions; demonstrates consistency with late-time acceleration while offering deviations from ΛCDM and standard HDE.

Conclusion: FHDE provides a theoretically grounded, non-local extension of HDE that bridges fractional calculus, black hole thermodynamics, and cosmic acceleration—resolving Hubble-cutoff limitations and offering testable predictions across modified gravity frameworks, thereby advancing beyond ΛCDM.

Abstract: This dissertation aims to put forth a systematic construction of a fractional-calculus extension of holographic dark energy (HDE). We show that linking late-time cosmic acceleration to non-local or memory effects encoded in a fractional (Riesz) derivative within black hole thermodynamics produces deviations from standard HDE and can address some challenges of the Hubble cutoff. In particular, a Riesz fractional spatial derivative is introduced into the Hamiltonian constraint of a Schwarzschild black hole in quantum geometrodynamics, leading to a Fractional Wheeler--DeWitt equation whose solutions yield fractionally corrected thermodynamic quantities, notably fractional Bekenstein--Hawking entropy governed by the Lévy index \(α\), with \(1<α\leq2\). Using this entropy with Cohen's inequality, a new dark energy density is constructed, defining the Fractional Holographic Dark Energy (FHDE) framework.
  The cosmological implications of FHDE are then investigated. Within the Hubble cutoff, its late-time evolution is analysed through cosmological observables, and the model is reconstructed using effective field descriptions with spin-\(0\) and spin-\(1\) candidates, allowing kinetic and potential terms to be extracted as functions of redshift \(z\) and \(α\). The framework is then extended to BD, DGP, EPN, and Horndeski theories to derive the equation-of-state and deceleration parameters in terms of \(z\) and \(α\). In addition, the fate of the Universe is studied through late-time singularities, namely the big, little, and pseudo-rip, within the Granda--Oliveros FHDE setting. In short, this dissertation proposes FHDE as a theoretically motivated extension of HDE, bridging non-locality in quantum gravity with the late-time dynamics of the Universe, and offering a route toward understanding cosmic acceleration beyond \(Λ\)CDM.

</details>


### [152] [Steering a warp drive without exotic matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22531)
*An T. Le*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper resolves the long-standing problem of accelerating warp drives within general relativity by proving a conservation-law obstruction and constructing the first explicit, positive-energy, accelerating warp spacetime—realized as a photon-rocket-driven timelike shell enclosing a flat passenger cavity; steering is achieved via Bondi mass loss and recoil, satisfying dominant energy conditions under bounded acceleration and shell compactness.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the fundamental incompatibility between warp drive acceleration and energy conditions: prior constructions were either static/constant-velocity or required exotic matter; Bobrick & Martire highlighted the absence of a natural velocity-changing mechanism in warp drives.

Method: 1) Derives a Bondi–Sachs four-momentum conservation law showing acceleration requires radiation to null infinity; 2) Constructs an exact exterior Kinnersley photon rocket matched to a perturbatively and numerically verified timelike shell; 3) Analyzes energy conditions (dominant/surface DEC), stability (marginal vs. strict), acceleration bounds (Buchdahl-type frontier), and optimal maneuver (Damour dipole) on GW-silent class.

Result: First positive-energy, accelerating, subluminal, causal warp spacetime; exact exterior + certified shell; acceleration bounded by $a_{\max} R \le g(2m/R)$ with $g$ ranging from ~0.2 to $\frac{1}{2}(1 - 2m/R)$; shell satisfies surface DEC for $2m/R < 24/25$; steering obeys $-\dot{m} \ge 3m|a|$; marginal wall stability established; no exotic matter required.

Conclusion: Warp drive acceleration is possible without exotic matter—it is fundamentally constrained by energy budget and radiative losses, not by violation of energy conditions; the drive operates as a tidally shielded flat interior propelled by photon-rocket recoil, establishing a physically viable, though energetically costly, relativistic propulsion paradigm.

Abstract: A useful warp drive must change its velocity, yet known positive-energy constructions are static or constant-velocity, while accelerating ones require exotic matter; Bobrick and Martire noted that warp drives ``do not have any natural way of changing their velocities.'' First, a conservation law settles the principle: for any asymptotically flat drive with a confined dominant-energy source and standard peeling, the Bondi--Sachs four-momentum changes only through radiation to null infinity, so the drive cannot steer without radiating. Second, we construct a positive-energy spacetime that saturates this bound. Prescribing the passenger worldtube, we take the exact Kinnersley photon rocket as exterior and solve for the matching timelike shell; the exterior and steering law are exact, while the shell is certified perturbatively and numerically. The mechanism is photon-rocket recoil on a tidally protected, exactly flat cavity; the warp drive is the decoupled flat interior, not a warp field. The exterior energy conditions reduce to $n^2\ge0$, and steering obeys $-\dot m\ge 3m|a|$, paid for by Bondi mass loss. The shell satisfies the surface dominant energy condition for $2m/R<24/25$; admissible accelerating shells exist near the Schwarzschild--Minkowski anchor and, for slow burns, as time-evolved spacetimes. A Buchdahl-type frontier $a_{\max}R\le g(2m/R)$ caps the acceleration between a numerical lower bound $\sim\!0.2$ and a closed-form ceiling ${1\over2}(1-2m/R)$. On the gravitational-wave--silent class, the optimal maneuver is the Damour dipole. The wall is marginally stable, with strict stability available at no energy-condition cost; fully dynamical flux-coupled stability remains open. The drive is causal, subluminal, and energetically costly but positive-energy: steering is a problem of energy budget, not exotic matter.

</details>


### [153] [Extended parameterized spin expansion formalism for ringdown analysis with GW250114](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22580)
*Jia-Ning Chen,Liang-Bi Wu,Zong-Kuan Guo*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends the ParSpec framework for black hole ringdown tests by treating the scaling index p and length scale ℓ as free parameters (tilde{p}, tilde{ℓ}), enabling a theory-agnostic effective-field-theory-inspired analysis of Kerr ringdown spectra; applied to GW250114 (and jointly with GW231123), it finds that current data are insufficient to constrain tilde{p}, while tilde{ℓ} constraints are geometry-driven by the γ < 1 condition, yielding weak upper bounds (~83 km) and minimal information gain per Kullback–Leibler analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To move beyond theory-motivated integer assumptions for the scaling index p in ringdown tests, enabling a more general, effective-field-theory-inspired, theory-agnostic characterization of deviations from Kerr geometry via simultaneous inference of the characteristic length scale ℓ and scaling index p.

Method: Extended the Parameterized Spin Expansion Coefficients (ParSpec) framework to jointly sample ℓ and p (reparameterized as tilde{ℓ} and tilde{p}); performed Bayesian inference using pyRing on GW250114 with informative priors on final mass M_f and luminosity distance D_L; compared 220-only vs. 220+221 mode models under varying γ conditions (e.g., γ < 1); analyzed posterior geometry and information content via Kullback–Leibler divergence.

Result: Posterior on tilde{p} is prior-dominated — current ringdown data cannot distinguish scaling behaviors; tilde{ℓ} constraints are governed by parameter-space geometry induced by γ < 1, yielding stable but weak 90%-credible upper bound tilde{ℓ}_90 ≈ 83 km; KL analysis shows negligible data-driven information gain on tilde{ℓ} and log γ; 220-only model outperforms 220+221 in constraint informativeness at current SNR; joint analysis with GW231123 is dominated by GW250114.

Conclusion: The extended ParSpec framework confirms the limitations of current ringdown SNR in probing non-Kerr physics: while γ < 1 provides the most robust and stable prescription for bounding tilde{ℓ}, the lack of constraining power on tilde{p} and minimal KL information gain underscore the need for higher-SNR events or improved modeling to meaningfully test alternative gravity theories via ringdown spectroscopy.

Abstract: We extend the Parameterized Spin Expansion Coefficients (ParSpec) framework for ringdown tests of black holes by simultaneously sampling the characteristic length scale $\ell$ and the scaling index $p$, where the two parameters are replaced by $\tilde{p}$ and $\tilde{\ell}$ in subsequent analysis, respectively. Unlike previous analyses that fix $\tilde{p}$ to theory-motivated integer values, this extension provides a theory-agnostic description inspired by effective-field theory to the Kerr ringdown spectrum. Using \texttt{pyRing}, we apply the framework to GW250114 with informative priors on $M_{\rm f}$ and $D_{\rm L}$, comparing the $220$-only and $220+221$ mode models under different conditions on $γ$. The posterior distributions of $\tilde{\ell}, \tilde{p}$ and $γ$ are presented with different ringdown start times. We show that the posterior of $\tilde{p}$ remains largely prior dominated, indicating that current ringdown data cannot distinguish different scaling behaviors of the correction. The inferred constraint on $\tilde{\ell}$ is instead mainly controlled by the geometry of the allowed parameter space induced by the $γ$ condition. In particular, $γ<1$ provides the most natural prescription in the $(\tilde{\ell},\tilde{p})$ plane and yields stable but weak upper bounds of $\tilde{\ell}_{90}\simeq 83\, {\rm km}$. An analysis based on the Kullback-Leibler divergence further shows that, after accounting for the effective prior associated with $γ<1$, the data-driven information gain on $\tilde{\ell}$ and $\logγ$ is very small. At the current SNR level, the $220$-only model provides a more informative constraint than the $220+221$ model. We also perform a joint analysis including GW231123 and find that the combined constraint is dominated by GW250114.

</details>


### [154] [Bianchi IX dynamics with a phantom field](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22675)
*Adel Awad,Dmitry Chirkov,Alexey Golovnev,Alexey Toporensky*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies Bianchi IX cosmological dynamics with a massless phantom field, revealing novel BKL oscillatory behavior—including simultaneous negativity of two Kasner indices and large-magnitude negative indices—and provides an analytical explanation for recently observed volume oscillations.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand how a massless phantom field modifies Bianchi IX (Mixmaster) dynamics compared to vacuum or standard scalar field cases, especially in light of numerically discovered volume oscillations.

Method: Analytical investigation of the Bianchi IX dynamical system coupled to a massless phantom field, focusing on Kasner parameter evolution and BKL-type oscillatory behavior near the singularity.

Result: Demonstrates that phantom matter permits two simultaneously negative Kasner indices with arbitrarily large magnitudes—unrealizable in standard settings—and analytically explains the origin and structure of volume oscillations observed numerically.

Conclusion: The phantom field fundamentally alters the BKL map and singularity structure, enabling new oscillatory regimes and providing a mechanistic explanation for volume oscillations via modified Kasner transitions.

Abstract: We consider Bianchi IX dynamics of a Universe filled with a massless phantom field. Such an exotic matter source enables regimes impossible in vacuum or with a standard scalar field. In particular, two Kasner indices of BKL oscillations can be simultaneously negative, and the absolute value of a negative index can be large. We describe the consequences of these features and explain the nature of volume oscillations recently discovered in such a system by numerical methods.

</details>


### [155] [Orientation matters: Consequences for gravitational-wave background detectability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.22985)
*Nelson Christensen,Joseph D. Romano,Mairi Sakellariadou,Jishnu Suresh*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper examines how detector geometry—specifically separation and relative orientation—affects cross-correlation sensitivity to gravitational-wave backgrounds, showing that orientation strongly influences detectability of both isotropic and anisotropic signals.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To quantify the impact of detector geometrical configuration (separation and relative orientation) on the sensitivity of cross-correlation searches for gravitational-wave backgrounds.

Method: Analytical and numerical application of standard cross-correlation techniques to simple configurations of Earth-based L-shaped laser interferometers.

Result: Relative orientation significantly alters the overlap reduction function, thereby drastically affecting signal-to-noise ratio and detectability for both isotropic and anisotropic gravitational-wave backgrounds.

Conclusion: Optimal network design for gravitational-wave background searches must explicitly account for detector orientation—not just baseline separation—to maximize sensitivity.

Abstract: Cross-correlation searches for gravitational-wave backgrounds depend on the geometrical configuration (physical separation and relative orientation) of the detectors comprising the network. Applying standard techniques to a few simple examples, we illustrate how the relative orientation of a pair of Earth-based L-shaped laser interferometers can drastically impact the detectability of both isotropic and anisotropic gravitational-wave backgrounds.

</details>


### [156] [Periodic Timelike Motion and Gravitational Wave Signatures around a Magnetically Charged Black Hole Surrounded by Quintessence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23021)
*R. H. Ali*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies timelike geodesics and gravitational wave signatures of periodic orbits around a static, magnetically charged black hole in a quintessence background; it classifies bound trajectories using the zoom-whirl taxonomy, quantifies how quintessence shifts orbital parameters, and models EMRI gravitational waveforms showing quintessence-induced modifications in phase, burst timing, harmonics, and strain distribution—especially in the mHz band for LISA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe the imprint of quintessence—a dark energy candidate—on black hole spacetime geometry and its observable gravitational wave signatures, particularly through periodic orbital dynamics and extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (EMRIs).

Method: Analytical and numerical study of timelike geodesics in a nonlinear electrodynamics-derived magnetically charged black hole metric immersed in a quintessence field; effective potential analysis; computation of marginally bound and innermost stable circular orbits (MBCO/ISCO); zoom-whirl trajectory classification (z,w,v); gravitational waveform modeling via the numerical kludge approximation for EMRIs; Fourier spectral analysis and characteristic strain estimation.

Result: Quintessence parameter $c_q$ systematically shifts orbital radii, conserved quantities, and turning points; zoom-whirl waveforms exhibit burst-like structure with $c_q$-dependent phase evolution, burst timing, and harmonic content; Fourier spectra show discrete comb structure; characteristic strain peaks in millihertz band, relevant for LISA.

Conclusion: A quintessence background leaves detectable, systematic imprints on both geodesic dynamics and gravitational wave observables of periodic orbits—suggesting EMRIs around exotic black holes could serve as probes of dark energy models.

Abstract: We investigate timelike geodesics and gravitational wave signatures of periodic motion around a static magnetically charged black hole arising from nonlinear electrodynamics and immersed in a quintessence background. We analyze the effective potential for massive particles, determine the marginally bound and innermost stable circular orbits, and classify the resulting bound trajectories using the zoom-whirl taxonomy $(\mathit{z},\mathit{w},\mathit{v})$. We show that the quintessence parameter $c_q$ systematically shifts the orbital radii, conserved quantities, and turning-point structure associated with representative periodic families.
  We then model the gravitational radiation emitted by periodic extreme-mass-ratio inspirals within the numerical kludge approximation. The resulting waveforms exhibit the characteristic burst-like structure of zoom-whirl motion, while variations in the quintessence coupling parameter modify the phase evolution, burst timing, and harmonic content of the signal. The corresponding Fourier spectra display a discrete comb-like structure, and the characteristic strain is concentrated in the millihertz band relevant for space-based detectors such as LISA. These results indicate that a quintessence background can leave systematic imprints on periodic orbit dynamics and on the associated time and frequency-domain gravitational wave observables.

</details>


### [157] [MaxWave Signal: Rapid, coherent maximum likelihood wavelet reconstruction of transient signals in gravitational wave data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23035)
*Sudhi Mathur,Neil J. Cornish*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper extends MaxWave, a fast maximum likelihood wavelet reconstruction algorithm, to enable coherent multi-detector gravitational-wave signal reconstruction and glitch rejection via synthetic detector formation using z-statistic alignment and geometric noise weighting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Increasing gravitational-wave detection rates demand faster, automated, low-latency, model-independent analysis capable of distinguishing weak astrophysical signals from non-Gaussian instrumental glitches.

Method: Coherent multi-detector wavelet reconstruction using z-statistic–based time/phase/amplitude alignment, geometrically averaged noise spectrum for adaptive weighting, and synthetic detector synthesis to preserve Gaussian noise statistics while amplifying coherent transients.

Result: Improved sensitivity to weak gravitational-wave transients, robust rejection of coincident non-coherent glitches, real-time low-latency reconstructions, and compatibility with existing burst search frameworks.

Conclusion: The extended MaxWave algorithm provides a model-independent, statistically principled, and computationally efficient method for coherent denoising and reconstruction—enhancing detection confidence and broadening sensitivity across diverse signal morphologies.

Abstract: Advances in gravitational-wave detector sensitivity have increased the rate of transient signal detections, demanding faster automated analysis. We extend MaxWave, a fast maximum likelihood wavelet reconstruction algorithm, to perform coherent multi-detector signal reconstruction and glitch rejection. We coherently search for a common set of wavelets modeling the signal in all detectors. Multi-detector data are aligned using z-statistic time and phase offsets and amplitude scalings relative to the dominant reconstruction, as well as adaptive noise weightings derived from a geometrically averaged noise spectrum. By aligning and weighting individual detectors, we form a synthetic detector that amplifies non-Gaussian features, down-weights noisy detectors, and preserves Gaussian noise statistics. We extract the coherent signal using this synthetic detector, improving sensitivity to weak events while rejecting coincident glitches that lack consistent phase and amplitude evolution. Our algorithm provides real-time, low-latency, model-independent signal reconstructions, safely denoises gravitational wave data without removing transient signals, and can complement existing burst search and reconstruction frameworks through a fundamentally distinct approach, strengthening detection confidence and improving sensitivity to diverse signal morphologies.

</details>


### [158] [Polarization States and Effective Stress Energy Tensor of Gravitational Waves in Metric $f(R)$ Gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23056)
*Sakshi Srivastava,Utkal Keshari Dash,Murli Manohar Verma*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies gravitational wave polarization and energy transport in metric f(R) gravity, revealing additional scalar-driven polarization modes (breathing and longitudinal) beyond GR's tensor modes, and derives an effective stress-energy tensor showing frequency-dependent suppression of scalar energy flux due to subluminal propagation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore how the scalar degree of freedom in metric f(R) gravity modifies gravitational-wave polarization states and energy transport, enabling observational tests of modified gravity with gravitational-wave detectors.

Method: Linearized analysis of metric f(R) gravity around Minkowski spacetime; use of electric components of the linearized Riemann tensor to derive polarization amplitudes; application of Isaacson’s high-frequency averaging formalism to obtain the effective stress–energy tensor.

Result: Gravitational waves in metric f(R) gravity exhibit breathing (massless scalar) and breathing + longitudinal (massive scalar) polarization modes; both tensor and scalar perturbations contribute to the effective stress–energy tensor; scalar energy flux is suppressed frequency-dependently due to subluminal group velocity.

Conclusion: The work unifies polarization properties and energy transport in metric f(R) gravity, identifying distinctive observational signatures—e.g., extra polarizations and suppressed scalar energy flux—that can be probed by current and future gravitational-wave observatories.

Abstract: We investigate the polarization properties and effective stress--energy tensor of gravitational waves in metric $f(R)$ gravity within the linearized approximation around Minkowski spacetime. Owing to the additional scalar degree of freedom inherent in the theory, gravitational waves exhibit polarization states beyond the two tensor modes predicted by general relativity. Using the electric components of the linearized Riemann tensor, we derive explicit expressions for the polarization amplitudes and show that a massless scalar field excites a transverse breathing mode, whereas a massive scalar field generates both breathing and longitudinal responses through a single propagating scalar excitation. Employing the Isaacson high-frequency averaging formalism, we further derive the effective stress--energy tensor and demonstrate that both tensor and scalar perturbations contribute to the total gravitational-wave energy density. The energy transport associated with the massive scalar mode is reduced by its subluminal group velocity, leading to a frequency-dependent suppression of the scalar energy flux. These results establish a unified connection between gravitational-wave polarization and energy transport in metric $f(R)$ gravity and provide potential observational signatures for testing modified gravity with current and future gravitational-wave detectors.

</details>


### [159] [Dark matter from the quadratic spinor Lagrangian II: A spin-3/2 no-go and the uniqueness of the spin-1/2 candidate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23273)
*Roh-Suan Tung*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: The paper proves a no-go theorem: promoting the spinor 1-form Ψ_μ in the Quadratic Spinor Lagrangian to an independent Dirac-vector field does not yield a viable spin-3/2 dark matter candidate; only a composite spin-1/2 Dirac fermion—interpreted as the Goldstino of spontaneously broken local supersymmetry—propagates, making it the unique dark matter candidate.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether promoting the spinor 1-form Ψ_μ to an independent Dirac-vector field in the Quadratic Spinor Lagrangian yields a genuine spin-3/2 dark matter candidate.

Method: Exact Clifford reduction of torsional terms; analysis of second-order kinetic structure 2DΨγ₅DΨ; decomposition of the spinor-curvature identity; second variation of the curvature-coupled action S = −∫ψ̄ψ R √−g with metric and scalar both composite in Ψ; pole analysis in momentum space.

Result: Three key results: (i) torsional mass is frame-aligned and confined to time-component sector—not uniform spin-3/2 mass; (ii) 2DΨγ₅DΨ contributes only boundary terms—no bulk kinetic or cross terms; (iii) physical dynamics reside solely in S = −∫ψ̄ψ R √−g, whose second variation factors through massless linearized metric and scalar → all poles on k² = 0 light cone → only graviton and massless scalar propagate; no massive spin-3/2 mode exists on any background.

Conclusion: The composite spin-1/2 Dirac fermion is the unique propagating matter excitation—and thus the unique dark matter candidate—of the QSL; its interpretation as the Goldstino of metric-induced local supersymmetry breaking provides a gravitational, composite realization of the light-gravitino scenario.

Abstract: The composite Quadratic Spinor Lagrangian (QSL) propagates a spin-1/2 Dirac fermion whose mass is generated geometrically by cosmological trace torsion. It is natural to ask whether promoting the spinor 1-form $Ψ_μ$ to an \emph{independent} Dirac-vector field yields a genuine spin-3/2 dark-matter candidate. We prove that it does not. Three results combine into a no-go theorem. First, the torsional term, computed exactly by Clifford reduction, is a frame-aligned mass confined to the time-component sector -- not a uniform spin-3/2 mass. Second, the second-order form $2 DΨγ_5 DΨ$ has identically vanishing kinetic and cross terms for the independent field: as a component expression it is the boundary part of the spinor-curvature identity and carries no bulk dynamics. Third, the genuine dynamics therefore reside in the curvature side of that identity, $S=-\int\barψψR\sqrt{-g}$, in which the metric $g=Ψ\otimes_SΨ$ and the scalar $\barψψ$ are \emph{both} composites of $Ψ$; the second variation consequently factors, $δ^2S=\mathcal Q(h_{μν}[δΨ],δΦ[δΨ])$, through the linearized metric and a scalar, both massless. Every propagating pole therefore lies on the metric light cone $k^2=0$ -- the graviton and a scalar -- and no massive spin-3/2 mode exists, on any background. This is the dynamical completion of the kinematic fact that the composite spinor 1-form has no spin-3/2 part, and it establishes the composite spin-1/2 Dirac fermion as the unique propagating matter excitation, and the unique dark-matter candidate, of the QSL. Through the super-SL(2,C) structure this surviving mode is naturally read as the Goldstino of the local supersymmetry broken by the metric condensate -- a composite, gravitational descendant of the light-gravitino dark matter of Pagels and Primack.

</details>


### [160] [Primordial Black Holes from Vector-Induced Curvature Perturbations Sourced by Primordial Magnetic Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23307)
*Chang Han,Zu-Cheng Chen,Hongwei Yu,Puxun Wu*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes a novel post-inflationary mechanism—driven by vector perturbations from primordial magnetic fields during a kination epoch—to enhance small-scale curvature perturbations and generate primordial black holes (PBHs) potentially accounting for a significant fraction of dark matter.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain the observed abundance of primordial black holes (PBHs), which requires strong enhancement of primordial curvature perturbations on small scales—a challenge for standard inflationary models.

Method: Analyzes second-order scalar curvature perturbations induced by first-order metric vector perturbations sourced by the electromagnetic stress-energy tensor of primordial magnetic fields (PMFs) during a stiff/kination epoch; computes power spectrum scaling and applies to Ratra-type magnetogenesis.

Result: Vector-induced curvature perturbations exhibit infrared amplification and power-law scaling 𝒫_ℛ(k) ∝ k⁻⁵; PBH production from this mechanism can reach Ω_PBH ≈ Ω_DM, i.e., constitute a substantial fraction of dark matter.

Conclusion: A kination-era vector-induced mechanism via PMFs provides a viable, testable pathway to generate observationally relevant PBH abundances without requiring extreme inflationary model tuning.

Abstract: Generating an appreciable abundance of primordial black holes (PBHs) requires a substantial enhancement of primordial curvature perturbations on small scales. In this work, we propose a new post-inflationary mechanism in which such an enhancement arises during a stiff, or kination, epoch. The mechanism is driven by metric vector perturbations sourced by the vector component of the electromagnetic stress-energy tensor associated with primordial magnetic fields (PMFs). Since these first-order vector modes remain approximately constant during kination, they act as persistent nonlinear sources for second-order scalar perturbations. We show that the resulting vector-induced curvature perturbations are amplified toward the infrared cutoff of the kination band and exhibit the characteristic scaling $\mathcal P_{\mathcal R}(k)\propto k^{-5}$. As a concrete realization, we consider PMFs generated in a Ratra-type magnetogenesis scenario and find that the induced curvature perturbations can produce PBHs with an abundance large enough to constitute a substantial fraction of the dark matter.

</details>


### [161] [Scattered wave functions and worldline instantons for particle production in curved spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23389)
*Philip Semrén,Greger Torgrimsson*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper develops two complementary methods—the extended scattered-wave-function (SWF) method and the open-worldline-instanton method with pre-exponential factor—to compute spin-1/2 particle-antiparticle pair production in multidimensional curved spacetimes; both yield consistent results for 2D metrics, with SWF offering numerical precision and instanton scaling better to higher dimensions and extreme regimes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To study quantum vacuum pair production of spin-1/2 particles in generic curved spacetimes with nontrivial multi-coordinate dependence—beyond the reach of existing methods such as constant-field or 1D approximations.

Method: 1) Extension of the scattered-wave-function (SWF) formalism from electromagnetic to curved spacetime backgrounds; 2) Completion of the open-worldline-instanton method by analytically deriving the pre-exponential factor in the semiclassical pair-production probability.

Result: Both methods yield quantitatively consistent pair-production probabilities for several two-dimensional metrics; SWF delivers numerically exact results efficiently in those cases, while the instanton method demonstrates superior asymptotic and dimensional scalability.

Conclusion: The two developed methods constitute complementary, robust tools for first-principles computation of gravitational pair production in generic, multidimensional curved spacetimes—enabling studies previously inaccessible to standard techniques.

Abstract: We study the production of spin-$1/2$ particle-antiparticle pairs in curved spacetimes with nontrivial dependence on more than one coordinate. To this end, we develop two complementary approaches. First, we extend the scattered-wave-function (SWF) method, originally introduced for pair production in electromagnetic backgrounds, to curved spacetime backgrounds. Second, we complete the development of an open-worldline-instanton method by deriving the pre-exponential factor of the pair-production probability. We apply both methods to several two-dimensional metrics and find good agreement between the resulting probabilities. While the SWF approach provides numerically exact results and is particularly efficient for the examples considered here, the instanton approach offers favorable scaling to higher-dimensional backgrounds and more extreme parameter regimes. These methods provide new tools for studying pair production in multidimensional gravitational backgrounds beyond the reach of many existing approaches.

</details>


### [162] [High-Stability Deformable Mirrors for Correcting Non-Axisymmetric Residual Aberrations in Thermal Compensation of Future Gravitational Wave Interferometers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23415)
*Luciano Antonio Corubolo,Matteo Lorenzini,Lorenzo Aiello,Elisabetta Cesarini,Maria Cifaldi,Matteo Ianni,Diana Lumaca,Yury Minenkov,Ilaria Nardecchia,Alessio Rocchi,Claudia Taranto,Viviana Fafone*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes using deformable mirrors with a modified Gerchberg-Saxton algorithm to correct non-axisymmetric optical aberrations in gravitational wave detectors, validated via simulation and experiment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Non-axisymmetric wavefront distortions in current gravitational wave detectors (e.g., Advanced LIGO/Virgo) remain uncorrected, threatening stability and sensitivity—especially critical for future high-power instruments like the Einstein Telescope.

Method: A modified Gerchberg-Saxton algorithm computes the target phase map for a deformable mirror, which shapes the reflected CO2 laser beam to imprint asymmetric intensity patterns onto lensing optics; simulations and experimental validation assess pattern fidelity.

Result: Simulations and experiments demonstrate consistent, accurate reproduction of desired non-axisymmetric intensity patterns using the deformable mirror–based projection strategy.

Conclusion: Deformable mirrors controlled by a modified Gerchberg-Saxton algorithm provide a viable, flexible, and low-noise method to mitigate non-axisymmetric optical aberrations in next-generation gravitational wave interferometers.

Abstract: In gravitational wave detectors, optical aberrations arise mainly from laser absorption in coatings and production process defects in the optics along the laser path. If left uncorrected, these optical path distortions drive the interferometer away from its optimal working point, degrading both stability and sensitivity. Future instruments such as the Einstein Telescope high-frequency detector will operate with unprecedented circulating power, further amplifying the aberration budget. In the current detectors Advanced Virgo and Advanced LIGO, the axisymmetric distortions are corrected using thermal actuators and CO2 laser projectors, however, non-axisymmetric wavefront distortions remain unmitigated. Deformable mirrors are investigated as a flexible solution for mitigating such defects: by shaping the CO2 beam phase upon reflection, they can imprint the required asymmetric intensity pattern on the lensing optics without introducing frequency dependent noise. The target phase map is computed via a modified Gerchberg-Saxton algorithm. We present simulations of this projection strategy and experimental validation demonstrating consistent reproduction of the desired intensity patterns.

</details>


### [163] [Spacetime torsion fixes the mass and spin of gravitationally produced dark matter](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23418)
*Roh-Suan Tung*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper proposes that spacetime torsion alone—without a Higgs mechanism or free mass parameter—generates the Dirac mass of fermionic dark matter produced gravitationally from stochastic gravitational waves; the effective mass is locked to the Hubble rate at production, yielding a one-parameter prediction for relic abundance and uniquely identifying the dark matter as a composite spin-1/2 Dirac fermion.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explain how dark matter fermions acquire mass in gravitational production scenarios without invoking unspecified late-time physics, Higgs mechanisms, or ad hoc mass parameters.

Method: Uses the Quadratic Spinor Lagrangian formulation of gravity, extended to Einstein–Cartan theory with torsion; derives vectorial trace torsion from a cosmological spinor condensate; performs explicit Clifford reduction to obtain a pure Dirac mass term for the produced fermion.

Result: Derives an exact effective Dirac mass M_eff = (1/√6)|χ̇/χ| ∝ H_*, shows relic abundance scales as Ωh² ∝ H_*^{5/2}, and proves absence of propagating spin-3/2 modes—establishing the composite spin-1/2 fermion as the unique dark matter candidate.

Conclusion: Spacetime torsion suffices to generate the required fermion mass for gravitational dark matter production; the framework yields testable, parameter-free predictions for abundance and fixes the spin definitively to 1/2.

Abstract: The gravitational production of dark matter from stochastic gravitational waves requires the produced fermion to acquire a mass by unspecified late-time physics. We show that this mass is supplied by spacetime torsion alone -- no Higgs sector and no free mass parameter. In the Quadratic Spinor Lagrangian formulation of general relativity, extended to Einstein--Cartan, a cosmological spinor condensate generates a vectorial trace torsion $K\propto\dotχ/χ$; an explicit Clifford reduction confers on the produced spin-1/2 fermion a pure Dirac mass $M_{\rm eff}=(1/\sqrt6)\,|\dotχ/χ|$, with no pseudoscalar or cross terms, locked to the Hubble rate at production, $M_{\rm eff}\simeq(c_χ/\sqrt6)H_*$. The relic abundance is then a one-parameter prediction, $Ωh^2\propto H_*^{5/2}$, and the spin is fixed: the same framework admits no propagating spin-3/2 mode, so the composite spin-1/2 Dirac fermion is its unique dark-matter candidate.

</details>


### [164] [Closed and broken electromagnetic orbits in Kerr--Newman spacetime](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23446)
*Erasmo Caponio,Giulio Sanzeni,Stefan Suhr*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper investigates timelike solutions of the Lorentz force equation in sub-extremal Kerr–Newman spacetime, focusing on electromagnetic orbits in the time-machine region where the axial Killing vector is timelike; it constructs closed, spherical, and flyby orbits, introduces 'broken electromagnetic orbits' to model charged-particle decay, and demonstrates energy extraction and causality-violating processes via concatenated orbits.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand the dynamics of charged particles in strong-field regimes near rotating, charged black holes—particularly in the time-machine region where causality violations may arise—and to explore physical mechanisms (e.g., particle decay) that could realize closed timelike curves or energy extraction.

Method: Analytical study of the Lorentz force equation in Kerr–Newman geometry; construction of explicit electromagnetic orbits (circular, spherical, flyby) using symmetries and conserved quantities; introduction and analysis of piecewise smooth 'broken orbits' satisfying four-momentum and charge conservation at junction points.

Result: Existence proofs and explicit parameter relations for closed circular and spherical electromagnetic orbits in the equatorial time-machine region; sufficient conditions for flyby orbits with radial turning points in that region; construction of closed broken electromagnetic orbits enabling causality violation via concatenation of flyby and spherical branches across inner horizons.

Conclusion: Charged particle motion in the Kerr–Newman time-machine region admits rich orbit structure—including stable spherical orbits and causality-violating broken orbits—providing concrete classical mechanisms for both energy extraction and closed timelike-like behavior, contingent on specific charge-to-mass ratios and radial matching.

Abstract: We study future-pointing timelike solutions of the Lorentz force equation in the sub-extremal Kerr--Newman spacetime, with special attention to the time-machine region $\mathfrak T$, where the axial Killing field $\partial_φ$ is timelike. We first construct smooth closed electromagnetic orbits tangent to $\partial_φ$ in the positive equatorial part of $\mathfrak T$: the radius of such a circle determines, and is determined by, the charge-to-mass ratio of the particle which must have opposite sign to that of the black hole charge. We then prove the existence of spherical electromagnetic orbits contained in the equatorial time-machine region and derive explicit relations between their radius, charge-to-mass ratio, energy and angular momentum. Next we give sufficient conditions ensuring that an equatorial electromagnetic orbit is a flyby orbit with radial turning point in $\mathfrak T$. Finally, to describe charged-particle decay processes whose fragments have different charge-to-mass ratios, we introduce the notion of a broken electromagnetic orbit: a continuous, piecewise smooth, future-pointing worldline whose smooth pieces solve the Lorentz force equation. Imposing conservation of kinetic four-momentum and electric charge at the decay vertices, we exhibit an energy extraction process followed by a causality-violating one. The latter is realized by a closed broken electromagnetic orbit, which we construct in both the $r$-positive and the $r$-negative regions inside the inner horizon, by concatenating two flyby branches sharing a common radial turning value $\bar r$, and having charge-to-mass ratios with opposite sign to that of the black hole charge, with a spherical electromagnetic orbit of radius $\bar r$.

</details>


### [165] [Dark matter from the quadratic spinor Lagrangian I: Geometric mass for a gravitationally produced spin-1/2 fermion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23447)
*Roh-Suan Tung*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper introduces the Quadratic Spinor Lagrangian (QSL) framework in Einstein–Cartan gravity to geometrically generate Dirac mass for dark fermions produced via gravitational-wave freeze-in, linking their mass directly to the Hubble rate at production and fixing relic abundance with a single scale.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To resolve the need for ad hoc mass generation in gravitational-wave-induced freeze-in of dark fermions by providing a geometric origin for Dirac mass within a consistent spinor-based formulation of gravity.

Method: Develops the Quadratic Spinor Lagrangian (QSL) formalism extended to Einstein–Cartan gravity; constructs a spinor 1-form from a Dirac field; derives effective Dirac mass via torsion sourced by a cosmological spinor condensate; performs Clifford reduction to isolate mass term; analyzes propagation content to exclude spin-3/2 modes.

Result: The QSL framework yields a geometric, non-free Dirac mass $M_{eff} = (1/\sqrt{6})|\dot{\chi}/\chi| \simeq (c_\chi/\sqrt{6}) H_*$, fully determined by the Hubble rate at production; the only propagating fermionic degree of freedom is a spin-1/2 Dirac fermion; relic abundance scales as $\Omega h^2 \propto H_*^{5/2}$; spin-3/2 excitations are absent in the QSL dynamics.

Conclusion: The QSL formulation provides a self-consistent, geometric mechanism for generating the required fermion mass in gravitational-wave freeze-in, making the Dirac fermion the unique dark-matter candidate within this framework and tightly constraining its cosmology to the scale $H_*$.

Abstract: The gravitational-wave induced freeze-in of Maleknejad and Kopp (2026) produces dark fermions from a stochastic gravitational-wave background, but requires them to acquire mass by separate means. We develop the Quadratic Spinor Lagrangian (QSL) formulation of general relativity, extended to Einstein--Cartan, as a framework that supplies this mass geometrically. The spinor 1-form built from a single Dirac field is purely spin-1/2 -- its gamma-traceless (spin-3/2) part vanishes identically -- so the propagating excitation is a Dirac fermion, the same content as the produced Weyl fermion. A cosmological spinor condensate sources a vectorial trace torsion $K\propto\dotχ/χ$, and an explicit Clifford reduction shows that this torsion gives the fermion a pure Dirac mass $M_{eff}=(1/\sqrt6)\,|\dotχ/χ|$, with no pseudoscalar or cross terms. The mass is not a free parameter but is locked to the Hubble rate at production, $M_{eff}\simeq(c_χ/\sqrt6)H_*$, making the relic abundance a function of essentially the single scale $H_*$ ($Ωh^2\propto H_*^{5/2}$) and supplying the mass the parent mechanism must postulate. Whether promoting the spinor 1-form to an independent field yields a propagating spin-3/2 candidate is a distinct dynamical question; Paper II shows that it does not -- the QSL channels all propagation into the gravitational sector -- so the composite spin-1/2 Dirac fermion is the unique QSL dark-matter candidate. We discuss the resulting dark-matter phenomenology and its link to asymptotically free scalar-field cosmology.

</details>


### [166] [Affine quantization of the dynamical Reissner--Nordström region](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23552)
*Morteza Bajand,Babak Vakili*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper applies affine quantization to the dynamical region of Reissner–Nordström black holes in a minisuperspace model, yielding separable Wheeler–DeWitt solutions (Hermite and Gaussian-like), modified short-distance behavior, and charge-dependent semiclassical wave packet dynamics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To extend affine-quantization methods—previously applied to Schwarzschild black holes—to the charged Reissner–Nordström geometry, addressing quantum behavior near the singularity where standard quantization fails due to positivity constraints on geometric variables.
