Method: Minisuperspace reduction of the Reissner–Nordström interior followed by affine quantization (using positive-definite canonical variables); derivation and separation of the Wheeler–DeWitt equation; construction and normalization of semiclassical wave packets; analysis of probability distributions in minisuperspace with varying electric charge.

Result: The Wheeler–DeWitt equation becomes analytically separable; one sector yields Hermite-polynomial eigenmodes, the other Gaussian-like radial solutions; affine quantization introduces nontrivial short-distance corrections that regularize small-radius behavior; normalizable wave packets show charge-dependent spreading and localization in minisuperspace.

Conclusion: Affine quantization successfully accommodates the positive-definite nature of the interior geometry and reveals qualitatively new quantum features—especially charge-modulated dynamics and improved short-distance behavior—extending the applicability and physical insight of quantum cosmology techniques to charged black hole interiors.

Abstract: We study the quantum dynamics of the dynamical region of the Reissner--Nordström geometry using a minisuperspace reduction and affine quantization, which is naturally suited for positive-definite geometrical variables. The resulting Wheeler--DeWitt equation becomes separable, yielding Hermite-polynomial modes in one sector and Gaussian-like radial solutions in the other. Affine quantization introduces additional short-distance contributions that modify the small-radius behaviour of the wave function. By constructing normalizable semiclassical wave packets, we analyze the resulting probability distributions in minisuperspace and the role played by the electric charge in the quantum dynamics. Our results extend previous affine-quantization studies of the Schwarzschild case to the charged Reissner--Nordström geometry.

</details>


### [167] [Equatorial Periodic Orbits and Gravitational Wave Phenomenology around Spherically-symmetric vacuum solution in Freund-Nambu scalar-tensor gravity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.23635)
*Dhruba Jyoti Gogoi,Jyatsnasree Bora,Himanshu Chaudhary,M. Yousaf,G. Mustafa*

Main category: gr-qc

TL;DR: This paper studies test particle dynamics and gravitational wave signatures in a spherically symmetric vacuum solution of Freund–Nambu scalar–tensor gravity, generalizing the JNW naked singularity via coupling parameters q and g_s; it finds parameter-dependent shifts in ISCO/MBO, identifies extreme zoom-whirl orbits, and shows scalar–tensor corrections cause measurable temporal dephasing in EMRI GW bursts—providing a detectable signature for LISA.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To probe strong-field deviations from general relativity using gravitational wave observables, specifically by analyzing how scalar–tensor couplings modify orbital dynamics and GW phasing in an exact vacuum solution beyond GR.

Method: Analytical and numerical analysis of geodesic motion in a generalized Janis–Newman–Winicour spacetime; classification of bound periodic orbits; application of the Numerical Kludge method to model Extreme Mass-Ratio Inspirals (EMRIs) and compute gravitational waveforms with scalar–tensor corrections.

Result: ISCO and MBO radii shift inward for positive scalar–particle coupling g_s; extreme zoom-whirl orbits are identified; scalar–tensor corrections induce macroscopic temporal dephasing in high-frequency GW bursts from EMRIs—even when spatial orbit topology remains unchanged.

Conclusion: Scalar–tensor modifications leave distinct, observable imprints on GW phasing in strong-field EMRI systems; such dephasing serves as a robust diagnostic for space-based detectors like LISA to constrain scalar–tensor gravity in the strong-field regime.

Abstract: We investigate test particle dynamics and gravitational wave (GW) phenomenology in an exact spherically symmetric vacuum solution of Freund - Nambu scalar - tensor gravity. This framework generalizes the Janis - Newman - Winicour (JNW) naked singularity via a geometric non - linear coupling $q$ and a direct scalar - particle coupling $g_s$. We demonstrate that these parameters systematically modify the Innermost Stable Circular Orbit (ISCO) - which shifts inward for $g_s > 0$ - and the Marginally Bound Orbit (MBO). Furthermore, we classify bound periodic trajectories to isolate extreme zoom - whirl orbits exhibiting intense periapsis precession. By applying the Numerical Kludge method to Extreme Mass - Ratio Inspirals (EMRIs), we reveal that scalar - tensor corrections induce a macroscopic temporal dephasing in high - frequency GW bursts, even when the orbit's spatial topology is preserved. These unique phase shifts offer a robust diagnostic signature for future space-based observatories like LISA to probe the strong - field regime and constrain scalar - tensor extensions of general relativity.

</details>
